# Decisions

The questions still open for interbus, first, then the owner's decisions. [PLAN.md](../PLAN.md) lists the open ones in short at its top and the decided ones in §13; this file wins if the two disagree. Lesson and bug IDs (WD-07, K15 …) are in [docs/history/](history/lessons-ledger.md).

**How this file works.** Only the owner decides; Claude records the answer, the date and the reason. A decision is never edited away: changing one adds a new dated entry that names the one it replaces. Until an open item is answered, the plan assumes its recommendation, except an open licence, which waits for an answer because it cannot be taken back. A deferred item is recorded like a decision, with the date it comes back, and stays in Open. A late answer takes effect from the next week and never reopens finished work without a cut elsewhere.

## Decided

### D1 · Real cities first; Boston is the first and test city

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "lets do real cities first, starting with boston as the test city."
- **What it means:** Boston is on `main` from week 1, baked offline from 2020 census blocks, LODES 8.4 (2023) and one pinned OpenStreetMap extract, behind numeric bake gates (PLAN §5). The play area is "Inner Boston" unless D11 says otherwise, or the Nov 8 trigger shrinks it to a core box that passes the bake gates (the extent flexes; Boston stays). Procedural cities survive only as small test fixtures. v1.0 has one city; January's third-city slot is gone, and a second real city comes after v1.0.
- **Why:** The owner chose real cities in all three earlier versions ([WD-07](history/lessons-ledger.md)). Measured on 2026-10-12, Inner Boston fits the 1,000-cell and 16,000-node budgets with headroom ([WD-09](history/lessons-ledger.md)). Static files and a pinned, dispatch-only bake remove V1's API failures by construction ([WD-01](history/lessons-ledger.md), [WD-03](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** Shrinking the extent (D11) is the cheap partial reversal. Going back to procedural cities before the pack format freezes (Sun Nov 8) costs about a week: a generated city in the same pack format ([WD-05](history/lessons-ledger.md)) replaces Boston, and the mini-Boston fixture stays for tests. After Nov 8 it is 1.1 work, because saves are tied to their city.

### D2 · A flat 2D mode that tilts into 3D, with a far more detailed map

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "have a flat 2d mode, but also be able to pan to 3d. more detailed maps that have a sleek but detailed intricate look."
- **What it means:** MapLibre GL JS, pinned exactly (6.11.2), is the only renderer. The map opens flat and top-down; the same camera tilts into 3D (the `T` key, a two-word chip, or right-drag), at 25–60° and zoom 14 or closer. The basemap is detailed OpenStreetMap vector tiles in one PMTiles file per city: every building, street, path, water body and land use, with no transit features (D17). Close-zoom lane markings (z≥17) are drawn from OSM lane tags in January and are the first January item cut (PLAN §8 rule 9). three.js draws only the artist's bus models, inside MapLibre's own layer, loaded lazily: proven behind a flag in week 5, shipped from January. The old Oct 25 fallback to V3's three.js scene is dropped.
- **Why:** One camera does both views over vector tiles ([RN-01](history/lessons-ledger.md)). V3's scene had no tile pipeline and tripled the critical path ([RN-03](history/lessons-ledger.md), [SC-05](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **Since amended:** 2026-10-14, D25: the chip became a compass; a double-click held tilts (dragged) or flips 2D and 3D (held still); in 3D the board turns (drags, Q and E; N or the compass faces north), and 2D always faces north. Camera input only: no renderer or custom-layer change.
- **How to reverse:** Before Sun Nov 1, dropping the tilt is a deletion (no extrusions, no model path), not a rewrite. After Nov 1 there is no renderer change ([SC-05](history/lessons-ledger.md)). Partial reversals are on the cut list: two fixed tilt states (cut 5) and no 3D models in v1.0 (cut 3). When a trigger reaches either, it becomes the owner's question that week, with a recommendation, and the answer is a new dated entry here.

### D3 · GitHub Pages now; Steam eventually

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "For now, have a github pages but eventually have a steam release."
- **What it means:** v1.0 ships free on GitHub Pages (target Sun Feb 7 2027, when the owner ships it). itch.io is dropped. From week 1 the code has the seams that make Steam a packaging job: one `src/platform/` module fenced by a lint rule, an async save store whose export file equals a desktop save, and an updates adapter that is inert on desktop. The Steam workstream is a proposal from Mon Mar 1 2027 (D15).
- **Why:** Pages needs no store accounts before February; V1's desktop build had no updater, a fake build id and no signing ([DP-09](history/lessons-ledger.md), [K15](history/salvage-matrix.md)); the seams cost about a day now and a rewrite later.
- **How to reverse:** Adding itch.io back is a zip upload plus an iframe save test, about two days. Going Steam-first would add Electron, account onboarding and the 30-day wait after paying the fee before any release ([Steam Direct](https://partner.steamgames.com/steamdirect), via search excerpts, checked 2026-10-12, as in PLAN §11; check before relying on it), and would move the first release past February.
- **Since replaced in part:** D4 and D12 (both 2026-10-12, below) replace "v1.0 ships free on GitHub Pages" and the updates adapter: the Pages build is a preview, and Steam is the only release. D15 (2026-10-12, below) replaces the Steam timing: a demo at Next Fest in February 2027, then the full release; D18 (below) moves the fest to June 2027.

### D4 · Steam will be the only version, at about US$10

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "Steam version will eventually be the only version, it will have a relatively low price around 10 dollars, but it is un important for now."
- **What it means:** Steam is the only public release, priced around US$10; the exact price, regional prices and discounts wait for the store page. There is no public web release: Feb 7 becomes "v1.0 complete on the preview", a game-complete build the owner may choose to `/ship 1.0.0`, and the public site, landing page, public credits page, the Jan 22 soft launch for about ten people and the `LAUNCHED` switch are gone, while the web release checklist becomes a v1.0 completion checklist (PLAN §4, §10, §11). The Steam workstream (Claude's code from Mon Mar 1 2027 at the earliest, release Tue Jul 13 2027) stays a proposal under D15, and no Steam work is added now. Replaces D3's "v1.0 ships free on GitHub Pages" and the open question of what Steam adds over a free web build.
- **Why:** The owner's call. Dropping the public web release frees about 0.75 Claude-weeks in weeks 15–17, more than dark mode's 0.6 (D13); D12 counts the rest of the saving.
- **How to reverse:** A public web release later needs an offline app, an update prompt, web save migrations and a final origin before any outside player saves (about 1–1.5 Claude-weeks), plus a decision on how a free web build relates to the paid Steam one.
- **Since replaced in part:** D15 (2026-10-12, below) replaces "timing later" and the Mar 1 / Jul 13 proposal: a free demo at Next Fest in February 2027, the full release after Mar 1. D18 (below) moves the fest to June 2027 and the release after it (proposed Tue Jul 27 2027, D22).
- **Since replaced in part:** D22 (2026-10-14, below) sets the full release for Tue Jun 29 2027, about a week after the fest, in place of the Jul 27 proposal above.

### D5 · The title is interbus

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D5 go with interbus."
- **What it means:** The game's title is interbus, matching the repo, the package and the `interbus.*` save keys; "Intralines" stays the name of V1–V3. Check the name before the Steam store page: INTERBUS is also the name of an industrial fieldbus that Phoenix Contact has offered since 1987, standardised in EN 50254 and IEC 61158 and hosted by PROFIBUS & PROFINET International since 2011 ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INTERBUS), via a search excerpt, checked 2026-10-12; also [Phoenix Contact's blog](https://blog.phoenixcontact.com/marketing-gb/tag/interbus/), via search excerpts). A web search on the same date found no Steam game called Interbus; Steam's own search ([store.steampowered.com/search/?term=interbus](https://store.steampowered.com/search/?term=interbus)) could not be opened from the session, so run it before the store page. Neither is a trademark search; check before relying on it.
- **Why:** The owner's call; one name everywhere.
- **How to reverse:** Before the store page, a new title changes the splash, the window title and the docs; save keys and the repo can keep `interbus`. After it, the store page and capsule art change too.
- **Since dated:** D15 (2026-10-12, below): the name check (a Steam search and a trademark search) is due Sun Nov 1 2026, before the Steam fee names the app. D18 (below) re-dates it to Sun Feb 7 2027, still before the fee.
- **Since amended:** 2026-10-14, D25's copy pass: the same name starts with a capital where players read it as a title ("Interbus" on Home's heading and the browser tab), since the owner asked that capitalisation be right everywhere. The repo, the package and the `interbus.*` save keys are unchanged.

### D6 · The co-developer is the 3D modeler and artist

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: the co-developer "is the 3d moddler/artist and will be making designs to be used."
- **What it means:** The artist makes the bus models the game uses (`metro-40`, `sparrow`, `goliath`, then a depot) to the contract in [docs/art/MODELS.md](art/MODELS.md), a draft the artist amends. The first box-level blockout is due Sun Oct 25; the checker, icon renderer and CI job are built around it in week 5, CI only; the 2D bus icons are rendered from the models, so 2D and 3D match. Later: landmark models and the Steam capsule art.
- **Why:** Two earlier pipelines were built before any model existed and never received one ([RN-07](history/lessons-ledger.md), [X-15](history/lessons-ledger.md)). Building around the first real file avoids that.
- **How to reverse:** Nothing in the game depends on models. Without any model the game uses a code-drawn top-down icon. Cut 3 (no 3D models in v1.0; 3D shows icons over extruded buildings) still uses the artist's models for the 2D icons, and is the owner's call when its trigger fires.
- **Still open:** the artist's hours and delivery route (D6 below).
- **Since answered:** 2026-10-12, in the next entry.
- **Since dated:** D15 (2026-10-12, below) brings the Steam capsule art forward: the capsule set by Thu Dec 10 2026 and library art by Sun Jan 10 2027 ([art/MODELS.md](art/MODELS.md) §11). D18 (below) moves them after v1.0, to Sun Feb 21 (latest Sun Mar 21) and Sun Apr 11 2027, and the depot back to Jan 10 if the buses are done; D20 (below) puts the art under an open licence.

### D6 (second part) · Hours untracked; PRs with Git LFS; the owner and the artist are the playtesters

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D6: hours unimportant, agree with recommendation with everything else, with me and [the artist] as the playtesters, more to be added later."
- **What it means:** The artist's hours are not tracked; the hour estimates in [docs/art/MODELS.md](art/MODELS.md) §10 only size the work. Deliveries come as a pull request under `art/models/` with Git LFS (the recommendation), not a shared folder. The playtesters are the owner and the artist, with more added later; no tester is invented. P2 (Dec 12–13) and P3 (Jan 22, which replaces the soft launch) are sessions the two of them run: each starts a fresh save and writes notes before talking. The owner watches every build and is never untold, so the artist is the fresher tester and skips the M4 milestone check (Dec 6) to come to P2 fresher. With two insiders the untold targets and P2's onboarding trigger are weak signals; the week-9 scripted pacing run ([GD-10](history/lessons-ledger.md)) is the objective check (PLAN §12 risk 6). No merge reaches `main` from 18:00 the day before a playtest until its notes are committed, and each session records the build chip's SHA at its start and end ([PR-13](history/lessons-ledger.md)). Once outside players are named, they take P2 and P3 first (PLAN §9); in the week of Dec 21 Claude asks the owner to name one or two for P3 or confirm that P3 stays the two of them (recommendation: name them). Under the Steam proposal, outside players reach the beta branch before the store page.
- **Why:** The owner's call. The PR route puts every file through the art check and LFS from the first delivery ([DP-11](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** Naming players upgrades P2 and P3 in any week. A shared-folder route costs the owner a few minutes per delivery to commit the file.
- **Since replaced in part:** D15 (2026-10-12, below) replaces "outside players reach the beta branch before the store page": the store page goes up in December, and the Next Fest demo's public players (from Feb 1 2027) are the first outside signal unless P3 testers are named. D18 (below): the page goes up in March 2027 and the demo's players arrive from May 24.

### D7 · Derived starting cash; peak, off-peak and overnight headways; no import

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D7: Starting cash should scale with the amount of money it takes for you to start a bus line or two, doesn't have to be decided on now, otherwise peak, off peak and overnight head way periods, no imports."
- **What it means:**
  - **Starting cash is derived, not a fixed number:** k × the cost of a typical starter line (a depot, four stops and the buses its peak and off-peak headways need), computed from the config rows so it follows prices, and written once at founding as the ledger's first row ("Founding capital"), so a later retune never changes an existing save (I1: cash = Σ ledger rows). k is tuned in week 9, not now. At V1's prices one Sparrow line costs about $261k ($150k depot, a $95k Sparrow, $16k of stops), so k ≈ 1.2 reproduces V1's $310k ([X-09](history/lessons-ledger.md)); "two lines" means one shared depot plus two lines' stops and buses, k ≈ 1.43. The cheapest four-bus line costs about $546k, and it must cost at least 1.15× the starting cash so [GD-11](history/lessons-ledger.md)'s middle game survives, which caps k near 1.8; k's config-test band is 1.0–1.8. Invariant I10 asserts three things: the sweep's best profitable first line, found independently of the reference line, fits the starting cash and turns a daily profit within a quarter (shown failing with a fare that makes every first line lose money); the best four-bus line the sweep finds costs ≥1.15× the starting cash; and k stays in its band.
  - **Three headway periods per line: peak, off-peak and overnight**, replacing the plan's "one city service day (05:00–24:00)". Period times are tunables shared by every line (proposed: peak 06:30–09:30 and 16:00–19:00, off-peak the rest of 05:00–24:00, overnight 00:00–05:00). A new game starts at 05:00 on day 1; founding sets peak and off-peak to one headway and overnight to off, so the checklist still asks for one number. Format 1 holds all three headways from the Nov 8 freeze even if the overnight control lands a week later.
  - **Recommendation applied with it** (the owner can overrule it): peak and off-peak always run, at most 60 minutes apart (a tunable); overnight runs no more often than off-peak, or is set to "no service", so neither a night-only nor a night-mostly line can exist; each limit is one `sim/rules` predicate with a cursor-card refusal. Riders board only in periods a line runs. Contracts, the report card and the "needs N buses" count read peak and off-peak (05:00–24:00), the count being the most any running period needs (since the M3 spec's item 15 gave each period its own round trip, the longest round trip over the shortest headway: the same where the peak is both slowest and busiest, and never short of what the day plan sends out; Measured, 2026-10-13); overnight counts at most toward Coverage. This keeps V2's midnight-only exploit closed ([EC-03](history/lessons-ledger.md): +461% in the crew's sweep, +149% on the review's re-run) along with its family: I3 checks per-period conservation (riders boarding a line in a period ≤ trips generated in that period's hours); I11 fails if a HEADWAY contract can be met by one period alone; the sweep runs "daytime at the longest headway, overnight at the shortest" and "overnight toggled alone", each of which must earn less than the best all-period setting, a check shown failing with V2's whole-day ridership restored; stop capacity and spill are checked per period.
  - **Recompute with three periods:** paths are searched once and shared, and only the per-period logit and assignment repeat. The week-3 bench measures that form, and the per-period figure is recorded below.
  - **No V1 save import.**
  - **Fare scope** was not in the answer, so per-line fares, the recommendation, stand; the owner can change this before the format freeze on Sun Nov 8.
- **Why:** A derived amount stays right as prices are tuned. Three periods give the player a night choice without reopening EC-03.
- **How to reverse:** A fixed starting cash is one config row. Before Sun Nov 8, dropping or reshaping the periods is a config and schema change of about a session; after it, a save-format bump (web saves are set aside on a bump anyway, D12).
- **Since clarified:** 2026-10-13, measuring the debug line (3,535 m by street) with V1's cycle formula: a 3–4 km line every 20 minutes needs 2 Sparrows, not 1. So the starter recipe costs $356,000, and founding capital at k = 1.2 is $427,200 (it was $313,200). The recipe counts its buses with the game's one cycle function (K07; `src/sim/economy/starter.ts`, M2 step 1). The rule itself, k × the starter line, is unchanged.
- **Since tuned:** 2026-10-14, the tuning freeze (Measured, below): k is 1.333, so founding capital is $474,548. With the two-Sparrow starter, I10's 1.15× clause caps k at 1.333 for a four-bus line of the starter's shape ($546,000), not "near 1.8" as above, which was the one-Sparrow starter's figure.
- **Since clarified:** 2026-10-21, the systems audit's seventh round: no period runs a bus more often than once a minute (`MIN_HEADWAY_SECONDS`, in the same `sim/rules` predicate, refused as "Run at most once a minute"). The line card offers every 5 minutes or longer, so play never meets it; a save running a line every second with 2,000 buses took 3.3–3.8 s a recompute, at every command and every close (`npm run bench headways`). D7's other limits are unchanged.

### D8 · The look: "sleek but detailed and intricate"

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner (same words as D2).
- **What it means:** "Quiet base, loud data" (PLAN §8): the whole real city in low-chroma tones with fine linework that appears with zoom; saturated colour only for the player's network, buses and overlays; road hierarchy by width and casing; one typeface; a light map with night as a tint. Which palette ships, light or dark, and the font are D13.
- **Why:** References the plan proposes: V3's recorded "Subway Builder: sleek and modern, but usable" target, whose author is not recorded, and Protomaps' light style. Both keep the city quiet and the network loud. The owner's own reference screens are due Sun Oct 25 (PLAN §14 item 14). One palette avoids doubling colour QA ([RN-13](history/lessons-ledger.md), [RN-14](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** The style is generated from one token file, so a different palette is a token swap until the Jan 17 content freeze.
- **Since amended:** D13 (2026-10-12, below) adds a dark theme and picks the font, replacing "a light map" and the one-palette reasoning above.

### D9 · The revised Out list is accepted

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D9: accept recommendation."
- **What it means:** The Out list in PLAN §2 stands; anything moved in names something moved out. With D4 and D12 it also lists the public web release (itch.io, a custom domain, offline play, the update prompt, web save migrations, the landing page) as dropped. `docs/SCOPE.md` copies it in week 1.
- **Why:** One city and one loop done well ([GD-03](history/lessons-ledger.md), [GD-12](history/lessons-ledger.md), [SC-05](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** A new dated entry that moves an item in and names the one moved out.

### D10 · Failure: the PLAN §3 floor

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D10: Accept recommendation."
- **What it means:** Cash may go negative to a credit limit with one lender; five days below it ends the run, with a countdown chip and **Restart this quarter** from an automatic save. Sandbox turns failure off. Built in week 6 (M3).
- **Why:** A real floor gives money weight, and a way back keeps the game calm ([GD-06](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** A gentler floor (a longer countdown, or no end) is a tunable until the Dec 6 system cutoff; no failure at all is Sandbox as the default.

### D11 · Boston's play area: Inner Boston

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D11: accept recommendation."
- **What it means:** The extent is Inner Boston (94 km², about 800 cells at 350 m). R2 (105 km², about 900 cells) is used only if recompute stays ≤100 ms p95 in week 3; that is a measurement on Sun Nov 1, not a new question. If the Nov 8 pack trigger fires, a core box that passes the bake gates ships and R2 and all of Boston become 1.1.
- **Why:** Measured on 2026-10-12, Inner Boston leaves 20% headroom under the 1,000-cell cap and 42% under the 16,000-node cap ([WD-09](history/lessons-ledger.md), [AR-15](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** R2 or all of Boston (with variable 350/700 m cells) after v1.0; before Nov 8 a different extent is a rebake.
- **Since clarified:** 2026-10-12, with D7's three headway periods, the 100 ms check times the three-period recompute (PLAN §5).

### D12 · The web build is a preview; saves don't matter yet

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D12: saves don't matter for the moment, the purpose of the web version is for me to b able to look at the game and test your work."
- **What it means:** The Pages build is a preview for the owner, and the artist, to look at the game and test Claude's work (PLAN §6, §11).
  - It stays on `bobbyjohn-nil.github.io/interbus`: no custom domain, DNS or `CNAME`.
  - No service worker, PWA, offline mode or update prompt. Assets are content-hashed, and an on-screen **build chip** (12 px, in the OSM-attribution corner) shows the commit SHA, so every test report names its build.
  - **`index.html` cannot be served uncached on Pages**, which departs from the first reading of this answer. Pages sets its own cache headers, reportedly `Cache-Control: max-age=600` on every file, and a site cannot change them ([Stack Overflow question 14798589](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/14798589/github-pages-http-headers), via a search excerpt, not an official page, checked 2026-10-12; the week-1 check records the real headers). Only a reload is sure to fetch the newest build.
  - **Old builds keep their files.** This is the preview's answer to "bugs out every time it updates" ([DP-01](history/lessons-ledger.md)). Pages replaces the whole site on each deploy, so a tab opened before a merge, or an `index.html` still in a cache, would ask for the previous build's hashed chunks (MapLibre, the model chunk, the sim worker) and city file, get 404s and fail mid-session; V1 hit exactly this (`1624f3b`, `d66e6dc`), and the PWA that later replaced V1's fix is dropped here. The deploy therefore copies the last two deploys' hashed `assets/` and city files into the new artifact, porting V1's `Intralines@7af45b1:scripts/retain-assets.mjs` (`d66e6dc`); an old tab runs its own build intact, and the chip shows which. If a file is gone anyway, `src/platform/web` turns the failed dynamic import, worker load or tile read (`vite:preloadError`, a 404) into one card, "This build was replaced · Reload". That is error recovery, not an update prompt: there is no service worker and nothing swaps. Since amended (2026-10-15, the second audit round): a game that fails for any other reason (its sim file unread after a server error or a dropped connection, its worker not loaded, or its worker failing mid-game, which is then stopped) shows one sibling card on the same Reload, in its own words: "Boston couldn’t be loaded" or "The game stopped working" (copy.ts, `failedText`); the replaced build's words stay for files that are gone (`e2e/errors.spec.ts`, `e2e/swap.spec.ts`). And since the third audit round (2026-10-15, below): once the game stops, every panel and card of it goes too, Saves opened from Home included, with the depot tint and a line being drawn, so only the card with Reload answers. And since the fifth audit round (2026-10-16, below): the map's own file unread for another reason (a server error, a dropped connection, MapLibre failing to mount, the load's timeout) says so in the map's card, "Boston’s map couldn’t be loaded.", with Reload, high on the board above Home and the founding card; while the game's own card shows, the map's says nothing, so one card answers. A committed e2e test opens build A, deploys build B to a local server, then loads every lazy chunk and a tile read in the A tab with no error, and fails without the retention step (PLAN I15, [TS-04](history/lessons-ledger.md)). About 0.25 session in week 1.
  - Every merge to `main` deploys, always; the deploy's last step loads the live URL with a cache-busting query and checks that the build chip and `version.json` show the SHA. The `LAUNCHED` switch is dropped, and tags publish nothing. No merge lands from 18:00 the day before a playtest until its notes are committed (D6).
  - On a schema change, older web saves are set aside under a backup key, as V3 does ([DP-06](history/lessons-ledger.md)), with a one-line notice and a fresh start; no web migrations are written. The save envelope and the Export session file record the build SHA, so Claude replays a bug report at that commit, and Export session can attach a set-aside save. Save migrations and update robustness move to the Steam build and start with its beta.
  - City tiles load by HTTP Range with ordinary browser caching and no Cache Storage copy. The week-1 Pages Range check stays, with a whole-file download as its fallback.
  - `src/platform/` stays: the desktop build still needs it.
  - With no service worker and no Cache Storage, V1's same-origin service worker can no longer wipe anything that matters, so the old reason for a custom origin is gone.
  - Replaces the open origin question and, with D4, D3's updates adapter.
- **Why:** The owner's stated purpose. Together with D4, removing the web release saves about 1.25 Claude-weeks (PLAN §10): about 0.5 in weeks 1–9 (PWA, tile cache, domain, nightly update test) and 0.75 in weeks 15–17 (soft launch, landing page, public release checklist). Keeping old deploys' files costs back about 0.1.
- **How to reverse:** Making the web build public again needs an offline app, an update prompt, migrations and a final origin before any outside player saves, about 1–1.5 Claude-weeks (V3's PWA, [S08](history/salvage-matrix.md)).
- **Since dated:** D15 (2026-10-12, below): save migrations start with the full Steam release, not with a beta branch; the Next Fest demo's beta branch opens in December 2026 without them, and the demo sets older saves aside like the preview. D18 (below) opens the beta branch in March 2027.
- **Since dated:** D20 (rest) (2026-10-14, below): the repo goes private on Sun Mar 14 2027, a week before the store page is public. On GitHub Free that unpublishes the preview; on GitHub Pro the preview would stay public to anyone with its address. What the preview becomes is D20 (preview) (Open, by Sun Feb 21 2027).

### D13 · Light and dark modes; the font is Afacad Flux

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D13: have light and dark modes, font Afacad Flux."
- **What it means:**
  - Light and dark themes from **one style generator and two token sets**. The theme follows the system setting, with a toggle in settings (week 14), and changes no sim number, save byte or rule verdict (I16). Replaces D8's "a light map with night as a tint" and its one-palette reasoning.
  - Palette and contrast tests ([S21](history/salvage-matrix.md)) run on each theme that exists: light from week 1, dark from week 13, when its token set lands; until then the generator only has to accept a token set, and no dark work starts early (it would be look work before M3). They run in three states, light at day, light at the darkest tint step, and dark, because two palettes double colour QA ([RN-13](history/lessons-ledger.md), [RN-14](history/lessons-ledger.md)). Bus icon ink and halo and the player's line colours are theme tokens the tests cover; I12's geometry runs once, on light, with bus-versus-base contrast checked per theme; I13 runs on each theme. If the dark runs push PR checks past 8 minutes, they go nightly. On Sun Nov 8 the network hues are checked against the light set and a placeholder dark land and void (two hex values), so January adds only the dark base tokens.
  - No separate night palette (the recommendation): the day/night tint applies in light mode and is off or much reduced in dark mode. In both themes the clock chip names the period (Peak, Off-peak, Overnight) and a line with overnight off fades while it is stopped, from one `sim/rules` predicate, so the time-of-day read never depends on the tint (RN-13's warning, made sharper by D7's overnight period).
  - The light token set is picked on Sun Nov 8 (warm-neutral or V1 paper, side by side on Boston) and ships with the prototype; the dark set lands in the January look week (week 13). Dark mode makes the close-zoom lane markings the likely January cut. If dark is not green by the content freeze (Jan 17, or Jan 31 under the M5 trigger), it becomes the owner's question that week, with the recommendation that dark waits for Steam, in which case P3, the v1.0 checklist and the theme tests run light only; a decided item is never cut automatically.
  - **Afacad Flux** is a variable font (weight 100–1000, slant −14 to 14) under the SIL Open Font License 1.1, designed by Kristian Möller (Dicotype), © 2023 The Afacad Project Authors, with the subsets latin, latin-ext and vietnamese, per its folder in the Google Fonts repository ([google/fonts ofl/afacadflux](https://github.com/google/fonts/tree/main/ofl/afacadflux); [`METADATA.pb`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/fonts/main/ofl/afacadflux/METADATA.pb) and `OFL.txt`, checked 2026-10-12; the fonts.google.com page could not be opened from the session). The folder ships only the variable `AfacadFlux[slnt,wght].ttf` (437,512 bytes on 2026-10-12), so the static instances are generated. The game self-hosts it because it loads only its own files. The build pins a google/fonts commit and the file's SHA-256 like a city source, cuts static 400 and 600 instances with a pinned fontTools `varLib.instancer`, and subsets them into two Latin woff2 files for the UI. In a trial subset with fontTools 4.66.1 on 2026-10-12 (not committed; the week-1 script re-measures it), the two came to about 23–29 KB together depending on the range, against 85–98 KB for the whole variable font and 35–42 KB with only slant pinned; the fonts count toward the 200 KiB menu budget. The same instances feed MapLibre's SDF glyph PBFs: all 256 ranges, empty where the font has no glyphs, so no glyph request 404s, with the glyph tool chosen in week 1. Every style `text-font` is one of the two Afacad stacks, and `glyphs` and `sprite` are relative (a style test). The licence text ships with the credits.
- **Why:** The owner's call. One generator, a fixed three-state test matrix and theme-blind geometry tests keep the second palette from doubling the manual work.
- **How to reverse:** Dropping dark deletes a token set and its test runs; a different font is a token and a glyph build, until the Jan 17 content freeze.

### D14 · Copyright is held by Miticept Studios, the two members jointly

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D14: Miticept Studios, as the two of us."
- **What it means:**
  - Copyright in the game, art included, is held by Miticept Studios, meaning its two members jointly; the studio is not a legal entity. `art/LICENSE.md` lands by Sun Oct 18, before the first `.glb`, and reads in substance: "All files under `art/` and the generated `public/models/` are © Miticept Studios (its two members jointly), all rights reserved; they are not covered by the code licence."
  - The rest of the recommendation stands. Both city files are published under ODbL as public release assets with the bake recipe in this repo (from week 5). By **Sun Dec 6 2026**, a week before the Steam store page is submitted on Dec 14 (was Sun Mar 21 2027, moved by D15), the two members sign a short written agreement covering: joint copyright; the studio's right to use, change and sell the work in the game, its updates and its marketing on every platform, permanently, even if either member leaves; the Steamworks and Apple accounts held by the owner (D15) and what happens to the app and its revenue on a transfer; the revenue split; the credit line; who keeps the source `.blend` files.
  - The repo licence is still open (D20, by Dec 6). Until it is chosen, all rights are reserved.
  - Not legal advice; if either member is unsure, ask someone qualified before signing.
- **Why:** The owner's call. The account holder (the owner, D15) is not the sole author, and Steam's onboarding expects whoever signs the distribution agreement to have the right to publish everything in the game ([onboarding](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/gettingstarted/onboarding), via search excerpts, checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on it). The signed agreement is what gives the owner that right.
- **How to reverse:** A different holder (one member, or a company once formed) is a new signed agreement and a one-line change to `art/LICENSE.md`; after the store page is public, the store's developer and publisher names change too.
- **Since re-dated and answered in part:** D18 (below) moves the signed agreement to Sun Feb 21 2027, a week before the store page is submitted on Mon Mar 1; D20 (below) keeps the code all rights reserved and puts the studio's own art under an open licence, which the agreement records, and narrows `art/LICENSE.md` to that art (`art/models/`, `art/store/`, `public/models/`), so `art/refs/` and anything listed in `art/CREDITS.md` keep their own licences.
- **Since answered in part:** 2026-10-14 (below). D20 (rest) makes the art's licence CC BY 4.0 with the credit "Miticept Studios", which the agreement records, and makes the repo private on Sun Mar 14 2027; a private repo's releases are not public, so the city files need a public home outside it before then. D21 (a) keeps the accounts the owner's, as an individual trading as Miticept Studios, with no company, so the agreement also records Steam's payments to the owner and how they are shared, the owner's right to publish the artist's work, and what happens if either member leaves or a company is formed later. "Miticept Studios" in the © lines still means the two members jointly.

### D15 · Next Fest in February 2027; macOS first, then Windows, both at launch; the account held by the owner for now

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D15: aim for next fest February, start with mac and then windows both at launch, for now as miticept studios is small it will be under me and will be transfered if needed."
- **What it means:**
  - **A free demo at Steam Next Fest, February 2027.** Valve's dates (search excerpts of [Next Fest Feb 2027](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/marketing/upcoming_events/nextfest/feb_2027), checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on them): the fest runs Mon Feb 22–Mon Mar 1, 10:00 PST; registration closes Sun Jan 10, 23:59 PST, and needs the base game's Coming Soon page already public; the demo and its store presence must be in review by Mon Jan 25 (one third-party tool says Jan 18) for the Press Preview, which opens about Thu Feb 11; everything by Mon Feb 8; the game may not release before the fest ends. Valve pulls trailers for its own fest trailer on Mon Jan 18 and recommends the page carry its newest marketing (trailer, capsules) by Jan 10 (same page). A game gets one Next Fest, ever.
  - **The plan's dates** (PLAN §10, §11): Steamworks sign-up by Oct 25; the name check and Apple enrolment by Nov 1; the US$100 fee by Nov 15, so Valve's 30-day wait ends Dec 15, before anything is public; Apple approval aimed for Nov 22, the certificate and notary key set by Nov 29; the D14 agreement, D20, a Steam build account, a second test account and demo keys by Sun Dec 6; store copy and screenshots approved Wed Dec 9; capsules from the artist Thu Dec 10; the Steam gate Fri Dec 11 18:00, when the P2 merge freeze starts; store page submitted Mon Dec 14; the February-or-June choice Sun Dec 20, with the prototype; Coming Soon public and registration targeted Sun Jan 3, with Fri Jan 8 the last gate (Jan 3–8 is the holiday-review slack) and registration by Sat Jan 9 (Valve's deadline Jan 10); library and demo art by Jan 10; the freeze candidate on the beta branch Fri Jan 15; the demo, cut from RC1, in review Wed Jan 20 (three business days before Valve's Jan 25) and public Mon Feb 1; everything in review by Feb 8. The full release follows the fest, proposed Tue Apr 13 2027 (D22). This replaces the earlier proposal (Claude's Steam code from Mar 1, Next Fest in June, release Tue Jul 13 2027) and D4's "timing later".
  - **The demo** (PLAN §4; scope sign-off D19): all of Inner Boston, flat and tilted, for the first two quarters (about 40 minutes), ending on the report card and a wishlist card; Export session stays in, so a player's bug report replays at its commit; later quarters, medals, Sandbox and slots held back; the artist's models and the dark theme only if green in RC1. RC2 goes public on Feb 1 if Valve confirms an approved demo needs no new review (check), else by Feb 8. An emergency demo, the weeks 11–12 round-trip build with the demo flag, waits on a second beta branch in case the RC1 demo is not approved by Feb 1 (PLAN §11). Uploading or setting live a Steam build never creates a version, and `/ship` never uploads to Steam; the demo is never given its own version heading or `v*` tag. CI uploads and sets builds live only on password-protected beta branches; only the owner sets a build live on a default branch.
  - **macOS first, then Windows, both at launch.** "First" is the order of work: the Mac pipeline (one universal app, Developer ID signing, notarization and stapling, which Valve requires for new Mac apps; [platforms](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/store/application/platforms), search excerpt, checked 2026-10-12) is the long pole, so it is built and tested first, and Windows (an unsigned x64 folder) comes from the same script a week later, smoke-tested nightly on a Windows runner and played on a real Windows PC by the Jan 8 gate. The demo ships both: macOS was about 2% of Steam's users in September 2026 ([GamingOnLinux](https://www.gamingonlinux.com/2026/10/steam-survey-for-september-2026-shows-a-drop-in-linux-with-simplified-chinese-rising/), checked 2026-10-12). The wrapper is V1's Electron `app://` shell with six fixes (PLAN §6); the Steamworks SDK, achievements, Steam Cloud and save migrations wait for the full release in March.
  - **The account is the owner's** for Steamworks and the Apple Developer Program (US$99 a year; [enrol](https://developer.apple.com/programs/enroll/), checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on it). If the studio becomes a company, Valve transfers the app ([transfer](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/gettingstarted/managing_apps/transfer), search excerpts, checked 2026-10-12): the receiving partner onboards fully and pays its own US$100 fee, both sides confirm, and Valve approves in about 2–7 business days; wishlists, reviews and sales move, Web API keys do not. Apple converts an individual membership to an organization once the company has a D-U-N-S number. Valve advises forming any company before onboarding ([onboarding](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/gettingstarted/onboarding), search excerpts, checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on it).
  - **Cost, plainly.** Valve's calendar fits; PLAN §10's Sessions column did not. The demo track is about 8 extra Claude sessions (6.5–10, about 3.2 Claude-weeks) on top of a column already about twice the old pace, and when they fall matters more than how many: 5.25 fall Nov 30–Jan 3 (week 8 +1.75, week 9 +1.5, week 10 +0.5, weeks 11–12 +1.5) and feed the Dec 11 gate, the Dec 14 store page, the Dec 20 choice and the Jan 3–8 round trip; 2 fall in January; 0.5–1 fall in the fest weeks, from their buffer. No January cut can pay for December sessions, and the December lever frees about 3 at most (D18), so **February needs at least about 2.25 extra sessions in December even with every December cut; without them the Dec 11 gate fails and June is the answer.** Keeping February open to Dec 20 costs about 3.75 extra sessions and about 10 owner hours in Nov 30–Dec 20, all reused under June; choosing June at D18 (Nov 22) avoids the December crunch. The owner adds about 18–31 hours by Mar 1: Steamworks onboarding, tax and bank 2–4; Apple enrolment and the certificate 1–2; the name and trademark check, D20 and D21 1–2; drafting and signing the D14 agreement 1–2; the two GitHub environments and their secrets, the Steam build account and its Steam Guard refreshes 1–2; approving store copy, screenshots and the Content Survey 3–5; the demo app, the test account, keys and registration 2–3; installing and playing builds (the holiday round trip on the Mac and the Windows PC, every demo build on both) 4–6; fest week 3–5. The artist adds 6–12 hours of capsule, library and demo art. Feb 7 holds; no Steam or desktop session starts before M3, so money-first holds. February is likely the weaker fest commercially: a page public from late December likely enters with a few hundred wishlists (estimate, from about 150 in a page's first two weeks, [How To Market A Game](https://howtomarketagame.com/2021/12/12/how-many-wishlists-can-you-expect-when-you-launch-your-games-coming-soon-steam-page) 2021, and [presskit.gg](https://presskit.gg/blog/steam-next-fest-2026-results)'s medians, search excerpts, checked 2026-10-12), and wishlists going in best predict fest results (presskit.gg: games entering with under 1,000 gained a median of about 460 in February 2026).
  - **Questions for Valve at onboarding:** (1) does the 30-day wait after the fee block a public Coming Soon page or demo; (2) does the free public preview, or the public repo, affect Next Fest eligibility; (3) does registering and then withdrawing spend the game's one Next Fest (answer recorded here before registering); (4) does the demo app need its own US$100 fee; (5) how is store and build review staffed over Dec 21–Jan 3; (6) would an ad-hoc-signed Mac demo pass review if Apple's approval stalls (asked the week of Nov 22 if Apple has not approved); (7) what trader or contact details does Steam show publicly for an individual partner account, for example to players in the EU? Question 7 is unverified: Steam may show such details for an individual account, but a search on 2026-10-12 found no Valve page on it; add the URL and the date once checked, and check before relying on it.
  - Still open from the old D15 row: the Windows test PC, the owner's legal name in Mac signatures and question 7 (D21); the preview and the repo's visibility (D20).
- **Why:** The owner's call. The plan keeps June as the fallback because a game gets one fest and the data favour a page with months of wishlists behind it.
- **How to reverse:** Before registering (by Jan 9), switch to the June 2027 fest (registration Sun Apr 25, everything in review Mon May 31, fest Mon Jun 14–21; [June 2027](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/marketing/upcoming_events/nextfest/june_2027), search excerpts, checked 2026-10-12) with release Tue Jul 27, clear of the Summer Sale (D22); nothing done by then is wasted, and the demo track moves after Feb 7 with no extra sessions. After registering, Valve's answer to question 3 says whether withdrawing spends the fest, and the emergency demo is the fallback. Dropping macOS removes the Apple membership and notarization; moving the account to a company is Valve's transfer above.
- **Since replaced in part:** D18 (2026-10-12, below) replaces the February aim with the June 2027 fest, and with it this entry's dates, demo track, cost, gates and question 5; macOS first, then Windows, both at launch, and the owner's accounts stand.
- **Since answered:** 2026-10-14 (below). D21 (a) keeps the accounts the owner's, as an individual trading as Miticept Studios, with no company and no transfer planned, so the transfer above is now only the way back; the Mac version is sold only on Steam, and the owner's legal name is in its signatures. D21 (b) makes the artist's PC the Windows test PC. D20 (rest) makes the repo private before the store page is public, so question 2 asks only about the preview. D22 sets the full release for Tue Jun 29 2027, in place of the Apr 13 proposal and the Jul 27 in How to reverse. Question 7 is still asked by Feb 7, with a new question 8 (D21 (a)).

### D16 · The Dec 20 prototype is not a version

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D16:no".
- **What it means:** The prototype gets the non-`v` milestone tag `prototype` on Sun Dec 20, not `/ship 0.1.0`; the changelog's first version waits until the owner types `/ship`. Demo builds are not versions either (D15).
- **Why:** The owner's call; the plan already assumed the tag.
- **How to reverse:** The owner can type `/ship 0.1.0` at any later point; the `prototype` tag stays where it was made.

### D17 · No existing transit: the city starts with no buses, trains or ferries

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "since this is a game that is starting from zero bus system, have existing bus lanes be replaced with regular lanes so that there is no existing system", then, offered (A) remove all public transit, (B) rail as scenery only, or (C) keep the T running: "go with a for now".
- **What it means:** The bake removes every public-transport feature from the sim graph and the map tiles (PLAN §5): bus lanes and bus-only lanes become general lanes, bus-only surface streets open to all traffic, dedicated busways and bus tunnels go, and bus stops, stations, shelters, routes, all rail (subway, light rail, commuter rail, Amtrak, freight spurs) and ferries are removed. Former rail corridors draw as plain ground. Logan Airport stays as a destination. Gate G10 fails any bake where a transit feature survives. The demand model's bus–car–walk choice runs from scratch, with no allowance for today's T riders. MBTA data stays a bake-time calibration input only.
- **Why:** The player builds Boston's bus network from nothing, so the map must not show one that already exists. A red bus lane then always means the player paid for it.
- **How to reverse:** "For now" means this can return later as a feature: the T (or rail as scenery) would be new bake rules, a drawing layer and, for a running T, a transfer and competition model in the sim, sized and decided on its own. Before the pack format freezes (Sun Nov 8) keeping rail as scenery only is about a day; after it, any change is a city rebake and 1.1 work.

### D18 · Next Fest in June 2027, not February

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D18: C.", option C of the open D18 row (how the February fest is paid for): the June 2027 fest instead.
- **What it means:**
  - **Replaces D15's February aim.** D15's other parts stand: macOS first, then Windows, both at launch; the Steamworks and Apple accounts held by the owner until a transfer.
  - **The February demo track goes:** no extra Claude sessions, no December Steam gates or levers, no Dec 20 February-or-June choice, no emergency demo, no February deadlines for onboarding, the fee, Apple, the capsules or the store page. PLAN §10's column to Sun Feb 7 is back to its pace before D15, v1.0's hard deadline is Sun Feb 28 again, and no Steam or desktop session starts before v1.0 is complete or Mon Mar 1, whichever comes first; if v1.0 uses its buffer, the store copy, screenshots and shell slide and the store page goes in by its Mar 29 latest. The owner's paperwork can happen any time before its latest date. The Electron, signing and notarization design (PLAN §6) is unchanged, only later.
  - **Valve's June dates** (search excerpts of [Next Fest June 2027](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/marketing/upcoming_events/nextfest/june_2027), checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on them): the fest runs Mon Jun 14–Mon Jun 21, 10:00 PDT; registration closes Sun Apr 25, 23:59 PDT, and needs the Coming Soon page public; the demo and its store presence in review by Mon May 17 for the Press Preview (from an earlier search only), which opens Thu Jun 3, and Valve's general page advises submitting a demo that goes public before the Press Preview at least five weeks before the fest, here Mon May 10 ([Next Fest](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/marketing/upcoming_events/nextfest), search excerpt, checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on it); everything in review by Mon May 31; no release before the fest ends. A game gets one Next Fest.
  - **The plan's dates** (PLAN §10, §11): targets, with the latest date where it is later, each latest date a week or more before Valve's deadline. D21's account-holder part by Sun Jan 17 (a company only if chosen by about Dec 20); Apple enrolment any time (US$99 a year, so a membership started from now covers the July release), latest Sun Jan 31; Steamworks sign-up as D21 says, paperwork, bank and tax (started by Jan 24), Valve's questions 1–4 and 7 and the name check (D5) by Sun Feb 7; the US$100 fee by Sun Feb 14, so the 30-day wait ends Tue Mar 16, before anything is public; the D14 agreement, D20's preview part, D21's Windows PC and the artist's capsules by Sun Feb 21; Apple approved with the Developer ID certificate and Team API key in the desktop jobs, the store copy and screenshots, and D19 by Sun Feb 28 (store material at the latest Sun Mar 21); the store page in review Mon Mar 1 (latest Mon Mar 29); a Steam build account, a second test account, demo keys and the password-protected beta branch by Sun Mar 7; Coming Soon public by Sun Mar 21 (latest Sun Apr 11; the Spring Sale is reported for Mar 18–25, so Mon Mar 29 also works, PLAN §11); the Windows PC in hand by Sun Mar 28, when Valve is asked question 6 if Apple has not approved; the Steam round trip on both OSes and the library and demo art by Sun Apr 11; registration by Sun Apr 18; the demo in review Mon May 3 (latest Mon May 10, Valve's five-week advice; Press Preview deadline May 17) and public Mon May 24; the full release proposed Tue Jul 27 2027 (D22), its build in review by Mon Jul 12.
  - **Cost** (estimates): Claude about 7.5 sessions for the demo (Feb 8–May 9), 0.5–1 in fest week and 3.75–6.25 for the full release (from May 10), about 12–15 over 24 weeks (a D19 trailer moves 0.5–1 of the full release's share before Apr 18, leaving the total unchanged); the owner about 18–31 hours as D15 counted them, spread over January to July; the artist 6–12 hours of store art after v1.0.
  - **Questions for Valve at onboarding:** D15's list, less question 5 (holiday staffing); question 6 (an ad-hoc-signed Mac demo) is asked only if Apple has not approved by Sun Mar 28.
- **Why:** The owner's call, and the one the data favoured (D15): a page public from Mar 21 gets about 12 weeks of wishlists before the fest, against about 7–9 under February (late December or Jan 3 to Feb 22), wishlists going in best predict fest results, and the calendar to Feb 7 needs no extra sessions.
- **How to reverse:** Before registering (Apr 25), the October 2027 fest (expected, dates not announced; [Notebookcheck](https://www.notebookcheck.net/Valve-announces-Steam-Sales-and-Festivals-for-first-half-of-2027.1344181.0.html), search excerpt, checked 2026-10-12) or a release with no fest. February 2027 is no longer reachable without the demo track's extra sessions from Nov 30.
- **Since answered in part:** 2026-10-14 (below). D21 (a) settles the account holder, so the Jan 17 answer date and the Dec 20 company date go; Apple enrolment (latest Jan 31) and the Steamworks steps (by Feb 7, tax started by Jan 24) stand and wait on nothing. D21 (b): the Windows PC is the artist's, ready on Steam by Sun Mar 28. D22 sets the full release for Tue Jun 29 2027 in place of Jul 27: its build goes into review Mon Jun 14 (latest Jun 21) instead of Jul 12, the full release's 3.75–6.25 sessions fall May 10–Jun 13, and the owner's hours run January to June. The rest stands.

### D19 · Deferred: the demo's scope and any trailer

- **Deferred:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D19: We can deal with trailers and demo gameplay after we start with the actual game."
- **What it means:** Nothing is decided about what the demo holds or whether there is a trailer until game work is under way, and no session goes to either before then. It comes back with the prototype on Sun Dec 20 and is decided by Sun Feb 28 2027, before the store page goes into review (the page may show only what the game will have) and before the demo flag is built from late March. The plan's starting point stays PLAN §4's: the first two quarters of Inner Boston, about 40 minutes, ending on a wishlist card. The old "no trailer before the fest" recommendation, made only because February had no session for one, is withdrawn: under June a trailer costs about 0.5–1 session from the replay and screenshot scripts, made in PLAN §10's weeks 25–30 and taken from the full release's estimate, so the Steam total stays about 12–15.
- **Why:** The owner's call: the game comes first.
- **How to reverse:** Deciding earlier is a new dated entry here.

### D20 (first part) · The art under an open licence; the code all rights reserved

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "D20: art work can be open license but code shouldn't."
- **What it means:**
  - **The code** is © Miticept Studios (D14), all rights reserved. While the repo is public, the source is visible, but with no licence default copyright applies, so no one may copy, share or adapt it beyond what GitHub's terms allow ([GitHub docs](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/licensing-a-repository), search excerpt, checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on it). GitHub's Terms of Service still let other GitHub users view and fork a public repo on GitHub ([ToS §D.5](https://docs.github.com/en/site-policy/github-terms/github-terms-of-service), search excerpts, checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on it). A short `LICENSE` says so and points to the art's and the city files' own licences (PLAN §14 item 1). Replaces the open code-licence question and the option of an open licence for the code.
  - **The art** goes under an open licence: the studio's own art, meaning the artist's models under `art/models/`, the icons generated from them in `public/models/`, and the Steam store art under `art/store/`. Reference images in `art/refs/` and anything listed in `art/CREDITS.md` are not the studio's to relicense and keep their own licences and credits, so `art/LICENSE.md` says so. Copyright stays with Miticept Studios (D14), and the D14 agreement records the licence, which both members must accept because it lets anyone reuse their work. Which licence is still open (D20 in Open, recommended CC BY 4.0, by Sun Oct 25); until it is chosen the art stays all rights reserved, because an open licence cannot be withdrawn from copies already shared.
  - The city files stay ODbL (D14), Afacad Flux stays OFL 1.1, and the preview and the repo's visibility stay open (D20 in Open).
- **Why:** The owner's call.
- **How to reverse:** Opening the code later is a `LICENSE` change. The art's licence cannot be withdrawn from copies already shared; only files published afterwards could carry other terms.
- **Since answered:** D20 (rest) (2026-10-14, next entry): the art is under CC BY 4.0 with the credit "Miticept Studios", and the repo goes private before the store page is public. The preview's part stays open (D20 (preview)).

### D20 (rest) · The art under CC BY 4.0; the repo private before the game is public

- **Decided:** 2026-10-14, by the owner: "D20: follow your recommendation for the art license, but for the github repo, rightnow no one knows about this other than Miticept staff so when we go public we will make the repo private beforehand." This answers the Open D20 (rest) row. The art licence follows the recommendation. The repo's visibility goes against it. The preview's part stays open as D20 (preview).
- **What it means:**
  - **The art is under CC BY 4.0, with the credit "Miticept Studios".**
    - It covers the studio's own art: the artist's models under `art/models/`, the files generated from them in `public/models/` and the store art under `art/store/`.
    - It never covers `art/refs/` or anything listed in `art/CREDITS.md`, which keep their own licences.
    - Anyone, other games included, may share and adapt the art for any purpose, even commercially, if they give that credit. The grant cannot be withdrawn from copies already shared, whether they came from the repo or from the game ([deed](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/)).
    - It licenses no trademark or patent rights, so the name interbus and any logo are not licensed ([legal code](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/legalcode.txt) §2(b)(2)). Both sources are search excerpts, checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on them.
  - **The artist's agreement** (D20, first part).
    - The artist commits `art/LICENSE.md` naming this licence by Sun Oct 18 (PLAN §14 item 8), before the first `.glb` (Oct 25). That commit records the artist's agreement.
    - Until the commit lands, the art stays all rights reserved.
    - If the artist does not agree, the file says "all rights reserved" and the question returns to the owner before Oct 25.
    - The D14 agreement (Sun Feb 21 2027) records the licence.
  - **The repo goes private before the game is public.**
    - "When we go public" is read as the Coming Soon page, the game's first public appearance (target Sun Mar 21 2027, latest Sun Apr 11).
    - The owner makes the repo private on **Sun Mar 14 2027**, a week before the page. That is after v1.0's hard deadline (Sun Feb 28), because the v1.0 checklist runs on the preview. If the page moves, the switch moves to a week before it.
    - The code stays © Miticept Studios, all rights reserved.
    - This replaces the recommendation that the repo stay public.
  - **What the switch does.** These are GitHub's docs, read from their source in the github/docs repository, checked 2026-10-14; check before relying on them.
    - On GitHub Free, the Pages preview is unpublished automatically. Public forks are detached and stay public; there were none on 2026-10-14. The repo leaves the GitHub Archive Program ([visibility](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/managing-repository-settings/setting-repository-visibility)).
    - In a private repo, Pages, rulesets (D24) and environment secrets need GitHub Pro or a higher plan ([Pages](https://docs.github.com/en/pages/getting-started-with-github-pages/what-is-github-pages), [rulesets](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-rulesets/about-rulesets), [environments](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/deployments-and-environments)).
    - Even on Pro, a Pages site stays public to anyone with its address. Only an Enterprise Cloud organization can publish privately ([plans](https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/learning-about-github/githubs-plans)).
    - On Free, Pro and Team, required reviewers exist only in public repos (environments page).
    - Actions stops being free. Private repos get 2,000 included minutes a month on Free and 3,000 on Pro. After that it costs US$0.006 a minute on Linux, US$0.010 on Windows and US$0.062 on macOS, with each job rounded up to a whole minute. With no payment method on file, Actions is blocked once the quota is used up ([billing](https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions), [prices](https://docs.github.com/en/billing/reference/actions-runner-pricing)).
    - Only people with access to the repo can see its releases ([releases](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/about-releases)). So before the switch, the city files need a public home for ODbL (PLAN §11). The scripts that download them without a token (`scripts/cities/fetch.mjs`, `tools/bake/sources.mjs`) then need that home or a token.
  - Nothing already public comes back. Clones, forks and copies made before the switch stay wherever they are.
  - **Still open:** what the preview becomes at the switch, and the GitHub plan (D20 (preview), Open, by Sun Feb 21 2027). With it, Valve's question 2 (D15) now asks only about the preview.
- **Why:** The owner's call. Today only the studio knows about the game, and its source should not be public once the game is. The licence is the recommendation, and it is settled before the first `.glb` because it cannot be taken back.
- **How to reverse:** Before Mar 14, not making the switch keeps today's setup, with free Pages and free Actions. Making the repo public again later publishes its whole history. The art's licence cannot be withdrawn from copies already shared; only art published afterwards could carry other terms.

### D21 (a) · The Steamworks and Apple accounts are the owner's, as an individual trading as Miticept Studios; no company; the Mac version sold only on Steam

- **Decided:** 2026-10-14, by the owner: "D21A: owner as an individual, un the DBA miticept studios, not selling the game on the app store but having a apple os version fo the app being sold in the steam store, so no need for incorporation." This answers the Open D21 (a) row with the recommendation. It adds that no company is formed and that there is no Mac App Store version.
- **What it means:**
  - **The owner opens and holds both accounts as an individual**, trading under the business name Miticept Studios.
    - No company is formed, so no D-U-N-S number, Apple organization membership or Valve transfer is planned.
    - The Dec 20 company date and the Jan 17 answer date go.
    - D15's "for now" and its transfer become only the way back.
  - **Steam.** Search excerpts of Valve's pages, checked 2026-10-14; check before relying on them.
    - An individual onboards as a sole proprietorship, with the owner's legal first and last name as the company name, not a DBA. The bank account must be in that same name ([onboarding](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/gettingstarted/onboarding), [FAQ](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/gettingstarted/faq)).
    - The store page's developer and publisher are separate fields set for each game ([creator homepage](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/store/creator_homepage)), so the page can show "Miticept Studios".
    - Sign-up, paperwork, bank and tax are done by Sun Feb 7, with the tax check started by Jan 24, as before.
  - **Question 7 stands:** what trader or contact details Steam shows publicly for an individual.
    - On 2026-10-14 no Valve page about trader details was found.
    - In the Steam Subscriber Agreement, Valve itself is the seller ([agreement](https://store.steampowered.com/subscriber_agreement/), search excerpt, checked 2026-10-14). That suggests no partner details are shown, but this is an inference, and EU commentary disagrees.
    - The owner asks it with questions 1–4 by Feb 7, and enters any public details only after the answer.
    - If the answer shows more than the owner accepts, such as a home address, it becomes the owner's question that week.
  - **New question 8 for Valve:** may an individual partner publish a game whose copyright two people hold jointly (D14), given a signed agreement that gives the owner the right to publish it?
    - Valve's FAQ asks for "the legal entity that owns or has rights to publish the game" (FAQ above).
    - A forum quote of an older onboarding page, from about 2016, said co-owners must form a legal entity ([GameDev.net](https://gamedev.net/forums/topic/678972-steamworks-16/)). That wording was not found on the live page on 2026-10-14. Unverified; check before relying on it.
  - **Apple.** Checked 2026-10-14; check before relying on it.
    - The Mac version is sold only through Steam, never the Mac App Store. The membership (US$99 a year) is used only for Developer ID signing and notarization (PLAN §6), so no App Store seller name shows anywhere.
    - An individual enrols under their legal name. An organization must be a legal entity with a D-U-N-S number, and Apple accepts no DBA or trade name ([enrol](https://developer.apple.com/programs/enroll/)).
    - A signing certificate's name includes the team name ([certificates](https://developer.apple.com/help/account/certificates/certificates-overview); format per Apple staff in [forum thread 811970](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/811970)). An individual's team name is the owner's name, so every signed Mac build carries the owner's legal name. `codesign -dv --verbose=4` on the first signed build confirms it.
    - The repo never writes that name (CLAUDE.md). The desktop jobs therefore hold it as a secret, and the logs mask it (PLAN §6).
  - **The D14 agreement** (by Sun Feb 21 2027) now records:
    - that the owner holds both accounts as an individual and Steam pays the owner;
    - how that revenue is shared;
    - the owner's right to publish the artist's work (question 8);
    - what happens if either member leaves or a company is formed later.
    - "Miticept Studios" in the © lines keeps meaning the two members jointly, while on the accounts it is the owner's trade name, so the agreement says both.
    - Not legal advice: whether two people sharing revenue under one trade name are a partnership, for tax or otherwise, is for someone qualified to answer before signing.
- **Why:** The owner's call. With no Mac App Store sale, the owner's name shows only inside the signed Mac builds and in what Steam's onboarding holds. No company means no formation cost and no wait for a D-U-N-S number.
- **How to reverse:**
  - A company later: Apple converts an individual membership once the company has a D-U-N-S number ([account](https://developer.apple.com/support/account/), checked 2026-10-14; check before relying on it), and Valve transfers the app. Under D15, the receiving partner onboards fully and pays its own US$100 fee. Builds signed before then keep the owner's name.
  - A Mac App Store version would need its own build and Apple's review. It is not planned (PLAN §2).

### D21 (b) · The artist's PC plays the Windows demo builds

- **Decided:** 2026-10-14, by the owner: "D21B: the artist." This answers the Open D21 (b) row with the recommendation.
- **What it means:**
  - The artist's own PC is the demo's Windows machine from the April round trip on: the Steam round trip by Sun Apr 11, the 60-minute soak of the Windows package by Sun May 2, the demo checklist's Steam install that plays to its card, and every demo build after that, fest hotfixes included. Nothing is bought or borrowed. Sun Mar 28 becomes the date it is ready on Steam.
  - The PC must run Windows 10 or later, 64-bit, on an x64 processor, because the Windows build is an x64 folder (PLAN §6). The artist confirms the OS and version with the answers to [MODELS.md](art/MODELS.md) §1 (by Oct 18). If the PC does not qualify, the fallback (a PC bought or borrowed, in hand by Mar 28) returns as the owner's question.
  - Builds reach the PC only from Steam's password-protected beta branch, since desktop builds never become CI artifacts (CLAUDE.md). The branch password and any key reach the artist privately, never through the repo.
  - The plan assumes the artist plays each build there and notes its SHA, while the owner plays the same build on the Mac and alone sets builds live (D15). It also assumes the full release's Windows check uses the same PC. The owner can say otherwise.
  - The nightly smoke test of the Windows package on GitHub's `windows-latest` (PLAN §6) stays. It shows the package starts; the artist's PC is where a person plays it.
- **Why:** The owner's call, and the recommendation: no purchase, and a PC someone uses every day.
- **How to reverse:** Name another Windows PC. That costs nothing before Sun Mar 28. After that date, the new PC repeats the Steam round trip before the next demo build is set live.

### D22 · The full Steam release a week after the fest: Tue Jun 29 2027, inside the Summer Sale

- **Decided:** 2026-10-14, by the owner: "D22: its fine if it coincides with the sale, just get it out like a week after the fest ends." This answers the Open D22 row and departs from its recommendation (Tue Jul 27 2027, after the Summer Sale).
- **What it means:**
  - **The date is Tue Jun 29 2027**, eight days after Next Fest closes (Mon Jun 21, 10:00 PDT; [June 2027](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/marketing/upcoming_events/nextfest/june_2027)), on the plan's usual Tuesday. Mon Jun 28, exactly a week, also works, with a day less slack. It is the sixth day of the Summer Sale, Thu Jun 24 to Thu Jul 8 2027 ([seasonal sales](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/marketing/discounts/seasonalsales)). Both are search excerpts of Valve's pages, checked 2026-10-14; check before relying on them.
  - **Discounts** ([discounts](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/marketing/discounts), [seasonal sales](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/marketing/discounts/seasonalsales), search excerpts, checked 2026-10-14; check before relying on them).
    - A game cannot join a seasonal sale in its first 30 days, so interbus is not in the Summer Sale.
    - It may have a launch discount, set up before release, running 7 to 14 days and at most 40% off.
    - Valve says a launch discount of up to 14 days may run into a seasonal sale. A 14-day launch discount from Jun 29 runs to about Jul 13.
    - The first seasonal sale it can join is the Autumn Sale 2027, whose dates are not announced.
  - **The release build goes into review Mon Jun 14**, as the fest opens, and Mon Jun 21 at the latest. Review takes 3–5 business days ([review](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/store/review_process), search excerpt, checked 2026-10-12; check before relying on it).
    - From Tue Jun 15, the date moves only through Valve ([Coming Soon](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/store/coming_soon), same check).
    - So the full release's work falls May 10 to Jun 13, beside the demo going public on May 24, instead of May 10 to Jul 12. That work is Steamworks, achievements, disk saves with Steam Cloud, migrations, update tests, the Mac check and the release checklist, 3.75–6.25 sessions. The Steam total stays about 12–15 sessions (PLAN §10).
    - Its features stay behind the build flag, so demo hotfixes never ship them (I16). Fixes found at the fest ship as the release's first patch.
- **Why:** The owner's call: release while the fest's players and wishlists are fresh, and accept the sale's competition, rather than wait five more weeks.
- **How to reverse:** Before Tue Jun 15, set a new date in Steamworks and add a dated entry here; after that, only through Valve. Tue Jul 27, after the sale, is the fallback if the release build is not in review by Mon Jun 21 (PLAN §10).

### D23 · Claude opens and merges pull requests into `main`

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "yes to your question", answering "May I open and merge pull requests into `main` myself, merge commits only and only when CI passes?"
- **What it means:** The session that owns `main` (CLAUDE.md) opens a pull request from its branch and merges it itself, with a merge commit, once every check on the pull request's head is green (CI, and the required "Versions only via /ship"). It never squashes, rebases or force-pushes `main`, never merges during a playtest freeze, and calls a change done only when the deploy's live check passes on the merged commit (CLAUDE.md "Done"). Three kinds of pull request stay the owner's to merge: a `/ship` release, a rebake after the Jan 17 content freeze, and anything that changes who can publish (the deploy's triggers, its environments or their secrets). The owner can revert any merge, and a revert is merged the same way.
- **Why:** Every merge to `main` deploys the preview (D12), so without this nothing reaches the preview between the owner's visits; CLAUDE.md's "one session owns `main`" assumed it ([PR-07](history/lessons-ledger.md), [PR-08](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** Say so; Claude then opens pull requests and leaves every merge to the owner.

### D24 · `main` is protected, and `v*` tags are locked

- **Decided:** 2026-10-14, by the owner, who set it up on GitHub ("done"). It answers the Open row on a `v*` tag ruleset with On, and does PLAN §14 item 2.
- **What it means:**
  - **`main`:** a branch ruleset on the default branch. Changes reach it only through a pull request, with "Versions only via /ship" a required check, and no approval required, since Claude's merges go out under the owner's account (D23). Deleting `main` and force pushes are blocked.
  - **Merges:** squash and rebase merges are off in the repository's settings, so a pull request merges with a merge commit only.
  - **`v*` tags:** a tag ruleset blocks updating or deleting them. Creating one stays open, since `/ship` creates them. No `v*` tag exists yet (the repository's tags are the city files' releases), so it targets none until the first `/ship`.
- **Why:** CLAUDE.md's rules (merge commits only, versions only via `/ship`) become GitHub's rules too, so a session's slip is refused rather than noticed later. The tag ruleset cannot tell who pushes; it keeps a released tag where it was made.
- **How to reverse:** Settings → Rules → Rulesets, to disable or delete either ruleset; Settings → General → Pull Requests, for the merge methods.

### D25 · The owner's initial playtest: what changes, and the clearance to move on

- **Decided:** 2026-10-14, by the owner, in the notes of the initial playtest ([docs/playtests/2026-10-14-owner-first-look.md](playtests/2026-10-14-owner-first-look.md), kept verbatim), ending: "fix the errors and the changes I described in the playtest thing, and I grant you permission to move on in the schedule if you run out of things to do." The owner asked that every sentence be read as written, including what worked.
- **What stays:** the map ("it is boston"), and the industrial land showing where a depot can go.
- **What changes**, each a small pull request in this order (Claude's readings in brackets, to be corrected by the owner):
  1. **Pathing:** no more loops in Back Bay and downtown. Stops and depots never stand on a motorway or a ramp, and buses drive one-way streets both ways (Measured, 2026-10-14).
  2. **Time:**
     - minutes and days on the clock;
     - 8 days to a quarter, 4 quarters to a year;
     - 1x is real time, a second a second, with faster speeds above it. [The ladder is 1x, 60x, 600x, 1,800x and 5,400x. 5,400x is today's fastest, and 600x and 1,800x are where every pacing number was measured. The chip shows the day within the quarter: "Year 1 · Q2 · Day 4 · 08:15". Contracts ask for a quarter in a row within two (8 days within 16). The report card's grant and fine are stated per day, so a quarter's card pays what its days earned. The fixed step falls from 30 game seconds to 1, so buses glide at 1x. Saves are set aside once (DP-06).]
  3. **Map:**
     - the demand dots toggle by kind, from a custom menu [homes in purple and jobs in blue, the only two kinds; both on at first, and the choice remembered in that browser; the menu says how many each kind stands for, so it is the legend too];
     - the population count shows only while the pointer is on a dot [a home dot's card names its people and the people within a walk; a job dot's, its jobs and the jobs within a walk];
     - the dashed circles with figures on them go [they are the contract rings, which the Contracts panel already lists; a festival's ring stays, as the event's only mark on the map];
     - every word on screen is in sentence case, with proper grammar, held by a guard test [`src/ui/copyRules.ts`, checked on every screen from Home to a day's close by `e2e/copy.spec.ts` and on every table of words; US spelling in what players read (Color, closer), since the game is Boston's and priced in dollars, with code and docs as they are; the title reads "Interbus" on screen, while the repo, package and save keys stay `interbus`];
  4. **Finance:**
     - charts of money in and out over the days [Finance opens on an Overview: cash, yesterday's and the quarter's running money, and a chart drawn in SVG by hand, no library, of the last two quarters' days (16 at most): the cash at each close as a line from Day 0, each day's money in above the axis and running costs below, and a mark under a day that bought or sold something. The table is the Breakdown view, where each group's heading gives its subtotals and folds it; Buying and building starts folded, and Finance is at most 52% of the window's height, so it stays inside I13 however late the game];
     - plain labels, with where the money goes, such as bus maintenance and stop upkeep [the rows split into what was bought (buses, new stops, stop upgrades, depots) and sold back, grouped as money in, rewards, running costs and buying and building; the per-km cost reads "Bus fuel and maintenance", since it already holds fuel and wear and PLAN §2 keeps per-bus wear out; stops gain an upkeep of 1‰ of their build cost a day (a Sign $4, a Transfer Hub $341), a new running cost, since the owner named it as one Finance should show];
     - each line's finances moved here from the line panel [the Lines view lists each line's riders, fare, yesterday's net and a day's forecast, and opens a line to show its split and the fares that net the most. The fare is picked here, since a fare is money; the line card still names the fare and links here];
     - the opening balance labelled "Day 0", not "Founding capital" [the starting cash is posted on Day 0, the money's day only: the clock still opens Day 1 at 05:00].
  5. **Building:**
     - a Build tab with Place stop (a stop that stands alone until a line uses it), Draw line (today's tool, reusing placed stops) and Place depot [a placed stop is a Sign for $4,000 on the street nearest the click, refused within 250 m of a stop already standing, where the line tool would join that stop instead; it costs its upkeep and counts for nothing (coverage, contracts) until a line calls there, so EC-07's poles that scored without service cannot come back; it reads no Quiet flag while it waits; and while no line calls there it can be taken down for half its cost. Build is one dock button that opens the three tools in a menu above it, so the dock has one slot fewer];
