     - the line panel shows only the line's information;
     - Edit route opens an expanded editor to reorder, add and remove stops, like Google Maps' trip planner [the editor sets the line's whole route in one command, one undo: it buys new stops and any bus the longer round trip needs, keeps the buses a shorter one leaves spare, and a stop it leaves that no other line calls at comes down for half its cost, as Remove does; Extend and Insert fold into it. A click on the map adds a stop at the end of the list, as a trip planner adds a destination, and the player drags or moves it into place; the draft's stops are numbered on the map to match the list; the card gives the route's change in length where Insert warned of a detour].
  6. **Panels and controls:**
     - panels can be moved and several used at once (the owner preferred this to one closing another) [each panel opens in a free place beside those open, the first at the top right and the next to their left, or below them when a row is full, and stays there; dragged by its heading it opens there again for the session; a press brings it to the front; a tool's card is a panel too, so a panel no longer puts the tool down; Escape closes them all; I13 measures each panel alone, as several open is the player's choice];
     - Saves leaves the bottom tabs for the top [a chip at the top right, the top bar's chips wrapping before they reach it];
     - the browser's dropdowns become custom ones [the line card's and new-line card's bus and headway menus join the speed and fare menus on the game's Dropdown, a locked bus greyed with its reason as the option's hint; `src/ui/controls.test.ts` fails if any screen draws a `<select>` again].
  7. **3D:**
     - double-click and hold to switch 2D and 3D [held still for half a second it flips; held and dragged, up tilts and down flattens; a quick double-click still zooms; only with no tool out, since a tool's first click is a real one];
     - the view rotates in 3D [dragging sideways turns it, with the right button, Ctrl or a held double-click, and so do Q and E; N or the compass faces north; 2D always faces north];
     - [the 2D | 3D chip goes and `T` stays. A compass half its size keeps a visible way into 3D, which the M5 spec never cuts, and faces the board north; the other half of its room goes to the demand menu, since I13's budget at rest is spent].
- **The clearance:** once these are built and deployed, work moves on to the schedule's next items without waiting for their calendar weeks. The first is the tuning freeze from the scripted pacing run, ahead of P1. CLAUDE.md's hard limits stand:
  - no Steam or desktop session before v1.0;
  - no renderer change after Sun Nov 1;
  - no new sim system after Sun Dec 6;
  - versions only through `/ship`.
- **Why:** the owner played the preview and named what confused or annoyed them. The owner's direction also brings the 3D and look items forward, ahead of PLAN §10's one session in three for look work before M3 is signed off.
- **How to reverse:** any item, by saying so. Each is its own pull request and can be reverted alone. The time change sets older web saves aside, and a revert does not bring them back.
- **Since amended:** 2026-10-14, the tuning freeze (Measured, below): the ladder's 4 is 2,700× (45 minutes a second, a game day in 32 s), not 1,800×, which with k 1.333 (D7, since tuned) brings the scripted player's second line on Inner Boston into PLAN §3's 15–25 minutes. 1×, 60×, 600× and 5,400× stay. The owner can move the rung back by saying so.

### D26 · Systems that work first; the real values later

- **Decided:** 2026-10-14, by the owner, while the gold measurements ran: "the money is not important right now, right now whats important is that the system work and we can fine tune the real values later".
- **What it means:**
  - Work goes to every system working end to end: no feature that fails, no screen that disagrees with the sim, no command that leaves state inconsistent, no undo or save that loses state. [First an audit of the game's twelve systems, each finding confirmed by running it and reproduced independently, then the confirmed bugs fixed in small pull requests, most severe first.]
  - Values wait: prices, rates, the pacing minutes and the medal bars are tuned later. What is merged stays (#102's Coverage fix, which was a bug; #103's k 1.333 and speed 4 at 2,700×, with their nightly bands), so the game keeps one set of numbers while the systems are fixed.
  - The gold measurement (a scripted player that builds toward the card, wider candidates and a coverage bench) is parked, not merged; the medal ladder (Open above) keeps its recommendation as the default until the values pass.
- **Why:** the owner's priority. A value tuned on a system that does not work yet is tuned against a bug, as the Coverage grade was until #102.
- **How to reverse:** the owner says the values come first again; the gold measurement picks up where it stopped.

### D27 · Fewer tokens a session: no stage a stronger model makes redundant

- **Decided:** 2026-10-17, by the owner: "can you find parts of the current workflow that is unproductive or redundant and remove or adjust them, we want to decrease the token usage", then "are there any major systems that are redundant due to the advanced nature of models? remove it if not needed".
- **What it means:**
  - **STATUS holds only what is live, waiting, in progress and next.** It had grown to 92,726 bytes (347 lines), about 60 KB of it finished work already in git, `CHANGELOG.md`, this file and the merged pull requests, and every check-in read it whole; it is now about 15 KB. Its long form stays at `git show a5c0ccd:docs/STATUS.md`, and the preview's feature list is `CHANGELOG.md`'s Unreleased, not a second copy.
  - **The session-start hook prints STATUS only at a start or a clear**, not on a resume (the conversation already holds it) or a compaction (its summary carries what is in flight): each restart of a session's worker had added another copy.
  - **A session searches this file** (318,778 bytes) for the decisions its task touches rather than reading it whole.
  - **The check-in fires every six hours, not hourly, with a fast path** (every two hours from 2026-10-17; every three from the owner's "change check ins to every 3 hours", 2026-10-19; every six from the owner's "actually every 6 hours", 2026-10-20): when `main` has not moved, no pull request is open and the next item is blocked, it fetches, answers in one line and reads nothing else, and firings queued together get one answer. Seven firings queued in P0's freeze, each asking for the whole file.
  - **No pull request only to cite a deploy:** the live check is cited in the next pull request's STATUS. #137 was a pull request, a CI run and a deploy for one line.
  - **CI is waited on through the pull request's events**, not by polling it.
  - **The audit loses its separate verifier.** One agent per finding reproduced it before the fixer reproduced it again as its failing-first test (TS-04); 16 of 157 findings were refuted, all as the recorded design or already known, which a fix checks in this file anyway. A fix sets aside, with its reason, a finding that does not reproduce or is the recorded design. Rounds still find bugs (40, 29, 18, 14 and 12 confirmed in rounds 1–5), so the audit stays, at most three lanes a round aimed at the newest code (the seventh round started six).
  - **The session fixes findings itself**, one small pull request at a time, in place of a fixer agent per batch that rebuilt context the session already had.
  - **A pull request that changes code has one independent reviewer** with both lenses (correctness, then the repo's rules and on-screen text), in place of two; a docs-only one has none. Fresh eyes still pay: the reviews of rounds 5 and 6 caught a take that blocked a Load, words untrue on screen and a cache with no bound.
  - **Nothing else runs as a workflow** (the owner, 2026-10-17: "if you think multi agent workflows are inefficient and turning them off will be the same quality of work then turn it off or at least tune it down"): docs, investigations, measurements and small changes are the session's own work. The owner's earlier "run more parallel agents" gives way to this.
- **Why:** the owner's priority. Each of these stages caught misses a session's own checks now catch, or rebuilt context a session reads from git. What guards the owner's say stays: versions only by `/ship`, the playtest freeze, the deploy's live check, TS-04's failing test and the review before a merge.
- **How to reverse:** the owner says so; the check-in goes back to hourly, workflows go back to every task, and STATUS's long form is in git.

### D28 · P0's merge freeze lifted before its notes

- **Decided:** 2026-10-21, by the owner: "skip the playtest freeze for now".
- **What it means:** merges to `main` resume without P0's notes. P0 plays V1, not this build, so a merge changes nothing it measures. P0 is not cancelled: its notes still go in `docs/playtests/2026-10-17-p0-v1-baseline.md` whenever they come. CLAUDE.md's freeze still holds for later playtests (P1, Nov 16–22) unless the owner says otherwise.

### CL · Versions only on the owner's say

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "create a changelog that will only make versions with my say."
- **What it means:** `CHANGELOG.md` follows Keep a Changelog 2.0.0. Every player-visible change adds a line under Unreleased. A version exists only when the owner types `/ship <version>` ([.claude/skills/ship/SKILL.md](../.claude/skills/ship/SKILL.md), which Claude cannot start); it merges a `Release v<version>` commit through a pull request and tags that commit. `node scripts/changelog.mjs check` runs in CI as the required status check "Versions only via /ship" and fails any version not added by its own `Release v<version>` commit, any `v*` tag not on that commit, and any later edit to released notes. It cannot tell who made that commit; the CLAUDE.md rule and the owner's yes inside `/ship` are the guard. Every v1.0 date in the plan is a target for the owner's `/ship`, never automatic; milestone tags such as `prototype` never start with `v`.
- **Why:** V1's "one version per session" rule produced 24 versions in about 8 days and two sessions both cut v1.18 ([PR-15](history/lessons-ledger.md), [PR-08](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** Remove the CI workflow and the skill. Not recommended.

## Measurements to record (weeks 1–3; the desktop from Feb 8)

Filled in as they are measured, with the date and the commit, each by a committed script ([TS-08](history/lessons-ledger.md)):
- Boston's map file size (`pmtiles extract --dry-run`); whether GitHub Pages answers Range requests with `206` and no `Content-Encoding`; and the `Cache-Control` it sends for `index.html`, hashed assets and `version.json` (D12).
- Gzip size per chunk, the menu's critical path including its font bytes (D13), and the first map frame under software GL. **First frame done 2026-10-13: 1.2–1.5 s on Inner Boston (Measured, below).**
- Whether census.gov, LEHD and Geofabrik accept downloads from GitHub's runners (measured below: they do).
- The number of Inner Boston buildings with OSM `height` or `building:levels` (week 2). Rule: under about half, the Overture height join goes into the r1 bake in week 5 and off cut 1; otherwise it is dropped. Record the count and the result here. **Done 2026-10-13: 14.1%, so the join goes in (Measured, below).**
- Sessions merged against sessions planned (PLAN §10), on Sun Oct 18, Oct 25 and Nov 1.
- Recompute time on Boston in the three-period form, total and per period (on Nov 1; R2 only if ≤100 ms p95 with three periods, D11).
- In week 9, 2D and 3D frame rates on the owner's Mac, in current stable Chrome; from Feb 8 (D18), when Electron is pinned, the same inside the packaged app, and the packaged desktop apps' sizes on disk and to download.

**Measured**
- **2026-10-23** (`e2e/boot.spec.ts` and `npm run measure:bundle` after `npm run build:fixture`, on `main` a5b0ca3 with this pull request's splash): **the boot splash and the bus wipe** (PLAN §4's January charm moments, ahead under D25's clearance; S18, GD-08), V1's (`Intralines@7af45b1:index.html:218-283`, `src/ui/BusWipe.tsx`).
  - **What it is:** `index.html` draws the splash itself, so it shows while the game's own files load: V1's bus, the wordmark, a bar, and a line of depot chatter every 950 ms from a random start, twelve lines in US English and the copy convention. It takes `app.css`'s tokens and Afacad Flux, as the page reads `app.css` before it draws. Once the sim leaves loading (App.tsx), the bus turns its engine over for 0.9 s and tows the splash away in 1.5 s, the first time in a tab (the platform's `firstInTab`, in session storage). Later in the tab, or with less motion asked for, the splash goes at once. It takes no pointer, so nothing under it waits for it, and after 20 s with nothing come it says "Still loading. Reload the page if this stays.", in its screen reader's status too.
  - **Cost:** the menu's critical path grows 2.1 KiB, from 120.5 to 122.6 KiB of 200: `index.html` from 0.3 to 1.6 kB gzip, the styles from 4.5 to 5.2 KiB and the code from 85.7 to 86.0 KiB.
  - **Tests:** `e2e/boot.spec.ts` sees the splash while the game's code is held back 1.5 s, in its tokens and type and at the floor's 12 px, then the tow, then no tow on a reload in the tab, and none with less motion asked for; with the code never coming and the page's clock run on 21 s, the word to reload. Shown failing (TS-04) on `main`'s page, which drew nothing, and with each of its rules taken out. The screenshots that count the map's colours (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`, `e2e/tilt.spec.ts`) wait for the splash to go.
- **2026-10-22** (`e2e/i13.spec.ts` and `npm run build:fixture`, on the credits screen's commit): **panels on the founding card**, which the review of #157 left open (STATUS).
  - **Measured:** the founding card alone takes 9.6%, 11.8% and 14.9% at 1440×900, 1366×768 and 1152×720. With Help, opened by ? from a colour, the two take 22.9%, 25.9% and 31.6%; with Saves at its tallest, left open from Home through New game, 24.9%, 28.0% and 34.1% (that review read 27.9% at 1152×720). Alone in the game Help takes 16.6%, 18.1% and 21.8%, and Saves at its tallest 18.6%, 20.3% and 24.3%.
  - **Kept as it is:** two panels the player has open together, each held to 26% alone, as D25 holds the game's panels ("several open is the player's choice"); Help and Saves each close with their Close. Saves stays there so a game not yet founded can load a slot or a file instead (`e2e/saves.spec.ts`), or be saved before its company is (`e2e/start.spec.ts`). The test measures both and holds neither. If you would rather the founding card stood alone, it can step aside while a panel is open on it, and New game can close Saves as it closes Help.
- **2026-10-22** (`e2e/i13.spec.ts`, `e2e/help.spec.ts` and `npm run measure:bundle` after `npm run build:fixture`, on `main` 1b0bf4f with this pull request's Credits): **the credits screen** (PLAN §10's week 14, ahead under D25's clearance), as Help's second view.
  - **What it holds:** everything `LICENSE-DATA.md` credits (`src/ui/credits.ts`): the city's five sources, OpenStreetMap's credit first and linked to its copyright page; the font, with its licence's text; and the open-source packages, with their notices. The map's three sources link to their pages through the platform's allowlist (`externalLink`), the Census Bureau's two are credits in words, and each licence text is the build's own file (`ownFile`: `fonts/OFL.txt` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`), so the game still loads only its own files (WD-01). `src/ui/shell/Help.test.ts` fails if a credit that `LICENSE-DATA.md`'s table quotes, or the font's, is missing (shown failing without the LODES row, TS-04), and `e2e/help.spec.ts` if either licence text is not served.
  - **Where it opens:** Home's Credits, beside its Saves and Help, opens Help on its credits, in Saves' place; in Help, two tabs choose between the keys and the credits, as Finance's views do, the arrows moving along them.
  - **Its room:** beside Home's card it takes Help's room there, 16.4%, 18.5% and 22.6% at 1440×900, 1366×768 and 1152×720, scrolling inside 45vh. In the game Help takes 16.6%, 18.1% and 21.8%, as before its tabs, which fit inside its cap. Nothing is added at rest.
  - **Cost:** the menu's chunk grows by 1.1 KiB gzip, from 84.6 to 85.7 KiB, and its critical path from 119.4 to 120.5 KiB of 200.
- **2026-10-22** (`e2e/i13.spec.ts` and `npm run build:fixture`, on `main` 0790145 with [#157](https://github.com/bobbyjohn-nil/interbus/pull/157)'s Help): **Help's room** (PLAN §10's week 14, ahead under D25's clearance). In the game Help takes 16.6%, 18.1% and 21.8% with the shell at rest at 1440×900, 1366×768 and 1152×720, its tables scrolling inside 60vh; Saves' Help opens it in Saves' place, since the two together took 35.5% at 1152×720. Beside Home's card, in Saves' place there, Home and Help take 16.4%, 18.5% and 22.6%, inside Saves' 45vh, and Continue and New game close Help: carried onto the founding card, the two took 31.6%. Saves, which stays there so a game not yet founded can load a slot or a file instead, takes 27.9% beside the founding card (Open, STATUS). It adds nothing at rest: ? opens it, Home's Saves and Help sit side by side, and Saves' Help takes its last row's 88 px to spare, so Saves at its tallest stays at 24.3%. The menu's chunk grows by 1.3 kB gzip, from 86.3 to 87.6 kB of its 200 KiB.
- **2026-10-22** (`npm run bench lategame` on the three companies its late-game measurement kept, below, now summing each one's ledger from founding; on `main` 9cc5c4a with this pull request's bench): **where a company's money comes from and goes** (D26's values pass: the running costs and the city's money).
  - **The profit-only company** (seed 1, to day 201):
    - it took in $1,969,348 in fares and the city's subsidy (fares $1,068,708, the subsidy $900,640) and $2,337,126 in rewards (contracts $1,950,000, card grants $387,126);
    - it spent $1,282,719 running (wages $815,816, the cost a kilometre $330,277, depot upkeep $107,338, stop upkeep $14,949, dead-heading $14,340) and $2,280,000 buying (buses $1,425,000, depots $715,000, stops $140,000), from $474,548 at founding.

    Its fares and the subsidy cover its running 1.5 times, its fares alone 0.83 times: the subsidy and the card grants ($1,287,766) about pay the running costs. Rewards are 54% of all it took in.
  - **The card-building companies** (seed 1 to day 145, seed 2 to day 152): fares and the subsidy $3,409,078 and $4,701,272, three times their running ($1,136,830 and $1,467,874), and fares alone 1.65 and 1.75 times; rewards $2,838,737 and $2,739,434, 45% and 37% of all they took in; and $5,130,000 and $5,138,000 bought, $1,980,000 of it the five depots.
  - **Running costs** fall the same way in all three: wages 61–64%, the cost a kilometre 26–30%, depot upkeep 7–8%, stop upkeep and dead-heading about 1% each.
  - **What it means.** The running costs' rates (all but stop upkeep, D25's 1‰ a day), the fare, the subsidy and the card's payout are V1's (PLAN §3: prices stay V1's, and `calib.tripScale` scales demand), and nothing here asks to move them. A network that reaches the dense neighbourhoods earns three times its running costs in fares and the subsidy, and one on the corridors alone one and a half times, where its fares alone fall short. What tips the balance is the rewards, mostly contracts, whose $60,000–240,000 are V2's (`Intralines_City_dep@archive/intralines-city-v1:Gaming/src/10-core.js:289`), so they go with the question on what a contract asks (STATUS, In progress). The rates stay.
- **2026-10-22** (`npm run bench pacing` on seed 1 with `PACING_REPORT_ONLY=1`: the profit-only player to day 200, and the card-building player, `PACING_CARD_CENTS=16000 PACING_CANDIDATES=wide`, to gold or day 200, each with and without `PACING_CONTRACTS=off`; `npm run bench fares` and `npm run bench crowding`; on `main` a842dcb with this pull request's switch, but for the card-building player's run with contracts, which is the gold measurement's below, on 37965a4 with #144's harness, `src/sim` having changed only in comments since): **what the contracts carry, and the fare band** (D26's values pass; the M5 spec, item 2's tunables; GD-04).
  - **What the scripted player does with contracts.** It takes every offer at each close and never builds for one. On the profit-only run it took 69 in 200 days, each on the day it was dealt: 50 lapsed, 3 were still held at the end, and the 16 its lines met paid $1,950,000: 13 HEADWAY (three every 10 minutes at $240,000, five every 15 at $150,000 and five every 20 at $60,000) and 3 RIDERSHIP at 150 boardings ($60,000). That is as much as all its fares and subsidy over the run ($1,947,303). The first paid on day 54.
  - **Without them** (`PACING_CONTRACTS=off`) both players meet the early targets on the same days: profit on day 1 (5.4 minutes), the card on day 8, the second line on day 33 (23.4 minutes), bronze on day 8 and silver on day 29. After that the contracts pay for the growth:
    - the profit-only player draws its third line on day 81 instead of 55 and its fourth on day 161 instead of 102, and ends day 200 with 4 lines, 8 buses, Coverage 18.1 and $202,086, against 7 lines, 15 buses, Coverage 31.0 and $1,207,664;
    - the card-building player has no gold by day 200 (129.5 minutes): it stands at 78.9, Coverage 55.5, with 12 lines on 24 buses in 4 depots, where with contracts it got gold on day 144 (103.2 minutes) on 30.
  - **What it means.** GD-04 keeps contracts as the goal layer and the early game's cash channel. Here none pays before day 54, after the early targets, and the player changes nothing for them: most of the 50 that lapsed asked for service its network lacked (no RIDERSHIP ask of 400 or 800 ever paid), and the 16 that paid were paid for service its lines happened to run. That pays for three of the six lines the profit-only player draws after its first and for most of its late cash ($1.2 million, the late game's measurement next). Whether an offer should ask for service its landmark does not have yet goes to the owner with next week's questions (STATUS, In progress).
  - **The fare band** (`FARE_BAND_SHARE`, the fares within 5% of the best fare's net; `npm run bench fares`, Inner Boston's ten corridor lines on Sparrows every 10 and 15 minutes). On the lines that earn, the band holds one or two fares, $1.75 and $2.00, around a best fare of $1.75 or $2.00. That is under the $2.25 reference, at or below which a paid boarding brings the whole $1.60 subsidy. On East Boston, which loses about $2,600 a day at its best fare, every fare from $1.00 to $5.00 nets within 5% of that, so all are in the band, which tells the player the fare does not matter there. The share stays.
  - **Crowding** stays (K27; `npm run bench crowding`). At calibrated demand, Inner Boston's ten corridor lines turn away nobody on Sparrows or Metro 40s every 10 and 15 minutes (8,407 riders a day on Sparrows). On Sparrows every 20 minutes, Washington Street turns away 127 a day at 17:00, 2.1% of the ten lines' 5,891. The profit-only company turns away none of its 4,676, and the card-building company at the 30-bus ceiling 4.7–4.8% (the late game's measurement, next). So full buses turn riders away only on the busiest corridor at a long headway, and at the ceiling.
- **2026-10-22** (`npm run bench lategame`: seed 1's profit-only player to day 200, and the gold measurement's card-building player, `PACING_CARD_CENTS=16000 PACING_CANDIDATES=wide`, on seeds 1 and 2 to gold or day 152; on `main` da186a6 with this pull request's bench): **what a late company can spend its cash on** (D26's values pass; the late game at the bus ceiling, Open above). Each company is measured on its first day with no event on, after playing on as it stands through any that is: seed 1's profit-only company through a festival on day 201, whose riders more than doubled that day's net, and seed 1's card-building company through the last day of roadworks, day 145. On that day, every stop's next tier, every line's bigger bus (at its headways, and less often, at the longest steps that keep its seats an hour) and every line a step more often are each played for a day and taken back. Each kind's best per stop or line that pays alone is then played all together.
  - **The profit-only company** (seed 1, day 202): 7 lines, 15 buses in 3 depots and 35 stops, with $1,218,303 and $4,182 a day more.
    - Its buses never fill: they turn away none of 4,676 riders a day. So no bigger bus pays at its headways (the 14 cost $9,075,000), and run less often the two best together cost $330,000, bring $781 a day (423 days) and free two bus places.
    - 34 of 35 Shelters pay alone; together they cost $408,000 and bring $1,835 a day (222 days; the best alone 57).
    - Two of its seven lines pay a step more often, both on spare buses, $355 a day together for nothing. Two more on spare buses lose, and the three that need a bus do not pay.
    - So about a third of its cash has a use that pays back, in 222 days. The ceiling never binds: it stops at 15 buses because no corridor line scores.
  - **The card-building company at the ceiling.** Seed 1 on day 146, two days after gold: 14 lines, 30 buses in 5 depots and 75 stops, with $455,533 and $46,922 a day more. Seed 2 on day 153, with no gold: 13 lines, 30 buses and 77 stops, with $1,309,380 and $50,254 a day more. Full buses turn away 4.7–4.8% of their riders (1,292 a day of 27,078 and of 27,424), up to 729 on one line.
    - Shelters: on seed 2 all 77 together cost $924,000 and bring $7,221 a day (128 days; the best alone 24). On seed 1, 74 of 75 pay alone (the best in 18 days), more than its cash and credit buy at once.
    - Bigger buses at their headways: seed 2 can afford every one, and 3 of 21 pay alone. The best is on its most crowded line (590 turned away): Metro 40s for $637,500, which bring $1,126 a day, 566 days. On seed 1, 2 of 11 tried pay, in 3,510 days or more, and 10 more cost more than its cash and credit. Seven on seed 1 and five on seed 2 need buses the full depots cannot park: a bigger bus is slower, so the same headways need more of them.
    - Bigger buses less often, the one way to free a bus place: 2 of 28 pay on seed 1 and 1 of 26 on seed 2. The best on each is a line's two Sparrows for one Metro 40 every 45 minutes: $165,000, for $178 and $100 a day (926 and 1,658 days) and one bus place. Of the rest, only one loses less than $850 a day, and added up, they take 9–11 points off the card.
    - A step more often: 10 lines on seed 1 and 11 on seed 2 have no room. Four lines on seed 1 and two on seed 2 can run a step more often on spare buses, for nothing: all four on seed 1 pay, $2,528 a day together, and one on seed 2, $1,457.
    - The trials add calls at stops only by running more often, which the full depots refuse, so none brings seed 2's Coverage (58.6) to gold's 60. Redrawing a line toward residents not yet in reach (Route), which can add Coverage on the same buses, was not tried.
  - **Where the ceiling binds.** At 30 buses a company can redraw a line elsewhere (Route), but has no plain way to move buses from one line to another. A line run less often, or redrawn shorter, parks its spare buses in their depot places, and only a model change sells a line's buses, buying what its headways need of the new model. No command closes a line or sells a spare bus. V1 could do both (`Intralines@7af45b1:src/game/store.ts:1391` and `:1461`), and its buses belonged to the company, not to a line, so a closed line's buses waited for another line to take them for nothing (`:1366-1384`). No decision dropped either.
  - **What it means for the values.** No price makes a bigger bus the late game's purchase. It can win back only the riders full buses turn away: on seed 2's most crowded line, 590 a day, at most $2,124 a day at $3.60 a paid boarding (its $2.00 fare and the $1.60 subsidy), of which the swap wins $1,126. For that swap to pay back within 100 days, a Metro 40 would have to cost no more than about $85,000, less than a Sparrow. So the prices stay, and the cash piles up: Shelters, about $900,000 paying back in about 130 days, are a company's one good purchase at the ceiling. What the ceiling takes away is the moves: under it a bus goes from one line to another only through a model change, which pays back in 926 days at best. The Open row above asks which verbs to add.
- **2026-10-21** (`npm run bench coverage`; `npm run bench pacing` on seeds 1–3, plain and with `PACING_CANDIDATES=wide PACING_CARD_CENTS=16000`; on `main` 37965a4 with the harness these runs brought back): **what gold takes on Inner Boston** (D26's values pass; the medal ladder, Open above; the M5 spec, item 1). A stop on every street would reach all of them within the 650 m walk, and the densest 26.8 km² (219 of 681 peopled cells) hold 60% of them. Lines are picked greedily by residents newly in reach per bus, within the 30 buses five depots park:
    - **T03's ten corridors alone** (33 lines priced): 14 lines on 29 buses every 15 minutes reach Coverage 58.2 by the greedy count. Founded and run a quarter, they grade 67.9 (Coverage 58.1, Money 33.5), losing $5,702 a day.
    - **With lines between the densest neighbourhoods** (310 lines, `bench/dense.ts`), every 15 minutes: 13 lines on 30 buses reach Coverage 81.6 and grade 76.7, with Money 45.2.
    - **The same pool every 20 minutes:** 13 lines on 28 buses grade **80.4** (Coverage 78.5, Reliability 100, Comfort 79.6, Money 63.4) at 8,721 trips, netting $8,152 a day. That meets every gold bar, for $5,064,000 of depots, stops and buses.
  - **The profit-only player** (the next day's net alone, on T03's corridors, as the nightly runs it), seed 1, 200 days:
    - 7 lines, 3 depots and 15 buses by day 160, at Coverage 31.0. Then no candidate scores, and its cash reaches $1,207,664 by day 200. It never gets gold.
    - Its bands hold: the founding recipe's day 2 at $1,109, the second line on day 33 at 23.4 minutes, and the first card at 64.8. Seeds 2 and 3 over 80 days match `main` exactly.
    - It is not quite the tuning freeze's player. It now buys a depot when its depots are full, parks a line at the depots there are when none can be had near it, and draws more than one line a day when the cash allows. On seed 1 the first of these brings Dorchester Avenue from its first end on day 102, where `main` drew it from its second end on day 105 and reached Coverage 31.6, 4,384 trips and $1,169,964, as the nightly measures.
  - **A player that builds toward the card:** it scores each card point at a day's grant ($160; `PACING_CARD_CENTS=16000`), with the dense candidates too, and tries each day as the profit-only player does.
    - **Seed 1:** past gold's overall on day 104 (75.4, at 71.8 minutes). **Gold on day 144, at 103.2 minutes:** 80.2 (Coverage 61.5, Reliability 100, Comfort 76.2, Money 83.3) at 19,838 trips, with 14 lines on 30 buses in 5 depots. Bronze on day 8, silver on day 29, the second line on day 33 at 23.4 minutes.
    - **Seed 3:** **gold on day 144, at 103.2 minutes:** 80.3 (Coverage 61.9) at 19,325 trips, with 14 lines on 29 buses. The second line comes on day 28 at 20.8 minutes, silver on day 28.
    - **Seed 2:** no gold. Its 13 lines fill the 30 buses by day 144 at Coverage 58.6, and nothing more fits; day 152's card is 79.7.
    - **Trying weekly** after a day that drew nothing (`PACING_RETRY_DAYS=7`), seed 1's player skips the line it drew after day 79's close and stops at Coverage 59.8 on 29 buses, with no gold to day 296 and $11.7 million unspent. So the knob is another player, not only a faster run. Tried daily, the wide run takes 80–100 s a game day past day 100, and 73–87 minutes to day 144.
  - **What it means for the ladder.** Gold's Coverage floor sits at what 30 buses buy a greedy card-building player: 58.6 to 61.9 across seeds 1–3 and both cadences. Such a player gets gold at about 103 minutes on two seeds in three, about 17 minutes before PLAN §3's 2–4 hours. The profit-only player never comes near, and a network planned for coverage clears it on 28 buses. Whether gold stays where it is, the owner decides (the medal ladder, Open above, by Sun Nov 22).

On Inner Boston's ten corridor lines (Sparrows every 10/15 minutes, ten buses each) a band now takes a median 296–345 ms and at most 825–1,034 ms (three runs), three to eight times the 101 and 132 ms measured on 2026-10-13 (below). - **2026-10-16** (`src/sim/versions.test.ts`; `npm run bench versions`, nightly): **a line's many kept versions within I14** (D26, the systems audit's sixth round; I14). No format change, nothing saved differently and no version let go sooner: the close keeps what it bills.

On c3c2d9a: a change's handling at p95 306–333 ms and 651–808 ms over the first day (max 1,023 ms), planInEffect alone p95 131–392 ms, `Fleet.setLines` p95 135–405 ms (max 496 ms on the main thread), the close 137–487 ms. A CPU profile put 63.0 s of 66.0 s of recompute in planInEffect, 57.2 s in merge(), and 46.2 s of that in handoverOf, which wrote every stint's remaining day as a string at each candidate moment.
  - **The change** (`rules/transition.ts`, which the worker and the page's map share). A plan depends only on the versions up to it and the changes between them, never on later ones, so each step of the fold is kept under its parent step's id and what it merges from, in a bounded, least-recently-used memo (4,096 steps; 256 plans, two a fold, so about 64 settling lines at once before a fold starts again from the first version, as every fold did before), and a fold merges only from the last plan kept: a change in the frame's day merges once a fold, an undo or a recompute with nothing changed not at all, and the close needs only the handovers kept. handoverOf builds each stint's signature once and compares them as counted sets, the same decision as the sorted strings; a fold computes each distinct version's day plan once. This is the first state a module of `src/sim` keeps between calls: deterministic, exact (the plans returned are shared and never changed: checked in costs.ts, position.ts, fleet.ts and world.ts), and a pure cache of what the plain fold returns; each realm (the worker, the page) has its own.
  - **After**, the same bench: a change's handling p95 33–38 ms over the first day whatever the version count (p50 21–25 ms at 101–114 versions), planInEffect alone p95 2–7 ms, `Fleet.setLines` p95 6–10 ms, the close 3–7 ms. Left: a change on a new day moves every change's second by the day, so it merges every version again, as does a close that lets only a line's first versions go, and a fresh worker or page: with 115 versions, a change's handling 104–176 ms and `Fleet.setLines` 96–226 ms on the main thread (was 328–784 ms and 166–469 ms), once a line a day. The bench fails over I14's 150 ms at the 95th percentile and now runs nightly; c3c2d9a fails it.
  - **Unchanged:** `npm run bench jumps`' positions, `npm run bench parking`, `npm run bench firstline` and the 40-day pacing run on seeds 1–3, byte-identical. The recompute bench's assignment, untouched, swung 108–300 ms on both trees under the shared load.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): a count of merges for a line changed every 10 minutes 60 times, at most 3 a change and 180 in all, fails on c3c2d9a with 3,600 (2k − 1 at change k). An equality guard over seeded chains of 150 changes of every kind (headways, the far and the first stop off and on, roadworks, model swaps, undos, a change made again with one field different, settledOnto), crossing midnight, holds every plan to a fold run from nothing, and the handover to a copy of c3c2d9a's handoverOf; dropping a field from the step's key, keying a trip suffix by the wrong index or using the last day plan for every step each fail it. The fold run from nothing is the new code's; its merges are the old code's, but for handoverOf, which the copy holds.

- **2026-10-16** (`src/sim/rules/transition.test.ts`, `src/sim/settling.test.ts`, `src/sim/economy/costs.test.ts`, `src/worker/i6.test.ts`; `npm run bench parking`, `npm run bench jumps`): **a line parks the buses a change leaves spare, so a line running overnight hands over** (D26, the systems audit's fifth round; I1, I6). No format change and no new saved field: the merged plan is still worked out from the versions kept.
  - **The bug.** After a headway cut on a line running overnight the merged plan gave each departure to the bus free longest, and a bus goes home only after idling about an hour, which buses rotating every 20 minutes never do: it kept every bus the line owned and never came to run as its day plan does. The handover then fell where both plans run out of departures, at the end of the plan's window two days on, and the vehicle layer and the costs, reading the day plan's middle day from there, put a bus driving home back at its first stop: 456 m on a fresh company's line every 10, 12 and 15 minutes cut to 15, 20 and 20 at 10:58 on day 1, and 1,360 m with Metro 40s and two changes on day 3. The spare was paid all the next day: day 2's wages $1,849.31 against $1,248.00 on days 3 and 4 on the same timetable. Versions kept across a close, laid again from an empty street, then missed the bus still out, so the Day forecast came up to $572 off the night's card.
  - **The rule** (`parkSpares`, after each of the merge's departures). Each bus of the day plan's that takes a departure after this one, out or still to pull out, needs a stand-in among the merged plan's buses: one free no later than it (its trip under way done, or its first departure for one still to pull out) and no earlier than the idle limit before its next departure, so it would not idle out the hour. They are matched latest departure first, each to the latest-free bus that can stand in; once every bus of the day plan's has one, the merged plan's others go home after the trip they are on, and otherwise none does (a bus then goes home only once it has waited out the idle limit at the first stop, as before). So the merged plan parks its spares as its day plan would, and hands over. The matching is the mirror of the textbook greedy for intervals and points, and the fifth round's verify found it always agreed with an exhaustive search on whether stand-ins exist. Across a chain of changes it can cost a departure: a bus parked for one version may be on its way home when the next version's first departures need it, and one no bus can reach then is not run (below); turning a bus round on its drive home would be a new behaviour, not kept for so few. One rule for every line, with service overnight or not, as `setHeadways.ts`' header promised ("a line with spare buses parks them and pays no driver for them"): a line that stops at night kept its spare out, and paid, until the evening's last departure, which 2026-10-13 below measured as the design (amended there; the fifth round's verify refuted the finding on that entry, and parking the spare turned out to be the fix for the jump). The lane's first rule parked the bus free longest, so another pulled out of the depot minutes later for the departure the parked one would have made (21 round trips in 13 of the bench's 2,500 longer headways; one in 2,235 random changes with the review's remedy); the matching wastes none. Weighed and not kept: refusing a handover where the plans place buses differently, which with no parking turned the 456 m jump into every bus gone at the window's end; and laying every kept version from the day before its oldest's last departure, which changed nothing once spares park and costs a fourth day of departures.
  - **The handover's second.** handoverOf's candidates at a drive out are its whole second: at the drive's own fractional second, the costs split that hour a float's width off the day plan's middle day, and neither part billed the drive (39 of 101 depot distances in `costs.test.ts`; on a random player, a $3.78 Day forecast mismatch on day 23).
  - **Numbers** (`npm run bench parking`: 10,000 seeded changes on synthetic lines, 2,500 each of longer and shorter headways and roadworks starting and ending, about half with service overnight). Handovers that fall back to the window's end: 0 (main: 10). After a longer headway, the handover comes at p50 50, p90 97, max 387 minutes with no service overnight and at p50 54, p90 85, max 337 with it (main: p50 87 to 103 and p90 750 to 964 minutes, 346 past the next midnight); the debug line's six changes, laid straight, 15 to 35 minutes. No departure is left unrun that main runs (those it leaves, between a change and its handover, no bus can reach in time). Buses sent home and then needed: 0. In 59 of the 2,500 longer headways the merged plan runs a bus fewer than its day plan for over an hour (the longest 322 minutes), where the day plan carries a bus that waits while one fewer runs every departure; every departure runs and the costs bill what runs. Chains of two or three headway changes, 1 s to 3 h apart on one day (2,500): no handover at the window's end (main: 4), the handover at p50 53, p90 144, max 645 minutes after the last change (main: 85, 807 and 2,688), and 47 departures in 46 chains left unrun that main ran, against 1 the other way (the bench copied into main's tree and run from the same directory).
  - **Sim numbers**, against main (b315d69, whose sim #133 left as it was), alternated on a loaded 4-CPU container: `npm run bench firstline` and the 40-day pacing run on seeds 1–3 byte-identical (cash on day 40 $38,940, $40,733 and $99,344; the second line on days 33, 33 and 28: the pacing run changes no headway in service). `npm run bench jumps`: every movement case 0.0 m; the 48 headway changes' p95 28–33 → 35–41 ms (the parking itself); planInEffect for Inner Boston's ten corridors settling 5.5–5.8 ms (about 5.3 before). I14: `npm run bench jumps`, six runs of each tree back to back at a load of about 1, put the ten corridors' recompute with every line settling at p50 about 100 ms on both and p95 a median 138.6 → 121 ms, and the draw day's 48 headway changes and 48 stop edits at p95 medians 27.9 → 29.4 and 50.8 → 58.5 ms; the recompute bench's assignment, which this does not touch, swung either side of 150 ms on both trees with the load. A line changed every few minutes all day keeps a hundred versions or more, and planInEffect then costs hundreds of milliseconds on either tree, a little more on this one: an I14 risk before this change, still to be measured by a committed bench (since measured and brought within I14: 2026-10-16, the sixth round, above).
  - **A save made mid-settle** on an earlier build loads into the new rule as any sim fix does: on the day it loads, the Day forecast can differ from that night's card, by up to the spare bus's wages for the day, since the hours already banked were the old rule's, and a spare may be drawn somewhere else at the load; the next day agrees. On the preview, where a line cut on an overnight service stayed settling into the next day, such saves are common: a playtest starts from a fresh game or a settled one.
  - **Verified** before merge by three adversarial checks of the plans, the money and the cost: none found a defect the change brings beyond the chains above and the saves just named.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): with the base's merge, the overnight transition case (every 10 minutes to every 15), the parked spare's day 2, three days of changes and a stop taken off and put back, and the fresh company's and the Metro 40s' handovers through the host fail; with the drive-out candidates unrounded, the costs test fails for 39 depots; with the lane's first rule, the depot round trip after a cut at 06:16:29 on a 6 km line fails. One existing test changed with the rule: a second change within an hour of the draw now also bills the parked bus's drive home in that hour.
- **2026-10-16** (`src/platform/saveStore.test.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/useSim.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/{Saves,Notices}.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`, `e2e/gpu.spec.ts`, `e2e/home.spec.ts`; `MEASURE_REACH=1` on `e2e/reach.spec.ts`): **cards true about saving, the game saved last wins across tabs, and the lost-graphics card in reach** (D26, the systems audit's fifth round; D12, DP-06). No format change: the worker gains `take-save` and `took-save`, page and worker only.
  - **The GPU cards' word on saves.** The no-GPU card and the lost-graphics card say "Your game is saved" only while the game saves itself. While it does not (the Saves chip's "Autosave off") or the browser keeps nothing at all (blocked site data, on Home before a company is founded too), they say "This game isn’t saving itself: export it before you reload." with Export this game: alone on the no-GPU card, before Reload on the lost card. Chosen over Reload asking first: Reload stays one press and the way on, and the card says what a reload loses and offers what keeps it. On 050dbdd the lost card's Reload lost the whole company with blocked site data, and everything since the store first refused with a full one (31,620 game seconds and a fare change on the audit's run). At 1152×720 the no-GPU card grows 452×86 → 452×116 px while the game isn't saving itself (its Export row); the lost card stays 452×96. Neither is chrome, so I13 is unchanged. Since amended (2026-10-19, the systems audit's seventh round): the map's start-failure cards and the replaced build's card offered Reload alone, which lost the whole company with blocked site data. While the game isn't saving itself they say so too, with Export this game before Reload; the replaced build's card then stacks its words over its buttons, at most a map card's 420 px wide (448×102 px at 1152×720; in one row it covered the Cash chip). A failed game's card still offers Reload alone, since Export went with its worker.
  - **Two tabs.** The page keeps what it last read or wrote under the live key and what it last knew the key to hold, from the window's `storage` event (`onOtherTabWrites`, in `src/platform`) or a read made before a write. The event for any key has Saves list its rows again, a slot's as the autosave's, so within Saves' 2 s listing a row names a save another tab has just kept there (the systems audit's seventh round). Not handled: a Save here, Load or Delete inside those 2 s acts on the new save, since the sim writes a slot without reading it first; it needs both tabs in Saves on one slot within two seconds. A job holding one of the game's own saves (the autosave, the quarter's start), or any job on the live key, reads the key first and is held back whole while the key holds anything else: nothing written or deleted, nothing refused, nothing owed. So the first tab to write after the other read keeps writing, and the other holds back, the saves its Load, Import, Restart this quarter or New game would make included; Save here and Export stay free, as the ways to keep its game. A removal counts as a change, so an old tab never writes its old format back into a key a newer build's tab emptied (D12).
  - **Said in the game, taken up on Home.** While left behind, the Saves chip reads "Autosave off" (a third cause, amending 2026-10-15 below; its name, tooltip and look unchanged), a notice says "Another tab changed the autosave" for 6 s once each time the tab is left behind (in the game only), Saves' line reads "Another tab changed the autosave. Export this game to keep it." in place of a full or blocked store's, the autosave's row drops "This game saves itself as it plays", and Saves lists again at each change, so the row names the browser's game. "Changed", not the lane's first "saved a newer game": a removal saves nothing, and a Load or a New game there is no further on. While Home stands the page reads the key as Home opens (a page back from the browser's cache was told nothing) and at each change, one take at a time, and the worker plays a save this build can play (`take-save`): paused, undo cleared, a new session, nothing written or set aside, the quarter's start the store's as after a reload; the page drops the replaced game's refused saves, day card, draft and refused Save here. Meanwhile Home says nothing of the change unless the take fails: said at once, Saves' line and the GPU cards' words came and went at each of the other tab's saves and the slots' rows jumped 44 px under the pointer (the review of #133). No take goes once Home is gone, nor once Home has asked for a game (a Load, an Import, a New game, Restart this quarter) until that ask is refused, so a game is never replaced in play or in the game it asked for: the lane's first cut sent one more after Continue, and on #133's first push 7 Loads from Home in 25 ended on the other tab's game with it saving every 0.67 s (both shown failing). A save this build can't play (another format, another city, a removal) leaves the game as it was, still not saving itself, and a reload then does what D12 does. A save held at the start (`unbacked`) stays held through a take, since its only copy may be the worker's, for Export.
  - **Not handled.** Chromium updates other tabs' view of `localStorage` asynchronously, so two writes landing within that lag (milliseconds) can still cross. The model picked for new lines is not reset by a take (Home has every tool down). Back to Home in a tab left behind takes the other tab's game at once, dropping the play this tab has had since, with no question: Saves' line above the button names Export, the way to keep it; asking first, or not taking a game that has moved on since it was left behind, is a later change. Since amended (2026-10-16, the sixth round, above): it asks first where there is play to lose.
  - **The lost card in reach.** High on the board the lost card sat under the cards that wait for an answer, at 60 px: on 050dbdd at 1152×720 the lender's card, which has no close, hid its words and Reload (`elementFromPoint` at 5%, 50% and 95% of their middle rows: words none of three, Reload one) and a day card its words. While a day card or the run's end is up, the GPU ladder's cards now drop to the board's foot, 80 px above the window's bottom (`.app:has(.day-card)`), which such a card leaves clear: at most 52vh below its 60 px, its foot at 458 px of 720 where the lost card's top is at 544 (592 of 768, 724 of 900). They stay on the map's layer, under the cards and panels (amending the fifth round's first lane, below). The lane's first fix lifted the lost card over the cards (101), where it hid the shortest day card's Continue (a company with nothing built: Continue at 154–181 px under the card's 72–168, at all three sizes) and Finance's tabs (3 of 4 at 1152×720, 2 of 4 at 1366×768 and 1440×900). Now, at all three sizes, the card's words and Reload and that Continue are on top at both ends and the middle, no panel button and no speed option is hidden, and the dock is 22 px below the card at 720 (`e2e/reach.spec.ts`; the lender's Restart this quarter in `e2e/gpu.spec.ts`). Since amended (2026-10-19, the seventh round): the map's start-failure cards, which stay up while the game plays on without a map, drop too. The day card and then the lender's card hid their words and Reload as they had the lost card's (`e2e/errors.spec.ts`). Since amended (2026-10-21, #143): the lender's card is on the panels' layer, in a free place from 56 px down and no taller than its place allows, and the map's cards still drop for it.
  - **I13** at 1152×720 on a fresh company (the lane's probe, on its first words): at rest 4.32% saving itself, 4.43% left behind ("Autosave off", 33 px wider); Saves 18.79% → 20.60% left behind (+44 px for the line). Its words now, measured in Saves' place for that line, take the same 292×38 px, two rows, as the first words and a blocked store's line, whose place they take, so Saves is never taller than with that line (19.13% with a blocked store's shorter autosave row).

- **2026-10-16** (`src/ui/format.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Contracts.test.ts`, `src/sim/demand/assign.test.ts`): **a HEADWAY figure that agrees with its verdict, and twin lines that split their changers** (D26, the systems audit's fifth round). No format change.
  - **The figure.** A HEADWAY contract's figure rounds toward its verdict (`contractMet`, the one predicate the card's colour, the day's grading and the map read): short of the ask, up to the next whole second past the float noise `contractMet` allows (`HEADWAY_HAIR_S`, 1e-6 s); met, to the nearest second. Within a minute of the ask, or where one decimal would read as a whole minute, it prints minutes and seconds ("every 10 min 1 s"); otherwise as before ("every 8.6 min"). Asks are whole seconds (`config/contracts.ts`). On f86993b two Copley lines in snow combined to 600.2499 s and printed "every 10 min" in red beside the ask "Every 10 min", and a line a second short of 20 minutes printed "every 20.0 min".
  - **Twin lines.** An exact tie (`===`) between ways off one first line (a change onto either of twin lines, or a change and a one-seat ride) splits that line's riders evenly among them, in the fast loop and the reference alike; the second leg still costs the second line's own wait (2026-10-13, "one change of line"), so twins carry no more changers than one line would. On the fixture, with Copley–Longwood–Kenmore and Kenmore–Symphony twice, the twins read 67.30 and 71.86 riders a day (0 and 4.56 changes onto them) by the order they were drawn, and the reference gave the changers to the other twin; now 69.58 and 69.58 (2.28 each) in either order, the reference 69.99 each. The express line, trips and paid riders are unchanged, and Inner Boston's assignment is identical to the rider (it has no exact tie). Not handled: ways that differ by float noise alone (by reading, two such lines drawn in opposite directions may) still go to the quicker by an ulp, which is no choice by draw order (EC-02).

I14: on a loaded 4-CPU container the recompute bench's p95 swung either side of 150 ms before and after (Inner Boston's assignment 137.7–227.8 ms before, 164.4–285.0 after); an alternating A/B in one process, 80 runs each, gave p50 102.0 → 101.2 and 95.9 → 100.9 ms, p95 154.7 → 156.5 and 153.9 → 166.7 ms. The nightly, on GitHub's runner, has the say.
- **2026-10-16** (`src/ui/{focusSource,onePress}.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Panels.test.ts`, `e2e/{menus,panels,presses,saves}.spec.ts`): **keys and focus** (D26, the systems audit's fourth round). No format change.
  - **A held key acts once on every control.** A repeat of Enter or Space on a button, a link, a dropdown's option or a menu's item, or on a dropdown's open list, is stopped at the window in the capture phase with its default (`onePress`), as a dropped click is. Other keys' repeats go on (an arrow moves, a text field types); the page's own Space and 1 to 5 drop their repeats themselves (App.tsx). The demand menu's and the route editor's own guards went. On abb5f2f a held Enter pressed a focused button at each repeat: two stop upgrades (a Shelter, then a Station), three undos, ten exports; Play flipped time, and the demand menu turned Homes on and off at each repeat of Enter.
  - **Escape and a menu's button.** Escape and a modifier alone move no focus of the player's: the focus handed back on one comes with the source the element had when the focus last left it (`focusSource`), and a press records the control it lands on as the pointer's, for the browsers that focus no button a press lands on (Safari, and Firefox on macOS; by reading: the e2e tests run in Chromium alone). So a menu the mouse opened hands its button back to the mouse as Escape closes it, whatever arrows, Home, End or Tab moved through it, and Space plays and pauses; one reached with Tab or opened from the keyboard hands it back to the keyboard, and Space opens it again. An element never focused before takes the last key's or press's source. On abb5f2f Space after Escape reopened a menu the mouse had opened.
  - **Saves' questions.** A question opened from the keyboard (a click with `detail` 0: Delete, Save here over a save, Load, Import a save file) takes the focus to its keeping answer, Keep it or Keep playing, never the one that deletes or replaces; answered from the keyboard, the focus goes back to the button that opened it, or to the slot's row once Delete emptied it, or to the panel once a set-aside save's row went with it. A mouse press moves no focus. On abb5f2f the focus fell to the page, so the next Space ran time behind the question. Not handled: Load or Import answered from Home's Saves, where the loaded game's Saves takes the place of Home's and the focus falls to the page, so Space plays the loaded game, as it does on the page; and a row or the panel holding the focus leaves Space to the page too.
  - **Panels past the edge.** The panel layer clips what hangs past the window (`overflow: clip`), so the page never scrolls. A control the keyboard focuses (`:focus-visible`) past either edge brings its panel across far enough to show it 12 px inside the window, and the panel stays there, its dragged place with it (`showing`). The pointer's focus moves no panel, nor does a dropdown's open list, portaled to the page with its place fixed as it opened. On abb5f2f a Tab into a panel dragged past the right edge scrolled the whole game 272 px left, and panels then opened and dragged 272 px from where they should.
  - **Create's flag.** A load from a slot, a file or Restart this quarter clears App's Create-in-flight flag (`creating`), as the created line does; nothing else clears it. A new game needs no clear: it has no line, and its load carries no date. On abb5f2f a Create refused, or answered together with its undo, left the flag set, and a game loaded later opened Lines on its newest line.
  - **The PR checks.** Five e2e tests more (104 on the fixture) move Playwright's shard boundaries, insert's and line's tests to the first third: about 62–66 s more summed, timed on a loaded 4-CPU container, where CI runs 2–3 times faster.

The map's cards take the game's font: MapLibre's container sets its own on what it holds. Home then shows New game and Saves, with no Continue. Chosen over the Saves chip on the card, which would bring "Autosave off" onto it and break the held save's rule that its notice waits on the founding card, "where no button opens Saves". Escape on the card still does nothing. I13: the founding card at 1152×720, 13.4% → 14.9% (340×271 → 340×309 px).

On abb5f2f it kept the name and colour typed for the company it replaced, and one press founded the loaded game as "Back Bay Transit" in Blue.
- **2026-10-16** (`e2e/i13.spec.ts`, `e2e/time.spec.ts`, `e2e/errors.spec.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Contracts.test.ts`): **the chip row late in a game, held speed keys, and a stopped game's map** (D26, the systems audit's fourth round). No format change.
  - **The row's last chip.** The checklist's step, or the Contracts chip in its place, gives up room as the Undo chip does (at least 6em, at most its whole label), its medals kept whole and its whole words in its title and accessible name. Its grow, a thousand times the Undo's, takes the free room first, so the Undo is down to its 6em (84 px) before the last chip is cut at all, and the row wraps only when 6em of each does not fit. I13 at 1152×720 in the countdown late in a game (Year 10, paused at 5,400×, a fare's undo, three medals and "3 on offer", or "4/5 Close a day in profit"), abb5f2f → after: with "Autosave off", 10.08% (two rows, 1003×62 px) → 5.97% (one row; with Inner Boston's numbers the last chip is cut to 117 px, "Contracts…" and its three medals); without it, with Inner Boston's numbers, 10.22% (1036×62) → 5.97%; with the fixture's numbers and no "Autosave off" the Contracts chip stays whole (159 px) beside a 110 px Undo. Every other I13 number is unchanged; "Contracts 1 held" is now one span, 0.6 px narrower, so the earlier countdown cases' Undo is 1 px wider at the same 5.97%. The three new cases add about 40 s to the PR checks.
  - **A held speed key.** A held 1 to 5 picks its speed once: its repeats send nothing, as Space's do. A speed command for the speed already running changes nothing in the worker: no clock reset, no autosave, no snapshot and no entry in the Export session's commands, the speed menu picking the running speed included; a pause sent while paused still saves, as Home's does. On abb5f2f, holding 5 with a repeat every 33 ms for 3 s ran 0 game seconds against 16,200, wrote 92 autosaves against 6 and recorded 91 session commands against 1. Older session files replay to the same hash (`replay.ts`), since a dropped re-pick never changed state.
  - **A stopped game's map.** Once the worker fails, the map's clock stops (`rate` 0), so the vehicle layer asks for no frame after one repaint, as while paused (render only on change, PLAN §6). On abb5f2f it asked for a frame after every frame until a reload (8 → 14 in 2 s, `e2e/errors.spec.ts`). In that repaint the buses go back to the last snapshot's clock, by at most the layer's 250 ms run-on: 15 game seconds at 60×, 22 game minutes at 5,400×.
- **2026-10-16** (`src/sim/world.test.ts`, `src/sim/commands/setRoute.test.ts`, `src/sim/economy/reportCard.test.ts`; `npm run bench recompute`, nightly): **I14 with depots, Edit route's depot room, and the report card's last day** (D26, the systems audit's fourth round). No format change: the drives' cache is derived and never saved, and the close's rows merge by kind as before.
  - **I14 with depots.** Every recompute routed each line's drives to and from every depot again, though a drive changes only with the line's first stop or its depot: with ten lines and five depots on Inner Boston, 166 router searches a recompute against 106 with none, `lineServices` 118 ms against 6, and a building command 230–350 ms at p50 (set a stop's tier 238 ms, take a stop off a line 357 ms, an undo 258 ms; the audit's harness). The world now keeps each drive by its depot's node and first stop's node (`depotDrive`, a cache on the world's router, each recompute keeping what it used), and the worker's line views read the same drives. The router depends only on the graph, so no number moves (the pacing runs are identical byte for byte). `bench/recompute.ts` adds the whole recompute with ten lines of three Sparrows and five depots: p50 196.0 / p95 226.1 ms → p50 90–100 / p95 135–154 ms over three runs on a shared container, where the plain assignment's p95 swung 121–405 ms meanwhile; the nightly times it on GitHub's runner. A command that moves a first stop or adds a depot still routes its new drives once.
  - **Edit route's depot room.** With every depot full, a route needing two more buses said "2 more Sparrows" and "Depots have room for 2 more": the line rule ran with the line's own buses taken off the company's, so the room counted them as free. Route, extend and insert verdicts now name the depots' real room (`editRefusal`, one predicate for the card and the command): "Your depots are full", as the headway card says; with room for 2, room 2 where it read 4. Whether a change is refused is unchanged. Not changed: the model swap's card names its room with the line's own buses in it, since a swap sells them.
  - **The report card's last day.** The close graded the quarter before posting the day's interest, so each quarter's last day of interest was graded in no quarter (the fixture's Money 73.5016 where the quarter's rows give 73.3700). The close now posts the day's contract rewards, then its interest, then grades the card and posts its grant or fine, then awards medals; interest is charged on what is owed before the grant or fine. The pacing runs (40 days, seeds 1–3) are unchanged, as no quarter owes there; run owing from the start (`PACING_RESERVE_CENTS=-15000000`), each card's Money grade falls by 0.03–0.09 and its payout by $0.01–$29.78 (seed 1, day 8: 69.8080 → 69.7534, $13,171.05 → $13,153.59), seed 3's day-24 card prints 67.8 where it printed 67.9, cash on day 40 moves by $62–$85, and every milestone day and minute is the same.
- **2026-10-16** (`src/sim/settling.test.ts`; `npm run bench jumps`, `npm run bench pacing`, `npm run bench firstline`): **a second change in a day, after the first has handed over** (D26, the systems audit's fourth round; the M4 spec, item 4; I1, I6). No format change: `EarlierRun` is unchanged, only how long the versions are kept.
  - **The bug.** On any day but a line's draw day, a change made after the line had handed over to its own day plan let go of its kept versions, so a second change that day dropped the version that ran before the first: the merged plan then held the first change's version as if it had run since midnight. Finance's Day forecast (the Breakdown's and Lines' running, wages and deadhead, and Costs/day) re-costed the hours before the first change at its timetable, and when both changes fell in one clock hour that hour banked off the rebuilt plan. On the fixture, the debug line every 20 minutes from 05:00 on day 1, every 10 from 08:00 on day 2 (handing over at 08:20) and every 15 from 08:40: forecast $485.97 running, $1,456.63 wages and $20.64 to and from the depot, against $433.59, $1,379.14 and $13.76 banked; hour 8 banked $32.01, $78.00 and $0 (a leg no bus ran, and no drive out for the bus that pulled out at 08:xx). In the audit's seeded runs of changes over three days, a day missed by as much as $2,161 of running forecast against $750 banked.
  - **The rule.** A line changed in service keeps every version it ran on the day of the change until that day's close; it lets them go at a change only if it had settled onto its own day plan by that day's midnight. At the close (`settle`), a line past its handover lets every version go, as before; one still settling (a change late in the evening hands over the next morning, about 04:56 on the fixture after 23:30) keeps only the versions from the last it had settled onto before it changed again (`settledOnto`, one pass of the merges `planInEffect` makes), so its plan from midnight is the same, minute by minute and hour by hour, and a run of late changes carries one or two versions past midnight, not a day's. The draw day is this rule's first case; `drawnAt` decides only that a line's fares count from the hour it was drawn.
  - **After.** The same case forecasts $430.68, $1,370.47 and $17.20 and day 2 banks exactly that; hour 8 banks $29.10, $69.33 and $3.44 (on 43554a9 the day banked $2.91 and $8.67 more and $3.44 less than its buses did). The one-change control is unchanged ($608.19, $1,381.30, $17.20). Three seeded runs of changes over two days, some after a handover and some moving the first stop, meet each day's card to the cent and bank each hour gone as forecast. A line changed every 50 minutes from 06:00 to 23:30 for five days carries 2 versions past each close (22 without the trim; on 43554a9, 1 or 2), its save 4.8 KB.
  - **Sim numbers**, against 43554a9: `npm run bench firstline` and the 40-day pacing run on seeds 1–3 are unchanged (cash on day 40 $38,940, $40,733 and $99,344; the second line on days 33, 33 and 28). I14: Massachusetts Avenue on Inner Boston changed 48 times through an ordinary day, the last at 23:50, keeps 48 versions at a p95 of 46.8 ms a change; the close's `settledOnto` over them takes 6.7 ms and the whole close 54.1 ms.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/platform/saveStore.test.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/useSim.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/{Saves,Notices}.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`, `e2e/saves.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **a full store said as full, Save here on its own row, and "Autosave off"** (D26, the systems audit's third round; D12, DP-06). No format change: the worker's `store` message gains `slot` and says which save `owed` marks (`autosave` or `quarter`), page and worker only.
  - **Full or blocked.** `src/platform` tells the two apart by the error's name alone: a QuotaExceededError is `full`, anything else (a SecurityError from blocked site data) `blocked`. Measured in Chromium only (`e2e/saves.spec.ts`, the store filled by other keys of the origin). A full store's notice reads "No room for saves: delete a save", naming no control, since the Saves chip may read "Autosave off" by then, and Saves' line "No room for saves. Delete a save, or export this game to keep it.": Export too, since other data of the same origin can take the room with nothing in Saves to delete, and "for saves", not "this game", since the quarter's start may be refused while the autosave is kept. Blocked site data keeps its words. With both pending, Saves' line says blocked; the notice keeps the words of the page's first refusal, once a page as before.
  - **Save here.** A refused Save here owes the sim nothing and is said on its slot's row alone, "Didn’t fit: delete a save to make room" or "Not kept: this browser isn’t keeping saves", until that slot is saved, any delete in Saves is kept (it made room, so the advice is followed; a press refused again says so again) or another game loads (New game, Load, Import, Restart this quarter). On 029387a it raised the once-a-page notice and held Saves' line only until a write was kept, so at 5,400× the next autosave (every 0.67 s) took it down and a second press left the slot Empty with nothing said. A refused delete still raises the notice and holds Saves' line until a write is kept.
  - **"Autosave off".** While the game is not saving itself, the Saves chip reads "Autosave off", in the countdown's red at the chip row's 14 px (with Saves open, in the pressed chip's colours): while a save the start could not set aside is held, or while the browser has refused the autosave and kept none since (a full store until a delete makes room; blocked site data for the page's life). It keeps those words while Saves is open, so its accessible name ("Saves: autosave off", which keeps "Saves") does not change and it does not move under the pointer as Saves opens; its tooltip reads "This game isn’t saving itself: open Saves to see why". On Home the held save's notice "Not autosaving: see Saves" stays until Saves opens, as before; in the game the chip says it for as long as it lasts, where the notice went for good once Saves had opened. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fifth audit round, above): also while another tab has changed the autosave since this tab last read or wrote it.
  - **I13 at 1152×720.** The chip is 105 px against "Saves"' 72, and the top bar's right edge moves from 104 px to 137 px, so a full row's Undo gives up the 33 px (165 → 132 px with the fixture's numbers, 131 → 98 px with Inner Boston's). In the countdown with the save held: 5.98% with both sets of numbers, where the notice beside the full row would have taken 6.61% (5.98% plus its 169×31 px, 0.63%, added from two measurements: 029387a cannot reach the countdown without opening Saves, which took the notice down). With the save held: at rest 4.95% → 4.4%, with a line 5.81% → 5.3%, the stop taken off ("Undo: Take Stop 2 off Line 1") 5.96% → 5.4%, and so with Inner Boston's numbers 6.07% → 5.5%; Saves 24.5% → 24.6% (the chip's longer words), with Load's or Import's question 24.6%; Home 8.7%, unchanged. Every other I13 number is unchanged (at 1152×720: rest 4.3%, with a line 5.2%, Saves 24.3%, Finance with purchases 24.6%; the busy day card 23.5% and, at 1366×768, 19.5%).
  - **Its limit.** In the tightest case measured (the countdown with Inner Boston's numbers) the Undo is 98 px, 14 px above its 6em floor (84 px), so a row wider still (more contracts on offer, a second medal) would wrap with "Autosave off" where it would not with "Saves". Not measured; I13's countdown test fails if the row wraps. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): measured late in a game, it wrapped (10.08%), and the row's last chip now gives up room once the Undo is at its 6em.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/sim/rules/transition.test.ts`, `src/sim/settling.test.ts`, `src/sim/economy/finance.test.ts`, `src/worker/{host,i6}.test.ts`, `e2e/line.spec.ts`; `npm run bench jumps`, now nightly): **buses after a line changes, and a line's first day** (D26, the systems audit's third round; the M4 spec, item 4; I6). No format change: a version that ran no bus is an ordinary kept version, its plan's buses 0.
  - **A second change.** Each kept version carries whether every change since kept its model (`sameModel`) and its first stop too (`carriesOn`). A bus finishing a trip of an older version goes home if any change since moved the first stop or swapped the model, and a sold bus is never counted as the line's. On 5f4bce3 a second change within a round trip of one that moved the first stop or swapped the model brought the old buses back: jumps of 1.0–2.8 km in the audit, 2,524 m and 2,137 m in `i6.test.ts`'s seeded changes in a row, and a sold Sparrow running Metro 40 trips. Now no bus moves over 5 m.
  - **Service with no bus out.** Every change in service is merged, with buses out or not, and a line just drawn changes from a version that ran no bus, so its buses pull out of the depot for the departures they can reach, as each morning's do, and their drives bill under To and from the depot. Before, a line drawn at 10:20 had three buses on its streets at once and no drive out billed; overnight service turned on with every bus home did the same. The close now retimes for an event before it lets settled lines go (`step.ts`), so roadworks or snow at midnight after a change that day leave every bus where it was.
  - **The draw day.** A line keeps the version before its draw, and every version since, until that day's close (`drawnAt`, the one predicate for its costs and Finance's forecast), and past a handover the merged plan carries on from what ran instead of starting again from the latest version's day plan. So a later change that day still finds nothing run before the draw. Finance's Day forecast counts the line's fares and subsidy from the clock hour it was drawn (the day banks riders by the whole hour) and its Bus fuel and maintenance, Driver wages and To and from the depot from its first drive out, as the day banks them; Costs/day reads the same column. Depot and stop upkeep in the column stay a whole day's. On the fixture (against the lane's own first fix, which let a drawn line's versions go once it had handed over), the debug line drawn at 15:20 and changed at 18:00: $3,110.80 of fares and subsidy and $2,073.82 of costs forecast (a whole day) → $1,374.45 and $877.99, the costs exactly what day 1 banks. Drawn at 15:01 and changed at 15:31 after a handover at 15:30, hour 15 billed $32.01 running, $78.00 wages and $0 of depot drives (the 15:00 departure no bus ran) → $23.28, $52.00 and $10.32. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): every day, not the draw day alone, a line keeps every version it ran until that day's close, and `drawnAt` decides the fares only.
  - **A new game's first line**, drawn at 05:00, skips the departures no bus can reach from the depot in time, its 05:00 departure among them, and day 1's line costs meet day 1's forecast to the cent (`host.test.ts`). Day 1's depot and stop upkeep are still 19 hours of 24 against a whole day's forecast.
  - **Sim numbers**, against cf80298 (e3f9bb9 changes no sim file; 029387a's fold leaves every figure the same to the cent). `npm run bench firstline` is unchanged: Washington Street, $2,523 on day 2, 18 of 18 fit, the four-bus line 1.1506×. The pacing run over 40 days, seeds 1–3, holds every band and PLAN §3 target (founding day 2 $1,109; the second line on days 33, 33 and 28; the first card at 64.8, 65.8 and 64.8 minutes). What moves: the second line's gain on its draw day, $995 → $1,002, $995 → $1,002 and $906 → $912, and cash on day 40, $38,896 → $38,940, $40,691 → $40,733 and $99,300 → $99,344, all from the draw-day billing.
  - **Cost of it (I14).** A line changed every 20 minutes through its draw day keeps every version, and the worker routed each kept version's streets for the vehicle layer afresh at every refresh. On Inner Boston, with Massachusetts Avenue drawn and then its headways changed 48 times, a change took 27 ms at the first and 262 ms at the 48th (p95 284 ms); with a stop added before its first and taken off again in turn, 38 ms and 306 ms (p95 290 ms). The worker now keeps each version's routed streets, by its stops and depot, from one refresh to the next while a line still keeps a version on them: p95 31 ms and 57 ms, the slowest of the 48 changes 34 ms and 65 ms. Carried only within one refresh, the route edits' p95 was 166 ms. Measured with `npm run bench jumps` as committed, with the lane's diff without the cache, and with the cache, alone on the machine. `bench/jumps.ts` fails when either p95 is over 150 ms, when a change is refused or when the versions are not kept, and now runs nightly. Not in the bench: the page prepares every kept version again on each change of the lines (`Fleet.setLines`), 2.7 ms at 2 versions and 7.5 ms at 49 in vitest; each frame's cost stays flat (since measured, by `bench/versions.ts`, and kept from one change to the next: 2026-10-16, the sixth round, above). Since the fourth round (2026-10-16, above) a line changed on any day keeps its versions so: the same bench holds for it.
  - **An older build.** By reading: a save written on a line's draw day after it has handed over holds versions an older build's merge drops, so an older tab that loads it reads that line's running costs as NaN until the close. Web saves are not kept across builds as a promise (D12), so no format bump.

A save from an older build folds at its next close.

I13 at 1152×720: in the countdown with a long undo and the Contracts chip, 10.2% (two rows) → 5.98% (one full row; also with Inner Boston-sized cash, riders and costs set in the chips' text); with the held save's notice and that undo, 6.05% → 6.0% (at the 6% budget: a full chip row with the notice leaves no room, so the held notice in the countdown is the next lane's). At 1366×768 and 1440×900 the undo shows whole (4.8%, 3.9%). Since amended (2026-10-15, above): in the game the Saves chip says "Autosave off" in place of that notice, 5.98% in the countdown with the save held.
