Labels are kept by rank (today, Day 0, each quarter's first day, the chart's first day) and one within 6 chart units of a label ranked before it is left out, judged by a hand-measured 4.4 units a character at the axis's 9 px (`FinanceChart.tsx`). Since amended (2026-10-22, #150): 12.5 units and 6.1 a character, about 12.9 px drawn, PLAN §8's floor for chrome text, so a crowded chart names fewer days.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/useSim.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Notices.test.ts`, `e2e/errors.spec.ts`): **saves the game owes, and a stopped game that shows nothing of itself** (D26, the systems audit's third round; D12). No format change.
  - **Owed by key.** The sim marks its own two writes (`owed`): the autosave and the quarter's start. When the browser refuses one, the page keeps its key and tells the sim (`store-refused`), and the sim owes that save; after any delete in Saves (a slot, a set-aside save, the save the start could not set aside) it writes what it owes at once, the autosave first, then the quarter's start from the copy it holds in memory, before the slots are listed again. A refused owed write is owed again; nothing retries on its own. While the start's hold lasts, the autosave stays owed and nothing is written over the held save.
  - **Saves' line.** "This browser isn’t keeping saves" holds while a refused key of the game's own is not kept since (a kept write of that key, or a delete of it, ends it), so a kept autosave no longer hides a quarter's start the browser refused, and a delete's job (removes only) never takes it down. Any other refusal (a Save here, a delete, a read) holds it only until a write is kept, as before (since amended, 2026-10-15, above: a full store's line names no room, Delete and Export, and a Save here refused is said on its slot's row alone). The refused keys live in the page alone: a reload forgets them while the save is still not kept. The notice still says it once a page. Before, any kept job took the line down, a delete's too, though nothing had been saved, and a reload then lost the play since (the audit's case: a depot and its $150,000).
  - **A stopped game.** Once the worker fails, the game renders no panel (Finance, Lines, Contracts, Saves, Saves opened from Home included, a stop's card, the new-line card, the route editor), no card beside the pointer, no day card, no lender's card, no depot tint and no line being drawn: only the card with Reload. The map, its credit and the compass stay. Before, they stayed on screen with buttons that sent to a worker that was gone.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/ui/focusSource.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/{RouteEditor,Tools,useSim}.test.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts`, `e2e/route.spec.ts`, `e2e/time.spec.ts`, `e2e/undo.spec.ts`): **keys that act once, and undos answered** (D26, the systems audit's third round). No format change.
  - **A mouse press takes Space back.** A pointer press clears where the focus came from (`focusSource`), so Space after a click on a button that Tab had once reached pauses and plays instead of pressing the button again (a second purchase undone from the Undo chip, Finance closed). Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): Escape moves no focus of the player's either, so a menu's button gets back the source it had when the menu opened.
  - **A held key acts once.** Space's repeats send nothing (no speed command, no autosave), as the demand menu and the tilt keys already did; the route editor's arrows drop a held Enter's repeats. Holding 1 to 5 still re-picks the same speed, which changes nothing on screen. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): each re-pick stalled the clock and saved, so a held 1 to 5 now picks its speed once, and the worker ignores a pick of the speed already running. And since (2026-10-16, above): a held Enter or Space presses every control and dropdown list once (`onePress`), and the demand menu's and the route editor's own guards went.
  - **The route editor keeps its rows.** Each row is keyed by its stop (or a new stop's click) and how many identical rows come before it, not by its place, so a stop moved from the keyboard keeps its arrow focused, or its other arrow at the list's end, and a second Space or Enter moves it again. × still drops the focus with its row.
  - **Undos answered.** The worker answers every undo once: `undone` when it took a command back, `refused` otherwise (`not_started` included). The page counts the undos it sent and the newest the sim did (`Sim.undos`, `{sent, done}`); the answer is page-and-worker only, in neither the save nor the session file. Save route's guard and Create's (`createWaits`) let go only on an undo sent after the press that the sim did; one only sent, or refused while time runs ("Time has passed since then"), lets nothing go. So Save route and an Undo answered in one render leave the editor open on the player's edit with Save route enabled; answered apart, the editor closes when the save lands, as before.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/useSim.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/{Saves,Notices}.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`, `e2e/saves.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **saves that keep what they cannot set aside** (D26, the systems audit's second round; D12 and DP-06). No format change.
  - **The hold.** The start's set-aside is read back once. If the backup did not land (no room), the live key keeps the old save's only copy and the game writes nothing there: its autosaves are owed, the quarter's save and the slots are kept as usual. Only Delete in Saves (after a question) or a later page load ends the hold; nothing retries the backup. The notice "Not autosaving: see Saves" is untimed, shown only where a button opens Saves, and gone once Saves opens or the hold ends (since amended, 2026-10-15, the third audit round, above: said on Home alone, the Saves chip saying "Autosave off" in the game while the hold lasts); the autosave's row names the old save, "not set aside · this game isn’t saving itself", with Export (`interbus-<city>-not-set-aside-<why>.json`) and Delete. One failure, one notice: the set-aside's refusal never says "This browser isn’t keeping saves". Before, founding wrote over the only copy.
  - **Restart this quarter** reopens the quarter's save as this page last wrote it, kept in memory, so it works for the page's life where the browser keeps no site data; after a reload it is read from the store, as before.
  - **Questions.** Once a company is founded, Load and Import a save file ask "Replace this game / Keep playing", in the game and on Home; before, there is no game to lose and both go straight ahead. One question is open at a time, and while a row's question is open, its other buttons hide (Export this game too, beside Import's question).
  - **A slot from another bake** is loadable only if its stops find this bake's streets, the check Load makes (`worldFrom`); otherwise it is listed as "A save with stops off the new map", with Export and Delete and no Load. Each answer is cached by the save's text; the first listing after a rebake builds up to three worlds once.
  - **I13** (1152×720, the tight size): Saves at its tallest 24.3% (with a slot's Load question or Import's open, too; on the round before, a slot's Load question with Save here beside it wrapped to 25.5%, and with the held row 26.0%, over the 26% budget). With the old save held: at rest 5.0%, at rest with a line 5.8% (a longer notice measured 6.05%, over 6%), Home 8.7%, Saves 24.7%.
- **2026-10-15** (`e2e/menus.spec.ts`, `src/ui/pressAway.test.ts`, `e2e/presses.spec.ts`): **menus in front, and keys through them** (D26, the systems audit's second round).
  - **Layers.** One set of tokens in `app.css`: the panels (10), the menus and the cards beside the pointer (20), the cards that wait for an answer (the founding card, the day's close and the run's end, 100) and the replaced build's card (150). The panels draw inside one layer of their own, so raising one never lifts it over a menu, however many are raised. The dock is in the menus' layer (its transform holds the Build menu), so a dragged panel stops with its heading above it (`inReach`: the window's height less the dock's 72 px and 48 px of panel) (since the third audit round, below: 160 px of panel, so it ends above the dock); the map's credit and the build chip sit just above the menus. A dropdown's list, at 200 before, shares the menus' layer: over every panel, under the day card. Since amended (2026-10-21, the ninth audit round's fixes, #143): the run's end has its card on the panels' layer, in a free place beside them, and no panel opened after it covers it, as it stays until Restart this quarter; at the top centre among the cards it covered a panel opened third.
  - **A press away.** A press that closes a menu does nothing else only when it lands on the map's canvas (`onCanvas`, shared with `onePress`); a press on a control in the map, the compass or the lost-graphics card's Reload, closes the menu and works the control, as a press on any other control does.
  - **Keys.** Tab and Shift+Tab with a dropdown's list open close it and go on from its button, as the browser's own select; the Build menu comes after its button in the tab order, closes when the focus leaves it, and Escape on a tool gives the focus back to Build.
  - **The held double-click** on the map tilts only when the map heard the click before it (`heardLast`), so a double-click whose first click was a Home button's no longer flips the board.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/ui/onePress.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/Tools.test.ts`, `e2e/presses.spec.ts`): **one press, one command** (D26, the systems audit's second round).
  - **The rule.** One window listener in the capture phase (`onePress`, installed once by App, so Home's buttons too): a person's click with `detail` 1 on a button, a link, an option or a menu item, anywhere but the map's canvas (the compass and the lost-graphics card's Reload count), arms it; every later click of that run (`detail` above 1) and its `dblclick` is dropped wherever it lands, before React or MapLibre hears it; the next click with `detail` 0 or 1 ends it. Clicks the page makes itself (Export's download, Import's file picker) are never dropped and never end it. No control asks for a double-click; one that starts on the map or on plain text is the map's, as before. Since amended (the third audit round, below): a run that starts on the map keeps its later clicks only while they land on the map; once one lands off it (on a card that opened under the pointer), the rest of the run is dropped.
  - **A held key** is one press too, since the fourth audit round (2026-10-16, above): a repeat of Enter or Space on a control or a dropdown's list is dropped by the same listener.
  - **What it costs.** The browser counts clicks at one spot within its double-click interval as one run, so quick repeats on one button count once: the Undo chip takes back one thing a run (Ctrl+Z still repeats), and the route editor's × drops one stop a run. Chosen over per-button guards, which round one tried and which missed Close, the Undo chip and Export.
  - **Create** also waits for its own answer, as Save route does: shut from the press until a line newer than the press stands, a Create is refused, an undo is sent, the tool is put down or another game loads (`createWaits`), so a second press before a slow city's answer buys nothing; a refresh of the lines that is not its answer (a day's close, roadworks) leaves it shut. Since amended (2026-10-15, the third audit round, below): the undo must be one sent after the press that the sim did (`undone`); one only sent, or refused while time runs, leaves Create shut.
- **2026-10-14** (`src/render/map/{tilt,loadMap,MapView}.test.ts`, `e2e/tilt.spec.ts`, `e2e/gpu.spec.ts`): **a map that works through cut-short moves, loads and lost graphics** (D26, the systems audit's map lane; no renderer change).
  - **A mode change cut short.** Once the camera rests after any move, a 3D board below 25° eases to the pitch its change was aiming for, and a 2D board not flat or not facing north eases flat and north (`resettle`, tilt.ts), checked one frame after the move ends, so a move that cut another short counts as moving. A 3D board stopped between 25° and 60° stays where it is.
  - **Loaded games.** The depots' layer is keyed by each depot's id and place, and the city event's by its kind, days and place (its label and its stretch's ends), since ids restart and every game starts from one seed; the map's `data-depots` says where the depots were last drawn.
  - **Lost graphics.** From MapLibre's `webglcontextlost` until the style it rebuilds has loaded, the game's layers wait as before the first load, so T, Homes and Jobs, a tool or Place a depot reach no missing style. MapLibre 6.11.2 (`src/ui/map.ts`, `_contextRestored`) sets the kept style before it fires `webglcontextrestored`, and that style has no custom layer, so once it loads the buses' layer goes back on and the buildings are set to the mode at once. A style lost again before it loaded is not kept: the board stays blank, and the card with Reload stays.
- **2026-10-14** (`src/ui/shell/Panels.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/besidePointer.test.ts`, `e2e/panels.spec.ts`, `e2e/dropdown.spec.ts`, `e2e/sim.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **panels, dropdowns and the cards beside the pointer that work** (D26, the systems audit's panels lane; amends D25 item 6).
  - **Panels.** A panel in a free place takes a row only with room for 160 px of it above the dock (72 px from the window's bottom), and a CSS variable, `--room`, caps each panel at the room below its top, so what does not fit, or grows later (Saves before its slots arrive, the inspector switched to a longer line), scrolls inside it; a panel dragged low shrinks to that room, down to 160 px. With no row free, a panel opens at the top right as far down as a step while 160 px stay above the dock. A panel never dragged stays whole when the window narrows; one dragged keeps its heading's first 48 px in reach. Since amended (the third audit round, below): every panel's top stays 160 px above the dock, dragged or placed, and 48 px of its width on screen sideways. A panel opened, or shown again while open (the Contracts chip, Take a contract, the line card's Finance link, a line or stop clicked), comes to the front. Left as it is: panels clamped one by one on a narrower window can come to overlap.
  - **Dropdowns.** The list is measured unseen, then opens below its button, or above where there is more room above, its height capped to the room on its side; it opens at the current choice, and the arrow keys, Home and End keep the highlighted choice in view. It is not limited sideways: a list wider than its button near the right edge can still run past it.
  - **Cards beside the pointer.** The dot card and the cards of Place a stop and Place a depot share one placement (`besidePointer`), flipping to the pointer's other side at the window's edge. A camera move under a still pointer reports the point now under it, so a dot's card goes once its dot moves away and the site cards judge the place a click would use (GD-05).
  - **I13** is unchanged: at 1152×720 at rest 4.3%, Saves at its tallest 24.3% and Finance with a bus bought 24.6%, as before (budget 26%).
- **2026-10-14** (`src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/sim/save/save.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/{Saves,Notices}.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`, `e2e/saves.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **saves that work** (D26, the systems audit's saves lane). No format change: `SAVE_FORMAT`, the schema and `schema.lock.json` are as they were.
  - **Rows.** A slot this game cannot load says why, as the set-aside rows do ("Slot 1 · A save from an older build (format 14)", or from a newer build, another city or one it can't read), never loads, and has Export (`export-slot`, the slot's own text as `interbus-boston-slot-N.json`) and Delete. Save here over a slot that holds anything, readable or not, asks first ("Replace Slot N" or "Keep it"), with Export still beside it, so a save this game can't load can be kept before it goes. Such a slot is not moved to a backup key on its own, since that key could hold the autosave's own set-aside save of the same format; the player's Export is how it is kept. The autosave has a row of its own, first, as a slot's (day, company, cash, lines, buses, saved when), in place of the hint "This game saves itself as it plays", now the row's tooltip. Saves lists its saves again as the store keeps writes, at most every 2 s (an autosave every 0.67 s at 5,400×), since each listing has the worker read every save.
  - **I13.** The autosave's row makes Saves at its tallest (every slot full and a set-aside save listed) **24.3%** of 1152×720, from 22.3% (budget 26%; 19.2% at 1366×768 and 15.5% at 1440×900).
  - **A browser that keeps no saves.** When the store refuses a write or a read (blocked site data, a full disk), a notice says "This browser isn’t keeping saves" once, timed only while it shows, and Saves says so with Export, until a write is kept again. Before, each failure went only to the console and a reload lost the game. Since amended (2026-10-15, above): a backup the start cannot write is not this failure; it holds the live key and says "Not autosaving: see Saves" instead. Amended again in the third audit round (2026-10-15, above): Saves' line holds while a save of the game's own the browser refused (the autosave, the quarter's start) is not kept since, and a delete in Saves writes what the game owes at once; any other refusal holds it until a write is kept, as before. And a third time (2026-10-15, above): a full store (a QuotaExceededError) says "No room for saves: delete a save", Saves' line names Delete and Export, and these words stay blocked site data's.
  - **Set aside off the map.** A save of this city from another bake whose stops find no street is set aside under `interbus.backup.<city>.off-street`, its own key, and listed as "A save with stops off the new map"; before, it shared `other-city` with a save from another city, so either could replace the other. A save set aside that way by an earlier build is still under `other-city` and still reads as another city's.
  - **Session files.** The save reader reads a file whose `kind` is `interbus-session` (Export session) as unreadable, so importing one is refused as a save it can't read, not as one from an older build.
  - **Restart this quarter** (D10) is refused ("No save from this quarter's start") when the quarter save's company differs in name or colour from the game being played, as after loading another company's ended game; it used to bring back the company played before. A save carries no game id (adding one is a format change), so two games of the same name and colour still pass; the failure card's Restart is then refused with that notice.
- **2026-10-14** (`src/sim/pacing.test.ts`, `src/sim/economy/startingCash.test.ts`; `npm run bench pacing` with `PACING_SEED` and `npm run bench firstline`, both nightly; `npm run bench report`): **the tuning freeze's starting cash and speed: k 1.333, and the ladder's 4 at 2,700×** (the M5 spec, item 2; PLAN §3; D7; I10; amends D25's ladder), measured after the report card's Coverage fix (below). Six variants were measured on the fixture and Inner Boston, each from 77d90ca, then judged and the winner re-measured over twelve seeds.
  - **What changed.** k goes from 1.2 to **1.333**, so a new company starts with **$474,548** (was $427,200) and keeps $118,548 after the founding recipe (was $71,200). The speed ladder's 4 goes from 1,800× (half an hour a second) to **2,700×** (45 minutes a second), a game day in 32 s instead of 48; 1×, 60×, 600× and 5,400× stay. Neither moves a save: the starting cash is written at founding (D7), the speed is not in the save, and the schema hash is unchanged. The grant's bar, the medals, the founding headways and the contract asks are unchanged.
  - **Why both.** k brings the second line earlier in game days; the speed turns game days into fewer minutes (the rate enters only the minutes, `src/sim/pacing.ts`, so game days do not depend on it). On Inner Boston, seeds 1, 2 and 3: the second line on **day 33 (23.4 min), day 33 (23.4) and day 28 (20.8)**, against PLAN §3's 15–25; at k 1.2 and 1,800× they came at 44.5, 42.1 and 27.7 min, and k alone at 31.7, 31.7 and 27.7. Profit on day 1 at 5.4 min and the report card and bronze on day 8 at **9.1 min** (11.0 before) on every seed; silver on day 29 (20.3 min), 28 (19.8) and 28 (21.3); no gold. At 2,700× the on-target days are 18–35, so seeds 1 and 2 have two days of margin. Seed 1 over 200 days: seven lines and three depots, 4,384 trips a day, cash $1,169,949 (five lines, 3,621 and $1,865,169 at k 1.2), cards 63.6–68.7, Coverage the lowest at 8.9–31.6.
  - **Game days are still long, and speed does not change that.** The spec expected 6–10 game days to a second line (V1: about 15); Inner Boston takes 28–33. The founding line nets $1,109 on day 2 at its best fare, a report card pays about $13k a quarter, and on seeds 1–3 no contract the founding line can meet pays before day 27: it reaches two of Inner Boston's six landmarks (two of the fixture's three). The fixture's second line comes sooner for the same reason. This is for P1 and P2 to feel; P2 should note which speeds testers use, since a player at 5,400× reaches day 33 at about 15 minutes.
  - **Across seeds it is not one number.** The scripted player draws its second line as soon as its cash covers the cheapest line, so an early contract decides the day. On Inner Boston, seeds 1–12: on target on 8 (seeds 1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 10: 16.0–24.5 min); **early on 4**, each after a contract on day 9 (seeds 4, 5 and 12 on day 10 at 11.2 min; seed 11 on day 15 at 13.8); none late. At k 1.2 and 1,800× none of the 12 was on target: 11 late (25.3–49.3 min) and seed 12 early (13.3). On the fixture, seeds 1, 5, 7 and 11 draw it on day 10 (11.2 min) after a $60,000 contract on day 9; seeds 2, 3 and 4 at 18.6, 19.2 and 16.0 min. An early second line follows luck the player saw happen, after the first card; a late one was the rule.
  - **What was not taken.** A grant paid from 40 instead of 55 (bronze kept at 55) put seeds 1–3 at 19.7–22.1 min, but the grant's bar is not on the spec's list of tunables, every running company would be paid at least $6,400 a quarter, and the debug line's card would become 6.4× its quarter's running net (about 3.5× now), deepening the finding below that the card is the main income; at 45 every seed waits for a third card (25.3 min). A founding line every 15 minutes put them at 23.7–27.7 (two of three late), doubles the price of a short line's default, and misses its own headway in snow. Contract asks (an easier HEADWAY card) gave 31.7, 31.7 and 20.5: the spread is the asks' luck. 3,600× (an hour a second), by the same arithmetic, would put 6 of the 12 seeds early.
  - **The bands.** The fixture's seed 7 is one of the early ones, so `pacing.test.ts` holds its own milestone, not PLAN §3's: the second line on days 9–13 at 10–14 minutes (measured day 10, 11.2), the report card on day 8 at 9.1, silver on days 18–30 (day 22). PLAN §3's minutes are held on Inner Boston instead: `bench/pacing.ts` exits 1 when profit, the card or the second line misses them, and with them **Boston's balance bands** (EC-12): the founding recipe's day-2 running net $800–$1,400 (measured $1,109 on seeds 1–3), the second line on days 18–35 (28–33), and the first card 60–70 (64.8–65.8). The best first line's day-2 net is held at $1,500–$3,500 by `bench/firstline.ts` (measured $2,523, Washington Street from its first end). The nightly runs both, pacing on seeds 1–3; a failure blocks no merge, so a PR that moves Inner Boston's pace is caught the next morning.
  - **I10, on both cities.** On the fixture (`startingCash.test.ts`): the best first line found without the debug line, Copley to Kenmore whole with one Sparrow ($261,000 with its depot, +$148.87 on day 2), fits the starting cash and turns a daily profit within the quarter; a four-bus line of the starter's shape (a depot, four stops, four Sparrows: $546,000), standing for the sweep's best four-bus line as PLAN §5 words I10, costs **1.1506×** the starting cash, which holds by $270 (so any later cut to a Sparrow's or a stop's price, or a dearer starter, must re-run it); k is inside 1.0–1.8. A two- or three-stop line run every few minutes can carry four Sparrows for less, $538,000 or $542,000 (1.134× and 1.142×): a short line run very often, not the network GD-11's middle game is about. Holding it too would cap k at 1.314, measured on Inner Boston seeds 1–12: 7 on target, 4 early and seed 6 late (day 41, 27.7 min, against day 35 and 24.5 at 1.333), so k stays 1.333. On Inner Boston (`bench/firstline.ts`, nightly, now played for a quarter): 18 of 18 corridor lines fit, 4 turn a day's profit, and the best of them, Washington Street from its first end, costs $356,000; the check picks the best profitable line first and then asks whether it fits. The clause is against cash, as D7 states; with the $150k credit line (D10) a four-bus line is buyable at founding with $78,548 of credit to spare (at k 1.2, $31,200), but it would run the company into the floor's countdown, so the middle game still has to be earned.
  - **What else holds.** The report card's bars, re-measured at k 1.333: ten opening companies grade 54.1 / 58.3 / 66.9, as at k 1.2. The speed changes only how fast the worker runs time: PLAN §6's heap budget is re-measured at 2,700× by the nightly, and the frame-rate steps in `playtests/PROTOCOL.md` now name 2,700×.

- **2026-10-14** (`e2e/copy.spec.ts`, `src/ui/copyRules.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`): **the copy pass** (D25 item 3: "Capitalization is important, make sure everything has proper grammar").
  - **The guard.** `copyRules.ts` asks every heading, button, label, option, column, caption, tooltip and accessible name to start with a capital, a digit or a sign, no word but 2D and 3D in capitals, curly apostrophes and no space before punctuation. `e2e/copy.spec.ts` reads every screen from Home through founding, the demand and speed menus, the depot and draft cards, the line panel, Finance, a stop's card, Saves, a day's close and Contracts; `copy.test.ts` reads every table of words (refusals, set-aside lines, ledger rows, the checklist, speeds, medals, event news).

Rewordings beyond the guard: refusals that stopped mid-thought ("The stop is already this kind"), counted letters for characters, or read as a past tense ("Name your company first"); the stop card's QUIET and LOSS ("Quiet:", "Losing money:"); "Most profit at" for "Best net at"; one wording for an unreadable save; the no-GPU card's "has none for it"; the silver medal's sentence; "Starting cash" for "Founding capital", ahead of Finance's Day 0.
  - **Kept:** the undo chip's "Undo: Line 2’s fare". With a verb ("Undo: Change Line 2’s fare") the shell at rest late in a game took 6.09% of 1152×720, over I13's 6%; as it is, 5.93%.

- **2026-10-14** (`e2e/demand.spec.ts`, `e2e/sim.spec.ts`, `e2e/tilt.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`; `src/render/map/{pick,overlays,tilt}.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/latest.test.ts`, `src/platform/web/prefs.test.ts`, `src/sim/rules/catchment.test.ts`): **the map and 3D items of the owner's initial playtest** (D25 items 3 and 7, but for the copy pass, which is its own PR).
  - **The demand menu.** A 26×26 chip beside the compass opens a menu of two checkboxes, Homes (purple) and Jobs (blue), each with how many people or jobs it stands for, so it is the legend too. Each kind's layer is shown or hidden once per change (RN-04), and the choice is kept in this browser under `interbus.prefs.map` through `src/platform`, apart from every save key. Escape closes the menu and stops there, so an open panel stays open. `data-dots` counts what draws: MapLibre's feature query leaves a hidden layer out.
  - **The dot's card, only on a dot.** A dot's radius is 0.12·√people × 0.5 at zoom 12 to × 2 at zoom 16 (a median home dot of 140 people is 1.8 px at the opening zoom 14), so the pointer picks the dot whose circle holds it, else the nearest edge within 6 px (`pick.ts`, sharing the paint expression's numbers). The card reads the dot's own place and count from the sim's list, not the map's tiles, which round its place: a home dot names its people and the people within a walk, a job dot its jobs and the jobs within a walk (`jobsWithinWalk`, a read-only rule beside `peopleWithinWalk`). The sim is asked once per dot, and only the newest question's answer counts, withdrawn when the pointer leaves the dot; before, a late answer could bring the card back over nothing.
  - **The contract rings** are gone with their layers and source; a festival's ring and the draft's walk rings stay.
  - **The gesture.** MapLibre's own mousedown is claimed for a right-drag, a Ctrl-drag and a double-click's second press (with no tool out), so its pan never runs on them. Moved 3 px (MapLibre's click tolerance), the press drags: up tilts at 0.25° a pixel, down flattens, and in 3D sideways turns at 0.8° a pixel (MapLibre's rotate speed). Held still 500 ms (MapLibre's long-press delay), a double-click flips 2D and 3D. A quick double-click zooms a step as before. Preventing the dblclick that follows a held one would also stop the flip's ease halfway, since MapLibre stops the camera whenever a handler takes an event (its `handler_manager.ts`), so the double-click zoom is switched off from the moment a hold is used until that dblclick has passed.
  - **Turning.** Released, a turn within 7° of north snaps to it (MapLibre's bearingSnap); flattening always faces north; Q and E turn 15° and a second press turns on from where the first is going; touch rotation is on in 3D only. No renderer or custom-layer change: the bus layer already drew through the full camera matrix.
  - **I13** at 1152×720: at rest 3.1%, the demand menu open 5.2% (panel budget 26%), and late in a game, paused with an undo to offer, 5.94% against 5.96% with the 56×28 chip; two 26×26 buttons take 1,352 px² where the chip took about 1,568.
  - **Tests** (TS-04), each shown failing with the old behaviour put back: the double-click held does nothing (mode stays 2D); the dblclick left to MapLibre (zoom 15, not 14); no turn on the drag (bearing 0, not 120); the toggle doing nothing (649 dots still drawn); Escape reaching the page (Finance closed); the card at any point (the card's place is the pointer's, not the dot's); the old overlays (three contract layers); no slop (a 1.8 px dot 4 px away not picked); the old probe rule (late and withdrawn answers kept).

- **2026-10-14** (`npm run bench routes`; `src/sim/world.test.ts`, `src/sim/city/route.test.ts`): **routes without loops in Back Bay and downtown** (D25, the owner's initial playtest: "it makes all these large loops").
  - **Two causes:**
    - **Stops on the highway.** 188 of Inner Boston's 11,687 street nodes touch only motorways or ramps: the Mass Pike under Back Bay and the I-93 tunnel and its ramps downtown. A click near one put the stop on the highway, and its buses drove to the next exit and back. Prudential to Back Bay station came back by 7,976 m for 442 m.
    - **One-way streets kept on the way out only.** A leg had to leave and reach each stop in the street's direction, so a stop on a one-way street facing against the line sent the bus round the block. The way back already drove the way out in reverse, against those streets (`rules/position.ts`).
  - **The fix:**
    - Stops, depots and route ends snap through one grid that leaves those nodes out (`world.ts` `stopNode`; `config/lines.ts` `NO_STOP_CLASSES`). Buses still drive the highways between stops.
    - The router drives every street both ways (`BUSES_KEEP_ONE_WAY`, false). One-way streets stay in the city file for the lane markings (PLAN §8 rule 9).
    - No rebake, and no save byte changes.
  - **Seeded click pairs 250–600 m apart**, the distance driven against the straight line between the nodes they snap to. The four routings are before, stops off motorways, one-way not kept, and both:

    | 500 pairs | Median | p99 | Worst | Over 2× | On a motorway |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Back Bay, before | 1.49× | 19.1× | 41.3× | 30.1% | 21 |
    | Back Bay, stops off motorways | 1.46× | 5.9× | 7.6× | 28.5% | 0 |
    | **Back Bay, both** | **1.27×** | **2.1×** | **2.4×** | **1.4%** | 0 |
    | Downtown, before | 1.62× | 9.8× | 20.4× | 28.8% | 31 |
    | Downtown, stops off motorways | 1.60× | 5.4× | 9.8× | 24.2% | 0 |
    | **Downtown, both** | **1.27×** | **2.1×** | **2.2×** | **1.6%** | 0 |

  - **Named legs, there and back** (metres, straight line in brackets):
    - Copley to Boylston at Mass Ave: 1,203 / 834 → 826 / 826 (825).
    - Prudential to Back Bay station: 907 / 7,976 → 600 / 600 (422).
    - Downtown Crossing to Government Center: 817 / 945 → 679 / 679 (481).
    - South Station to Park Street: 1,515 / 1,334 → 1,066 / 1,066 (693).

No earlier version had one; V1 had only a lost-context card (`Intralines@7af45b1:src/map/MapView.tsx:124-131`).
  - **The rungs** (`render/map/gpu.ts`, decided once before the map mounts): **full** where WebGL2 runs on a GPU; **Low graphics** where it runs in software, 2D only, the map at one pixel a pixel and the buses' frames asked for at most 30 times a second; **none** without WebGL2, a calm card, "Turn on hardware acceleration to see the map", high on the board so Home stays clear below it, with the game, Saves and Export working as ever. A context the browser takes away mid-game shows a card with Reload, and clears if the browser gives it back. (Since amended, 2026-10-16, the fifth audit round, above: both cards say the game is saved only while it saves itself, and offer Export this game otherwise.) Low graphics' `T` says "the map stays flat on this computer" for 4 s, until week 10's chip says why it is off.
  - **Software, known two ways.** WebGL's `failIfMajorPerformanceCaveat` alone missed it: the e2e tests' Chromium (with `--enable-unsafe-swiftshader`) grants WebGL2 on SwiftShader without the caveat, reporting "ANGLE (Google, Vulkan 1.3.0 (SwiftShader Device (Subzero) …), SwiftShader driver)". So a renderer named SwiftShader, llvmpipe, softpipe or Microsoft Basic Render Driver counts as software too.
  - **Tests.** Since CI draws in software, the e2e tests now play in Low graphics, which tests the probe on every run; the tilt tests ask `?gpu=full`, and `?gpu=none` forces the card. `?gpu=` and `?fps` are read through `src/platform/` (`web/switches.ts`); a player on a slow machine can ask for `?gpu=low`. On this container's SwiftShader the map draws about 5 frames a second while buses run, under the cap, so the cap itself is tested as a function (`frameDelayMs`). TS-04: with software taken for hardware, a rung granted without WebGL2, SwiftShader's name unknown or no cap, the matching test fails.

It sits beside the build SHA, outside the schema the hash covers, so it is no new format: an older build reads a newer save and ignores it, a save from before it shows no time, and a time that is not a number makes the save unreadable as any mistyped field does. Saves writes it in the player's own time zone ("saved Oct 13, 20:41", the year only when it is not this one).

  - **The rule** (`rules/express.ts`, one predicate for the riders, the line card, the draft card and the sweep): V1's, a line of 5,000 m or more of street with its stops 1,200 m apart on average and at least three of them (`Intralines@7af45b1:src/game/constants.ts:398-434`); the spec's two weights, a ride felt at 0.85 of its minutes, dwells and all, and the fare's distance from the reference minded at half, in the fast assignment and the reference loop alike; V1's ×1.5 subsidy left out (X-20: keep the predicate, taper the reward). - **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench jumps`; `src/sim/rules/transition.test.ts`, `src/worker/i6.test.ts`, `src/sim/settling.test.ts`, `src/sim/save/rebase.test.ts`): **no bus jumps on a change made with buses out** (the M4 spec, item 4; I6).
  - **Before.** Positions are a pure function of a line's day plan, its streets and the clock, so any change rebuilt every bus out at once. At 08:00 on day 2 with the debug line every 10 minutes (`npm run bench jumps`, the host and the fleet the vehicle layer draws, buses paired in order): taking Symphony off moved a bus **262.1 m**, extending beyond Longwood **2,774.4 m**, Metro 40s **94.4 m**, every 15 or every 5 minutes **1,570.3 m**, and roadworks starting at a midnight with the last trips out **189.6 m**.
  - **The rule** (`rules/transition.ts`), one timeline as the spec asks: a command, a new depot, or roadworks starting or ending recompute *in service*, and a line whose plan changed with any of its buses out keeps the version it ran on (its places, model, depot drives and plan numbers; save format 12). Its plan in effect is merged: every trip that left before the change finishes on its own version, route and times; the departures from the change on are the new version's, each taken by the bus free longest at the first stop or one from the depot that can reach it in time, never more than the line owns (one no bus can make is not run). Where the first stop or the model changed, the old buses go home after their trip, setting off no earlier than the change. The merged plan hands over to the line's own day plan at the first moment from which every bus does exactly what one of the day plan's does, so nothing moves there either; the close after it lets the earlier version go. Undo, a load and a rebased save recompute plainly, so an undo puts the game back byte for byte; a rebased save starts each line settled. Since amended (2026-10-15, the third audit round, above): every change in service is merged, with buses out or not; a line just drawn changes from a version that ran no bus and keeps every version until its draw day's close; a bus carries on only if every change since its trip kept its first stop and model; and the close retimes for an event before it lets settled lines go. And again (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): a line keeps every version it ran on the day of a change until that day's close, which lets them all go once it has settled and otherwise trims to the last version it had settled onto.
  - **After.** All six cases move no bus at all (0.0 m). `src/worker/i6.test.ts` drives each through the host and the fleet: no bus moves over 5 m at the change, every bus is within 25 m of where one was a second before for three game hours (leaving or reaching a depot aside), and buses out never exceed buses owned, a swap's old buses, sold at the click, finishing their trips beside the new ones. `transition.test.ts` adds 25 random instants for each of six changes on a synthetic line.
  - **The handover**, at 25 random instants of each change on the debug line laid straight (`transition.test.ts`'s six, no overnight service): **20–30 minutes** after a shorter headway, roadworks, an extension, a dropped first stop or a model swap, but **5 to 17 hours** after a longer headway on the same buses, which keeps a bus spare until service ends (the next morning's first departure at the latest). On lines running overnight, 20 minutes to 18 hours, always inside the plan's two days. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fifth audit round, above): a line parks the buses a change leaves spare as its day plan would, overnight service or not, so after a longer headway it hands over at p50 50 to 54 minutes, at most 387 on `npm run bench parking`'s grid, and never at the window's end, where a line running overnight had handed over after a cut and a bus jumped.
  - **Costs** bill what the buses do: each leg and depot drive at the length, model and depot of the version it ran on. Extending the debug line at 07:45 banks from the change exactly the forecast made at it (`settling.test.ts`); billed on the new version throughout, the same hours would cost $388.62 in running against $384.84.
  - **Cost of it.** Inner Boston's whole recompute: p50 98.4 ms with every line settled, 102.5 ms with all ten corridors settling at once.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): with every change recomputed plainly, all six I6 cases fail at the change; with a bus waiting at the first stop sent home as if it had set off after its last trip, dropping a first stop or swapping the model throws it 182.9 m (1,001 m through the host for Metro 40s); with the handover judged at a departure, the day plan's bus already pulling out for it was missing there; with every hour billed on the new version, the rows miss the forecast by $3.78.
  - **Not done.** A swap's old buses are drawn at the new model's length until they are home, and the line card does not say that a line is settling.

- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/demand/assign.ts`, `src/sim/demand/reference/assignNaive.ts`; `npm run bench congestion`, `npm run bench compare`): **riders feel rush hour too** (the M3 spec's item 15, its ridership half).
  - **The model.** A rider picks the stops the day's average traffic makes quickest, and each period's choice between lines, changes and the car is made on those stops' rides at that period's traffic. The reference loop does the same, so it still checks only the fast loop's groups. Made exact per period instead (stops chosen again in each period), it gave the debug line the same riders to 0.1, so nothing rests on the simpler choice.
  - **The fast loop** keeps each option's stops and adds, per period, the difference between its route's table at that period's traffic and at the day's: a one-seat ride's ride and fare, or a change's two rides, fare and choice of where to change. Its first version added the traffic outside the fare's floor (a felt ride never under half a minute): on the four-lines case, where one line charges $1.50, that line's short rides looked slower at peak than the reference's and its riders went to its neighbour, whose peak busiest leg came out 23.2% over (limit 10%). The four-lines test caught it.
  - **Riders.** The debug line: peak 307.5 → 290.4, off-peak 306.9 → 320.2, the day 614.4 → 610.5 (−0.6%). Inner Boston's ten corridors: 5,900.0 → 5,884.1 trips a day (−0.27%), peak boardings 3,581.9 → 3,331.8, off-peak 2,973.7 → 3,197.8.
  - **Against the reference.** The debug line −2.0% at peak and −1.1% off-peak (−1.3% in both without the rush, so the old "0.5% measured" was stale; the test now allows 2.5%); four lines within 5.1% in every line-period; Kenmore's changes −8.1% (−7.2% before; allowed 9%). A Shelter at Symphony now adds 39.9 riders a day where it added 32.0; the reference gives 32.5 (30.2 without the rush). The groups move their riders to Symphony together, so the rush's difference on their longer or shorter rides lands at once; the totals stay within 0.4%.
  - **Recompute** on Inner Boston, 100 runs each, interleaved on this container: p50 85.6–86.8 ms against 81.5–82.8 without it, p95 122.3–133.4 ms against 108.6–128.3, under I14's 150 ms. The route tables are read once per route and interleaved by period, which took p50 down from 98–108 ms in the first version. Thirty-run p95s swung from 122 to 219 ms here across both versions, so Nov 1's number comes from GitHub's runner, as planned.
- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/traffic/congestion.ts`, `src/sim/rules/cycle.ts`, `src/sim/rules/schedule.ts`; `npm run bench congestion`): **rush hour lengthens round trips by period** (the M3 spec's item 15, its timing half; PLAN cut 8, R10, K06).
  - **The factor.** V2's hourly curve, divided by its mean over the day's trips (the hourly profile's half-hours), so every street's factor averages 1.00 over the trips that ride it, and V2's class weights read off OSM's classes. A line's weight is its route's metres-weighted class weight. Each period takes its half-hours' trip-weighted mean: primary and secondary streets 1.111 at peak, 0.894 off-peak and 0.806 overnight; motorway and trunk 1.130 / 0.875 / 0.772; tertiary 1.053 / 0.949 / 0.907; every other street 1.017 / 0.983 / 0.970. Only the drive is scaled, never the dwells or layovers.
  - **Per period, not per half-hour.** A period's trips share one round trip, so the day plan and the demand model read the same three numbers. Even so, a slow period's trips can still be out when a busier one starts, so counting each period's buses on its own falls short: with the old count, the day-plan property test finds a timing whose plan needs 3 buses where the count says 2 (peak 6,365 s every hour, off-peak 4,884 s every 45 minutes). The count is now the longest round trip over the shortest headway, which no trip outlasts and no two departures beat; where one period is both the slowest and the busiest, as the peak usually is, that is its own count. A line short of buses spaces its departures by its longest round trip. With one round trip all day, both rules are what they were.

Its day's wages fall from $972.11 to $970.69, as the last off-peak runs end sooner; running costs follow the kilometres and do not move. The starved hourly line's Symphony stop goes from −$35.92 to −$33.51 a day.

Every piece with that id, at every zoom, then takes the height, so a building cut by a tile edge stands at one height, and the 1,324 pieces below zoom 15 with a height go from 1,062 to 1,207. Heights are written as numbers, as the style's extrusion reads them. The layer's 153,361 address points (144,444 ids, 128,819 of them their building's) are points and are left alone.

The file grows from 15,347,095 B to 15,750,925 B (+2.6%), G10 still finds no transit, and the join takes 22 s.

- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/demand/split.test.ts`, `src/sim/demand/assign.test.ts`, `e2e/transfer.spec.ts`; `npm run bench split`, `npm run bench recompute`, `npm run bench compare`, `npm run bench crowding`): **one change of line, paid once a trip** (step 11's second part; the M3 spec, item 13; I4, EC-04, EC-10; exit check 5).
  - **The rule** (`src/sim/demand/assign.ts`). A trip may change line once: from a stop of its first line to a stop of another that calls there or stands within 250 m. It costs the walk at the game's 12 min/km, the boarding stop's tier's penalty (6 / 6 / 5 / 3.5 / 2 minutes) and the second line's wait. The spec's "a 1.5-minute walk" for 250 m was wrong at that pace: it is 3 minutes, and the code walks it at the same pace as any other walk. A change and a one-seat ride starting on the same line take the same first bus, so each first line offers its quicker, and riders scan the first lines as before (I5). Since amended (2026-10-16, the fifth audit round, above): ways off one first line that tie exactly split its riders evenly. No change ends within 250 m of where it began (that rider would walk, and the captive share would otherwise send some round a loop).
  - **Who changes.** Nobody changes onto a line they could walk to from home, nor off one that reaches their work. That is how most riders behave, and it keeps the cost in budget: it alone cut Inner Boston's recompute from 182 to 126 ms p50 (223–265 to 192 ms p95), before the speed-ups below. The reference loop applies the same rule, sub-point by sub-point.
  - **Money (EC-04).** A trip feels its fare once, the first line's, weighed against both rides' km, and pays it and the city's subsidy once, on the first line. Each line now carries paid boardings beside all boardings; the hourly bank, Finance, the fare band, the line card's income and each stop's net read the paid ones, and the trips a day the sim panel shows are the paid boardings. The report card's comfort share still counts every boarding. A changing rider still shares the second line's costs at the stop where they board. Saves are unchanged: `boardedMilli` now banks paid boardings, which were all boardings before.
  - **I4.** Splitting the debug line where both halves call at one stop, every 10 minutes, cuts the model's day of fares and subsidy by 6.9% to 24.0% (at Symphony or the MFA, a Sign or a Transfer Hub); the test holds a day as banked. On Inner Boston's ten corridors split in the middle, the model's day falls 4.8% to 32.6%. Charging every boarding, as V1 did, the same splits gain up to +22.7% (the debug line every 5 minutes with a hub) and +15.7% (Beacon Street with a hub). The test is shown failing (TS-04) with a change paying on its second line: +2.0%, +13.2% and +19.4%. Every 20 minutes the second wait loses more riders than charging each boarding gains (−2.5% to −27.5%), so the test runs at 10.
  - **Exit check 5.** Copley–Kenmore and Kenmore–Longwood, drawn with the line tool, the second's click joining the first's stop, carry 52.6 changes a day at Kenmore. The stop card says "≈53 of them change here from another line, on the fare they paid". Without changes the e2e fails (TS-04). The fast loop holds to the reference there: trips −5.3%, the lines −5.7% and −5.4%, changes onto the second line −7.2%. Each line alone is already −5.3% and −4.6% (the groups' approximation on a line of two stops), so changes add nothing to it. The test fails with the change's walk and penalty left out (trips 13.0% off). On the fixture's four overlapping lines, trips are −1.21% (worst line-period 5.00%).
  - **Riders.** On Inner Boston, T03's ten lines (Sparrows every 10/15 minutes) carry 5,900.0 trips a day, up from 5,238.5 (+12.6%): 6,562.5 boardings, of which 662.5 are changes. The fixture's single-line tests, the fare band and I3's sweep are unchanged.
  - **Cost.** The same machine, the same minutes, `npm run bench`, `main` against this: Inner Boston 58–66 → 81–96 ms p50 and 79–96 → 131–157 ms p95 over eight runs (one at 157 ms). The synthetic grid, where no change happens, went 82.5–83.1 → 72.6–81.5 ms p50. The one-seat part got faster on the way: with changes switched off this code takes 40–42 ms p50. That came from carrying a route's riders as each option is found, a home group's reach to every stop worked out once per home cell, single-word masks, one carry per option for all three periods, and exponents built as products. The p95 is this container's noise as much as the code: after its first two, one run's 40 samples ranged 77–147 ms. So I14's 150 ms holds with little margin here; Nov 1's measurement (D11) should be taken on GitHub's runner. R2's 100 ms p95 is out of reach with transfers, so Inner Boston stays the extent.
- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/demand/hub.test.ts`; `npm run bench`, `npm run bench compare`, `npm run bench crowding`): **riders scan every line at a stop** (step 11's first part; the M3 spec, item 13; I5, K23).
  - **The rule** (`commonLines` in `src/sim/demand/assign.ts`, the classic common-lines choice). Of the lines serving a trip, lines join in order of their minutes before waiting, each only while it makes the expected trip quicker. That expected trip is the wait for the set's combined headway, 1 ÷ Σ 1/h, plus the ride weighted by frequency. A rider takes the first bus of the set, so each line carries its share of the set's frequency. Until now each pair of sub-points rode the quickest line alone.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): six lines along the debug line's four stops each carry a sixth of their riders, together more than one line carries but under twice as many. With the quickest line alone, line 1 boarded nobody, because ties went to whichever sorted first. Two equal lines every 20 minutes split evenly at a 10-minute headway's wait; lines every 10 and 30 minutes a minute apart split 75/25; a line 40 minutes slower is never worth the wait. The reference loop scans the same way, and `npm run bench compare` holds them within 2.3% per line and period (trips −0.79%).
  - **Cost.** The same machine, the same hour, `npm run bench`, `main` against this: Inner Boston 43.9 → 55.9 ms p50 and 63.3 → 83.6 ms p95; the synthetic grid 71.4 → 83.7 ms p50 and 93.9 → 118.4 ms p95. Both stay inside I14's 150 ms, and Inner Boston inside R2's 100 ms (D11). The fixture's single-line tests are unchanged; on Inner Boston T03's ten lines carry 5,238.5 riders a day (was 5,228.7).

  - **The scale.** At 1000‰ the debug line's riders differ sharply between extents. The fixture's box keeps every commute inside it, so it offers 5,534.9 a day; Inner Boston's workers spread over all of its jobs, so it offers 2,200.5 (0.398 times). Inner Boston therefore takes **279‰**, at which the debug line carries 613.9, what the fixture carries at its 111‰ (614.4). Economics measured on the fixture then hold on the extent the preview will play.

    - 16 of 19 cost $356,000 and fit the $427,200 founding capital; the three needing a third Sparrow cost $451,000 (1.06 times). Two more were refused for want of a street near a stop.
    - Four make a profit on day 2: Washington Street from downtown (+$2,267 a day, 1,030 riders), the debug line (+$698, 610 riders), Dorchester Avenue (+$497) and Huntington Avenue from Copley (+$326).
    - The other twelve lose $822 to $1,697 a day, so where the first line goes matters.
    - The cheapest four-bus line, $546,000, costs 1.28 times the founding capital. So D7's rule, k = 1.2 × the starter line, holds on Inner Boston unchanged.
  - **Crowding at calibrated demand.** No rider is turned away on T03's ten lines every 10 or 15 minutes, on Sparrows or Metro 40s. Every 20 minutes, Washington Street turns away 49.3 a day at 17:00. The 12.7% measured at 1000‰ was demand the extent does not have.
- **2026-10-13** (`node tools/bake/beta.mjs boston <extent>`; `src/sim/demand/beta.test.ts`; `npm run bench report`, `npm run bench crowding`, `npm run bench`): **the gravity decay β is fitted to Boston's commutes** (city-boston-r1, the M3 spec's Nov 15 item; PLAN §5).
  - **The fit.** β is the decay in the commuter matrix, exp(-d/β) (`src/sim/demand/commuters.ts`). The bake fits it so the matrix's mean commute between cells equals LODES's own, over its 2023 origin-destination pairs with both ends inside the area.
    - Inner Boston: 735 cells, 212,081 such commuters, mean **3.499 km**. V1's 4,000 m gave 3.655 km; **3,260 m** meets it.
    - The fixture's own fit would be 1,240 m (8,884 commuters, mean 1.222 km), because its box cuts off every longer commute. It takes Inner Boston's 3,260 m instead.
    - The value is per extent in `cities/boston/city.json`'s calib. `sim.json` carries it as `calib.gravityBetaM`, and the loader refuses a file without it. The fit's copy of the matrix is held to the game's to nine decimals, and it recovers a β of 2,500 m from commutes the matrix made (TS-04: counting a cell's own jobs breaks both).
  - **What moved on the fixture.** The debug line carries **614.4** riders a day (was 636.3, −3.4%), inside the calibration band of 600–1,000, so `tripScale` stays at 111‰. Its fare optimum and the starving clause are unchanged. Other changes:
    - a Shelter at Symphony adds 32.0 riders a day (was 32.4);
    - the starved line's Symphony reads LOSS at −$35.92 a day (was −$34.23), and Longwood is still QUIET at 1.7 boardings;
    - the debug line's report card grades **74.0** and pays $30,335, 4.2 times its quarter's running net of $7,154 (was 74.3, $30,813, 3.9 times);
    - ten opening companies grade 53.7 / 57.9 / 64.0 (was 63.8 at the top), so the bars stay.
  - **On Inner Boston**, Sparrows every 10 minutes turn away 12.7% of riders (Washington Street 2,378.6 a day; was 13.3%), and Metro 40s 0.7% (133.1; was 0.8%). The recompute takes 47.9 ms p50 and 60.1 ms p95.

- **2026-10-13** (`src/worker/session.test.ts`, `e2e/session.spec.ts`, `npm run bench replay <file>`): **Export session replays to the same game** (the M3 spec, item 12; AR-06; I15's "Export session replayed").
  - **The file.** Saves has **Export session**: the save the session began from, the speed at that moment, and every command since, each at the game second it ran (speed changes and undo included, since they decide what undo does), with the build's SHA and the game's fingerprint where it ends. A session begins at each quarter's start (the save Restart this quarter reopens), and again wherever a slot, a file or a restart is loaded. It lives in the worker, so a reload begins a new one from the autosave: a bug report covers play since the last reload, never a gap.
  - **The replay** is the same host the game runs, given the session's save and speed, then each command at its game second, stepping between them as ticks would. A session played in Chromium on the page (found, a depot, the debug line, a fare changed at 3×, paused) replays in Node, on the same sim file, to the same fingerprint. `npm run bench replay <file>` replays a player's file at a checked-out build.
  - **Size.** The unit test's session (founding to day 2, ten commands) is 2,118 bytes, 968 of them the founding save; the spec expected the save, about 10 KB, plus a few KB of commands. A later quarter's start save carries the ledger so far, so it grows by a day card a day. Since amended (2026-10-15, the third audit round, below): days older than the last 16 fold into totals, so no save grows past its first 16 days.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): with speed changes left out of the session, the replay never learns that time ran, so its undo takes back a fare set while it ran and the fingerprints differ; with a session begun mid-run at a quarter's close recorded as paused, the same.
  - **I13.** The Saves panel takes 12.7%, 15.7% and 19.8% of the screen at 1440×900, 1366×768 and 1152×720 (budget 26%); the shell at rest is unchanged.
  - **Contracts' draws.** Contracts are the game RNG's only user so far. Events (M4) should take their own saved stream, as V2's salted streams did, so adding them never shifts the contract deck.
- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench contracts`; `src/sim/economy/contracts.test.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts`, `e2e/contracts.spec.ts`, `e2e/line.spec.ts`): **HEADWAY contracts, and the checklist's last step** (the M3 spec, items 11 and 12; exit check 2; I11).
  - **The rules.** Three slots, filled at a day's start (and at founding) by the game's seeded RNG at landmarks with no contract. Each asks for a bus every 10, 15 or 20 minutes for $240k, $150k or $60k (V2's). A landmark's figure is its best stop within a 650 m walk: all the lines calling there together (1 ÷ Σ 1/h), at the slower of peak and off-peak, so no contract is met by one period alone. A held contract is checked at the end of every service hour (05:00–24:00) and graded at the close: a day met grows its run, a missed hour starts it again. Ten days in a row within the 20 from taking it pay once, as a new **Contracts** row; it lapses as soon as the days left cannot hold the days still needed. Taking one is a click and undoable while paused. Contract rewards, like the report card, stay out of "a day in profit" and the card's Money grade. Saves are format 9.
  - **The deck (I11, GD-04).** Asks are dealt from a shuffled deck of the three, saved with the game, so every three offers hold one of each. I11's company (the debug line every 10 minutes, taking every offer it meets) earns **$720k–$1,080k over 30 days on 100 seeds** (median $900k; 9 of 10 runs of ten seeds within 30% of their median), and **$1.11M–$1.29M over 40 days** (every run of ten within it), which the test holds. With V2's independent draws, 30 days give $540k–$1.35M and only 5 of 10 runs of ten pass.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): with V2's grading (the day's hour-weighted mean headway), a line every 10 minutes at peak and 16 off-peak reads 14.1 minutes and meets a 15-minute ask, where the rule reads 16; with independent draws, three offers in a row repeat an ask; with the day's count frozen, the e2e's contract (every 15 minutes at Longwood, nine days in at 23:50 on day 10) is never paid.
  - **On screen.** The checklist's "5/5 Take a contract" opens a Contracts panel, and once taken the Contracts chip ("1 held") takes its place, so contracts add nothing to the shell at rest: at 1152×720 with a line, the shell at rest takes 5.6% (budget 6%) and the panel 18.7% (budget 26%). Each card reads the ask, the reward, "Today: every 20 min" against it, and a held one's days ("9 of 10 days · 11 days left"). On the map each contract's reach is a ring at its landmark, dashed while on offer, with its ask and, once held, its days met. The day card names a contract paid or lapsed.
  - **A finding for week 9's tuning.** Contracts dwarf a line's running: a $150k contract held 10 days is $15k a day, about 8 times the debug line's best running net (+$1,905.82 a day). V2 found the same ("the early game's real cash channel", GD-04).
- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/economy/credit.test.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts` "the failure floor", `e2e/floor.spec.ts`): **the failure floor (D10): a credit line, a countdown and Restart this quarter** (the M3 spec, item 10; exit check 4).
  - **The rules.** Cash may go to −$150k on one lender's credit line (V1's lenders, `Intralines@7af45b1:src/game/store.ts:1765-1831`). A purchase spends into it and is refused one cent past it, with the shortfall in words, as before at $0 (the debug line on Metro 40s, after the first depot, is "$108,800 short"). Since amended (2026-10-15, the second audit round): a change that spends nothing or gives money back (a headway cut, a reordered or shorter route, a swap that sells for more than it buys) is no purchase and goes through below the floor too, as the ledger always let it; one predicate, `shortfallCents`, answers for every card and command. Each day's close charges 0.06% of what is owed as an Interest row, so $90.00 a day at the limit; Finance, the day card and the report card's Money grade count it as a running cost. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): on a quarter's last day the interest is charged after the day's contract rewards and before the card's grant or fine, and the card grades it. Only running losses and interest take cash below −$150k, and five day closes in a row below it end the run; a close above it starts the count again. Saves are format 8 (the Interest row kind and the count of closes below).
  - **On screen** (since amended, 2026-10-14, saves that work, above: Restart is refused after loading another company's ended game). From the first close below, a red chip reads "5 days to get above -$150k", counting down. On the fifth, time stops, play is refused ("The lender has called in the loan") and a card offers **Restart this quarter**, which loads a save the worker writes by itself at founding, at each quarter's close and after a slot or a file is loaded, so a loaded game restarts where it was loaded. An ended game is never kept as the quarter to restart: when the fifth close is also the quarter's last, Restart reopens the quarter just graded, and loading an ended game leaves the quarter save as it was. A missing quarter save is refused in words.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): with the count never reset by a close above the floor, `credit.test.ts` fails; with the count frozen, the e2e, an imported game owing $200k at 23:50 with four closes behind it, never shows the card; with either guard on the quarter save removed, `host.test.ts` restarts into an ended game or day 1.
  - **Sandbox** turns failure off (PLAN §3, D10), but no Sandbox mode exists yet; it reads `runEnded` when it lands.
- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench report`; `src/sim/economy/reportCard.test.ts`): **the quarter's report card, and its bars from a measured spread** (the M3 spec, item 9; exit check 3).
  - **The grades**, 0–100 each and weighed equally: Coverage counts residents by the best stop within a walk at (calls a day ÷ 64)^0.75, so a pole nothing calls at covers nobody (V2's fix, EC-07); Reliability is each line's running headway against its own timetable, and a company that runs nothing scores 0 (V2 scored 100); Comfort is 80% the riders not turned away and 20% the stops' tiers by boardings; Money is 50 plus 50 × the quarter's operating net ÷ its revenue, capital and cards left out. The card closes each quarter's last day and its payout or fine is one of that day's rows (a new row kind, save format 7).
  - **The spread** of ten opening companies on the fixture, each with a depot and one seeded random four-stop line at the founding headways, run untouched for a quarter: overall **53.7 min, 57.9 median, 63.8 max** (the spec expected 45–65 around 56; V2 measured 45.8 / 56.4 / 65.6). Coverage ranged 35.0–58.3, Money 0–20.0 (most random lines lose money), and Reliability and Comfort were 100 and 80 for all.
  - **The bars stay V1's**: a grant of (overall − 55) × $1,600 above 55, a fine of $8,000 under 35. The median opening company earns about $4,600 and none is fined; a company that runs nothing is fined.
  - **A finding for week 9's tuning.** The checklist's debug line grades 74.3 and the card pays $30,813, 3.9 times its quarter's running net of $7,997, so for a good network the card is the main income. V1's payout was sized against V1's economy, where a naive starter line made +$3,167 a day as an express ([EC-05](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **2026-10-13** (`src/worker/host.test.ts` "save slots, export and import", `e2e/saves.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **three save slots beside the autosave, and a save file out and in** (the M3 spec, item 8). The worker names the slot keys and asks the page to read them, as it asks for the autosave, so the save format stays in the sim. Loading a slot or a file asks first ("Replace this game"; since amended, 2026-10-15: Import had never asked, and now does, and neither asks before a company is founded, when there is no game to lose), and the game becomes it only if the sim can play it there: a file it cannot read, or one from a newer build, is refused in words ("Not a save this game can read") and the game and its autosave stay as they were, byte for byte. With the imported text written over the autosave first, as V1 did (`Intralines@7af45b1:src/game/store.ts:1883-1901`), that test fails (TS-04). Export hands over this game as `interbus-boston-day-N.json` through `src/platform`, and it imports back to the same game.
  - **I13 is getting tight at rest.** With a fifth dock button (Saves), the shell takes 3.0–4.7% at rest and 3.5–5.5% at rest with a line at 1440×900, 1366×768 and 1152×720 (budget 6%); the Saves panel takes 10.8–16.9% (budget 26%). A sixth dock button would likely break the at-rest budget at 1152×720, so contracts (item 11) go inside an existing panel or the dock's buttons merge.
- **2026-10-13** (`src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/sim/save/rebase.test.ts`): **a save from another bake of Boston carries on** (the M3 spec, item 8; the r1→r2 save test), replacing M2's setting it aside. Stops are anchored by place, so each goes onto the new bake's nearest street node within the stop rule's 150 m; the game is saved at once under the new bake's name, and a note says "Save carried onto the new map". A stop with no street within 150 m, or a line whose stops no longer join by street, sets the save aside under the backup key with "Save's stops miss the new map: set aside", and a new game starts. A save from another city is still set aside, never read into this one. With the same streets, every stop, rider count and cent comes through unchanged; a stop moved 40 m off its node lands back on one. A missing city file already reads as the replaced-build card (`e2e/swap.spec.ts`), never another city.
- **2026-10-13** (`src/worker/host.test.ts` "undo while paused", `e2e/undo.spec.ts`): **undo while paused passes I9.** Every command taken while paused (a depot, a line, headways, a model, a fare, a stop's tier) keeps the game's state from just before it, and Undo puts the newest back: state and cash to the cent, and the save written afterwards is byte for byte the one written before the command, with no Refunds row, since no time ran in between. The chip and Ctrl+Z (⌘Z) name it, as in "Undo: Create Line 1". Unpausing lets the stack go, so a later undo is refused with "Time has run since then" and the income since is kept; with the stack kept across the unpause, as V2's undo was (`Intralines_City_dep@archive/intralines-city-v1:Gaming/src/60-main.js:602-695`), the undo rewinds it (TS-04). Founding is not undoable, and undo lives in the worker, not the save.
- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/economy/finance.test.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **Finance lists every ledger row by kind**, for today so far, yesterday and this quarter so far, beside a whole day's forecast (the M3 spec, item 6). Since amended (2026-10-15, the third audit round, above): on the day a line is drawn, its fares and subsidy count from that hour and its costs from its first drive out. Capital rows are in, so each column adds up to the change in cash over it; on day 1 both today's column and the quarter's come to the cash, founding capital included. The forecast is the inspector's: the modelled riders' fares and subsidy, the day plans' costs to the cent and $300 a day for each depot; capital has none. With the Finance panel and a stop's card added, I13 still holds: Finance takes 11.0%, 13.6% and 17.2% of the screen at 1440×900, 1366×768 and 1152×720, a stop's card 6.2–9.7%, and the shell at rest 2.8–4.7% with the dock's fourth button.
- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/commands/setStopTier.test.ts`, `src/sim/demand/assign.test.ts`): **stops have tiers, Sign to Transfer Hub** (the M3 spec, item 5; V1's ladder, `Intralines@7af45b1:src/game/constants.ts:46-71`).
  - **The rules.** Every stop starts a Sign ($4k). A step up costs that tier's price: Shelter $12k, Station $35k, Interchange $90k (3 lines calling), Transfer Hub $200k (5 lines). A step down sells the fittings back at half what the steps cost, as a Sales row. A tier's walk bonus (0.8, 1.8, 2.6 or 3.4 minutes) comes off each walk to the stop, never below 0.2 minutes, and the 650 m reach does not grow (V1, `src/game/sim/demand.worker.ts:313-316`). Saves are format 6.
  - **On the debug line** (636.3 riders a day on Signs): a Shelter at Symphony adds 32.4 riders a day, about $124.74 of fares and subsidy, so it pays back its $12k in about 96 days; a Station there adds 88.1, and one at Copley 73.3; Stations at all four stops add 258.0 (+40.5%) for $188k. The fast assignment matches the reference within 1.5% with a Station and a Shelter on the line.
- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench compare`, `npm run bench`; `src/sim/economy/stops.ts`, `stops.test.ts`): **each stop has a day: boardings, alightings and net, flagged QUIET or LOSS** (the M3 spec, item 5).
  - **The rule.** The assignment counts who boards and alights at each stop, per period, and crowding takes its share off each. A stop's net is V2's apportionment (`Intralines_City_dep@archive/intralines-city-v1:Gaming/src/30-sim.js:3757-3792`): its boardings at each line's fare and subsidy, less each line's day of costs shared by boardings, or evenly when nobody boards. So the stops' nets add up to the line's. QUIET is under 5 boardings a day, LOSS under −$20 a day (V2's thresholds).
  - **Against the reference loop**, which counts each rider's stops one by one, the debug line's boardings and alightings per stop are within 4.7%.
  - **The debug line's stops on the starter** (no depot): Copley nets $508.86 a day, Symphony $714.74, the MFA $273.68 and Longwood $54.54, so none is flagged. **Starved** (hourly, one bus): Longwood is QUIET at 1.7 boardings and Symphony LOSS at −$34.23. With a depot's deadhead on the bill as well, Copley, Symphony and the MFA all read LOSS.
  - **On the map** a flagged stop has a ring and its word in the flag colour #c2410c (chroma above PLAN §8's 0.12; 5.2:1 on the panel's white), and a click on a stop opens its card.
  - **Recompute** grows by a few milliseconds for the per-stop counts: Inner Boston's p50 was 43.6–47.1 ms over four runs against 40.7 ms on `main`, and p95 50.8–71.7 ms, inside I14's 150 ms.
- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench strategies`, `npm run bench fares`; `src/sim/rules/fare.ts`, `src/sim/economy/fareBand.ts`): **each line has its own fare, and the line card names the band that nets the most** (the M3 spec, item 4; EC-09, X-12).
  - **The rules**, V2's (`Intralines_City_dep@archive/intralines-city-v1:Gaming/src/30-sim.js:94-100, 2636-2652, 3687-3700`, `10-core.js:58-67`):
    - a fare from $1.00 to $5.00 in 25¢ steps;
    - riders feel each dollar past $2.25 as 5.6 minutes × (3 km ÷ ride along the line)^1.5, kept within 0.15–1.6, so a short hop feels a dear fare most;
    - the city's $1.60 a boarding stays whole at $2.25 or less and tapers by 1 − (gap ÷ $2.75)² to nothing at $5.00.
  - **The debug line's day by fare**, on the starter's 20 minutes (2 buses): $1.00 nets $1,457.86, **$1.75 $1,959.81 (the best)**, $2.00 $1,928.26, $2.25 $1,550.86, $3.00 $562.74 and $5.00 −$332.74. The band within 5% of the best is $1.75–$2.00. On the best headways (10/15, 3 buses) the best is $2.00 ($2,314.87). Both are inside the spec's $1.75–$3.25.
  - **What moves it.** With a fare riders do not feel, the taper alone stops the best at $4.50; with V1's flat fare minutes the best is $1.75 on both. A 3.5 km ride keeps 0.810 of its riders when the fare goes from $2.25 to $3.00 and a 1.3 km hop 0.376 (V1's flat minutes: 0.763 and 0.507).
  - **Inner Boston's ten corridor lines** (Sparrows every 10/15 minutes): every line's best is $1.75 or $2.00, and the bands run from $1.75–$1.75 to $1.00–$2.75 on the emptiest line. The band is worked out only while a line's card is open, one assignment per fare over the line's own cell pairs on catchments built once: a median 101 ms and at most 132 ms a line, after warm-up. Recompute is unchanged (p95 55.3–64.5 ms).
  - **A finding for week 9's tuning.** Riders are very fare-sensitive: the debug line carries 34% more riders at $1.75 than at $2.25 (853.9 against 636.3), so every line measured so far does best below the default fare, where V2 found optima from $1.75 to $3.25. V1's walking factor, which cuts a trip's share to 0.45 or 0.15 once walking nearly matches the bus, is about half of it: held at 1, $1.75 carries 14.8% more than $2.25, not 34%.
- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench crowding`, `npm run bench compare`, `npm run bench`; `src/sim/demand/assign.ts`, `crowding.test.ts`): **full buses turn riders away, half-hour by half-hour (K27).**
  - **The rule.** Each option a rider picks records the stops where it boards and alights, so the assignment sums every leg's riders per period and direction. A half-hour's riders all travel the way the commute does then (home to work in the morning, back in the evening), so its busiest leg carries its share of the period's busiest leg. Riders past the seats the line offers each way in that half-hour (departures × the model's seats) are turned away in that half-hour only. A half-hour that fits loses nobody: V1 cut the whole day by its peak hour's ratio (`Intralines@7af45b1:src/game/sim/demand.worker.ts:575-590`), and one ratio per period, as the spec first read, would turn away riders at 06:30 who fit. That follows PLAN's "hourly crowding" and the salvage rule "per-hour everywhere" ([K27](history/salvage-matrix.md)).
  - **Inner Boston's ten corridor lines** (bench/inner.ts), riders a day and turned away a day:

    | Network | Riders | Turned away | Where |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Sparrows, every 10 min at peak, 15 off-peak | 15,452.2 | 2,377.8 (13.3%) | all on Washington Street, fullest at 17:00 |
    | Metro 40s on the same headways | 17,270.8 | 131.4 (0.8%) | Washington Street, 17:00 |
    | Sparrows every 20 min by day | 10,556.7 | 2,225.3 (17.4%) | Washington Street, 17:00 |

    Washington Street, from Downtown Crossing south, is offered 7,508 riders a day; on Sparrows every 10 minutes it boards 5,130.3, on Metro 40s 7,232.8. The other nine lines never fill. So the Metro 40 earns its price on the busiest corridor and nowhere else yet.
  - **The fixture's debug line never fills**: its busiest leg carries 18.7 riders in the 17:00 half-hour against 42 seats every 20 minutes. The sweep (best +$1,905.82, starved −$72.10) and the calibration are unchanged; the calibration test now scales the riders offered, before crowding. At nine times the fixture's demand, Sparrows board 3,855.8 and turn away 1,876.2, Metro 40s 5,228.4 and 416.7.
  - **Against the reference loop**, which counts each rider's legs one by one, the busiest leg is within 1.07% on the debug line in every period, and within 2.3% on eight of the synthetic grid's ten lines; the other two, whose busiest legs carry 46 riders or fewer, are 2.8–4.7% off. Run backwards, the debug line catches a sign slip in the down direction (51% off) that the forwards run missed.
  - **Recompute** is unchanged: Inner Boston's p95 was 56.6–63.0 ms over three runs, against 59.3 ms on `main`.
- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench strategies`, `npm run bench day`; `src/sim/demand/assign.ts`): **V2's wait model (EC-08's verdict) is ported, and a starved line no longer pays.**
  - **The model.** M1 had ported V1's waits: 70% of riders timed the stop and waited 2 minutes at every headway, and no wait counted past 15 minutes. Now:
    - 70% time the stop up to a 12-minute headway, and the share halves as the headway doubles, down to 5%;
    - a timed wait is never more than half a headway;
    - a wait past 10 minutes is felt as 10 × (wait ÷ 10)^1.5 (`Intralines_City_dep@archive/intralines-city-v1:Gaming/src/30-sim.js:250-319, 2549-2607`).
  - **The debug line's sweep**, before → after:

    | Setting | Riders a day | Net a day |
    |---|---|---|
    | The best (was 15, 60, off; now 10, 15, off on 3 buses) | 767.4 → 855.0 | $2,354.45 → $1,905.82 |
    | Starved: hourly all day, one bus | 681.0 → 59.6 | $2,318.75 → −$72.10 |
    | The starter, every 20 min by day | 795.3 → 636.3 | $2,163.01 → $1,550.86 |

    A starved line now earns less than nothing, against PLAN I3's 60% of the best, and both daytime headways' optima sit inside the range. `strategies.test.ts` holds both, and both fail with V1's waits restored.
  - **The debug line's whole day** (day 2): 636.3 boardings, net +$798.97 (was 795.3 and +$1,411.12), so a first line still pays. `tripScale` stays 111‰: the line sits inside the 600–1,000 band.
  - **I2 and the dwell.** A stop inserted between the MFA and Longwood, in the Fens, now cuts linked trips 0.68%: every rider riding through it pays its 20 s dwell, and few new riders walk to it. The other two insertions add 19.27% and 10.24%. With the dwell held at zero the cut vanishes, so the assignment itself still never loses riders to a stop (EC-02). `invariants-stop.test.ts` holds I2's 0.1% that way, and `invariants.test.ts` bounds a stop's dwell at 1% of trips; the owner's call is under Open.
  - **Recompute** is unchanged within the bench's noise: Inner Boston's p95 was 39.0–107.5 ms over four runs, and 59.3 ms on `main`'s code.

    |---|---|---|---|

    "Daytime longest, overnight shortest" earns less than the best, as the M2 spec asks. A night-only line cannot be set: overnight runs no more often than off-peak, and daytime at least hourly. A line off overnight carries nobody then.

Here hourly all day on one bus earns 98.5% of it, and the best off-peak headway is the longest allowed. Riders barely leave a line for a long wait, the captive-rider class V2 also found ([K28](history/salvage-matrix.md)): 4 times the peak service adds 12.7% more riders.   | Camera | Shortest bus (needs 20 px) |
  |---|---|
  | 2D zoom 14 | 21 px |

  The same check fails a 4 px glyph.
