- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench day`; `src/sim/rules/schedule.test.ts`, `position.test.ts`): **one day plan per line now drives both the costs and the buses' positions.** The plan holds every departure, each departure's round trip and each bus's stint (`src/sim/rules/schedule.ts`).
  - **Why.** The first cost model counted departures from each end of a line on separate clocks, each period window starting its own. Drawn as buses, a departure from the far end need not find a bus there, so the clocks could need more buses than the cycle function counts, and nothing tied the drawing to the bill.
  - **How the plan runs:**
    - Departures leave the first stop every running headway, each period its own. One clock runs through the day, and a shorter peak starts on time when the spacing allows.
    - Each round trip runs out, lays over at the far end until half the cycle, runs back and lays over home. A bus loops in exactly the cycle it is billed for (I6).
    - A bus pulls out when no bus is free at the first stop. It waits there unless its next departure is over an hour off, and goes home after its last trip.
    - A 300-case property test holds the buses out at once to at most what the line owns and its headways need. It found the day's last departure crowding the next day's 00:00 when overnight runs, and that is fixed.
  - **What the costs bill:**
    - running per leg begun in the hour;
    - wages for each bus from its first departure to its last arrival;
    - deadhead per drive from the depot to the first stop and back.
  - **The debug line's whole day** (day 2), parked 1,865 m out and 2,076 m back:

    | Item | Amount |
    |---|---|
    | Running | $362.52, from 57 departures out and back; the spec's estimate was ≈$363 |
    | Wages | $972.11, for 37.4 bus-hours |
    | Deadhead | $15.00 |
    | Upkeep | $300.00 |
    | Fares and subsidy | $3,060.75 |
    | **Net** | **+$1,411.12** |
  - **busPositionsAt** reads the same plan. Sampled every second through the day, no bus moves more than 7.3 m; 26 km/h is 7.22 m a second. A bus pulls out before 05:00, dwells 20 s at each stop between the ends, and drives home after its last trip, fixing V3's three bugs.

  - **Not yet timed:** both figures are Node's. The browser's worker is not timed yet.

  - **G10 reads every kind as words** (rail, bus, ferry, station, stop and so on), so a transit kind the strip does not list fails the bake. On this file the word check caught one false positive, a "trampoline" fitness centre, which is now allowed.
  - **Published.** City tiles run 38359580324 re-cut build 20261012 to the same extract (sha256 `57ac68da…`), stripped it, and published `city-boston-tiles-20261012-a35f1831` (15,347,095 B). That is the same bytes as the local strip, so the strip is reproducible. `cities/index.json` ships it, and a script test runs G10 on every map file the index names.
  - **sim.json.** The clips hold 789 (fixture) and 6,286 (Inner Boston) transit-tagged elements, and none reaches sim.json. Both goldens are unchanged. Depot land now also refuses land tagged as transit, such as a rail yard tagged industrial; no such area exists in either clip.

  - **Day 2's costs.** The line is parked at the fixture's largest depot parcel, 1,865 m out to Copley and 2,062 m back from Longwood. Its two Sparrows cost:
    - running $368.88: 2 × 58 departures × 3.5 km × $0.90;
    - wages $988.00: 19 service hours × 2 buses × $26;
    - deadhead $14.94;
    - depot upkeep $300.00.
  - **Income.** Against those, fares $1,788.75 and subsidy $1,272.00. Each cost equals the forecast to the cent, on day 1 (from 05:00) and on day 2.
  - **58 departures, not the spec's 57.** Each period window starts its departures at its own start, so 05:00–06:30 leaves at 06:20 and peak at 06:30, 10 minutes apart. The vehicle layer (step 6) reads the same timetable, so a bus runs what it is billed for.
  - **The day close merges rows.** Banked hourly, day 2 posts 96 rows (5,195 B as JSON) by 23:59:30. At 24:00 they merge into one per kind and line, 6 rows, and the save grows 354 B over the day. Cash and the day card's totals are unchanged (I1).
- **2026-10-12** (`.github/workflows/tiles.yml`, runs 38310137410 and 38310312956): Boston's map file, cut from Protomaps daily build 20261012 for Inner Boston plus 1.5 km at zoom 0–15, is **450 tiles and 15,560,031 bytes (14.8 MiB)**, under PLAN §5's 40 MB gate and at its 15 MB target; the extract took 3.6 s and 33 requests. Published as release `city-boston-tiles-20261012-57ac68da`. Served by `vite preview`, the file answers Range requests with `206`.
- **2026-10-13, commit 1bae419** (`scripts/deploy/live-check.mjs`, Deploy preview run 38339935614, job "live-check"): the first deploy is live at https://bobbyjohn-nil.github.io/interbus/. Its build chip and `version.json` show `1bae419`, and the map is ready (features drawn) and fills the window. **GitHub Pages answers Range requests on the city file with `206`** and `accept-ranges: bytes`, so tiles are read by range as planned (PLAN §6), and it sends `cache-control: max-age=600`, the ten minutes deploy gate 5 allows for. Deploy gate 2 bit as V1's notes warned: turning Pages on created the `github-pages` environment allowing only the branch that was default at the time, so `main`'s deploy was refused in about 2 s with no logs ("Branch "main" is not allowed to deploy to github-pages due to environment protection rules") until the owner set the environment to "Selected branches and tags" with one Branch rule, `main`.
- **2026-10-12, commit 8fea306** (`node scripts/measure/bundle.mjs` after `npm run build`): the menu's critical path is **76.6 KiB** gzip against the 200 KiB budget: entry script 45.8 KiB, stylesheet 0.6 KiB, `index.html`, and the two Afacad Flux woff2 files. The lazy map chunk is 280.2 KiB gzip (MapLibre, PMTiles and the basemap layers) plus 10.1 KiB of map CSS.
- **2026-10-13, on 328d40d plus the raw tilt** (`node scripts/measure/bundle.mjs` after `npm run build`; the script now also fails a board over budget): the menu's critical path is **77.6 KiB** gzip, its entry script 46.6 KiB with the `T` key. **The board is 433.0 KiB** gzip against I14's 520 KiB: the map chunk 281.2 KiB (MapLibre, PMTiles, and the style with its extrusions and board mask), map CSS 10.1 KiB, and MapLibre's worker 141.7 KiB, which the 2026-10-12 entry left out. Importing the style's layer ids from the menu pulled the Protomaps layers into the entry script (+8.6 KiB) until the ids moved to `tilt.ts`, which imports nothing.
- **2026-10-13** (`node tools/bake/sim.mjs boston <extent> --lock`; `tools/bake/places.test.mjs`): **depot land and gateways are in `sim.json` format 1, and gates G5–G7 pass on both extents.**
  - **Depot land** is OSM industrial land whose centre lies in the clip, as rings.
    - Inner Boston: 155 areas, 7.36 km², about 8% of the play area's 94 km².
    - Mini-Boston: three small parcels, the nearest about 330 m from the debug line.
    - V1's census fallback (more jobs than residents, below-median density) and its 60 m margin are not needed, so the tint draws exactly what the rule allows.
  - **Gateways** are found in the baked graph, not listed by hand. They are motorway, trunk or primary roads within 500 m of the extent's edge, merged within 600 m, plus Logan at the street node nearest the aerodrome's centre. That gives 20 road entries and Logan.
  - **Inbound commuters.** All 597,181 commuters who live outside the play area and work in it (LODES OD main, from 1,420 home tracts) enter at the road entry nearest their home tract's centre. The largest flows are Dorchester Avenue (131,801), Boylston Street/Route 9 (98,392) and Route 2 (62,722). Logan carries none until visitors. Out-of-state homes (the OD aux file) wait for r1.
  - **G5:**
    - Logan's gateway lies on its aerodrome.
    - 9 residents live in water, in census block 250259815011011 in the Charles River Basin, which the profile allows (≤10).
    - The first rule also counted any `water=*` tag, and so flagged 181 people in Brookline's Single Tree Hill Reservoir, a park over a covered reservoir. Water is now `natural=water` or a riverbank; the test fails with the old rule.
  - **G6:** no street node outside the map file's bounds and no person outside the extent, on either extent.
  - **G7:** `sim.json` is 2,033,387 B, 837,594 B (818 KiB) gzipped against 1 MiB. Mini-Boston is 135,413 B.
  - **New goldens:** mini-Boston `69866a1e`, Inner Boston `bafe84fa`. G9 (load time) and G10 (no transit feature in either file) are still to come.
- **2026-10-13** (`node tools/bake/sim.mjs boston inner-boston`, then `npm run bench`): **Inner Boston bakes, and its recompute takes 34.7 ms p50 and 44.6 ms p95** for three periods. The network is ten lines along real corridors with 116 stops, as in T03. That is under I14's 150 ms and **R2's 100 ms (D11)**, for the Nov 1 check to read.
  - **The play area:** the four municipalities inside the extent box. 5,610 of the box's 6,496 blocks lie in them; the 886 left out are in Chelsea, Everett, Winthrop and the like. That gives:
    - **735 cells**, against PLAN §5's 790–840 estimate;
    - 654,983 residents, 331,536 resident workers and 873,428 jobs.
  - **Gates:**
    - G1: 735 cells, in 400–1,000;
    - G2: 11,687 nodes and 17,221 edges, with 98.45% of drivable km off motorways inside the box in one piece;
    - G3: 99.98% of residents within 300 m of a kept street;
    - G4: exact for all four municipalities.
  - **Closed to buses: 258 ways.** By reason:
    - `adjust.json` 93 (River Roads pieces);
    - private access 70;
    - clearances 45;
    - `bus=no` 31;
    - `motor_vehicle=no` 13;
    - other access tags 6.
  - **The file:**
    - `sim.json` is 1,928,801 B, or 785 KiB gzip, against PLAN §5's 1 MB;
    - the golden is `8b87dfc2`, in `cities/boston/sim-inner-boston.lock.json`;
    - the cut takes about 4 min 40 s locally, almost all of it `osm.py clip`, and is reused while sources and box match.
  - **Mini-Boston's G2** reads 87.5%, now that it is measured inside the fixture box (82.0% counting whole clipped ways). Its `sim.json` is unchanged.
  - **Still to come:**
    - gateways (Logan, the main road entries) land with visitors; until then the play extent keeps only commutes with both ends inside, as the fixture does;
    - `calib` waits for r1 in week 5, so `tripScale` is 1000‰.
- **2026-10-13** (a trial bake of Inner Boston from the mirrored sources, to be re-measured by the committed bake job; then `npm run bake:sim` and `tools/bake/sim.test.mjs`): **`hgv=no` no longer closes a street to buses.**
  - **Why:** PLAN §5 honoured `hgv=no` to close the parkways, but in OSM it is a truck exclusion. OSM's access hierarchy for a bus runs `bus`, `psv`, `motor_vehicle`, `vehicle`, `access` and never reaches `hgv`. Sources: [the tagging list](https://lists.openstreetmap.org/pipermail/tagging/2017-February/031282.html) and [the OSM help site](https://help.openstreetmap.org/questions/20675/what-does-access-tag-vehicle-no/), checked 2026-10-13 through search excerpts.
  - **What the old rule did inside the Inner Boston extent:** it closed 353 ways of the routable classes, including bus streets such as Summer Street, Columbia Road, Rindge Avenue and Prospect Street. The closures fragmented the network: 97.8% of drivable km off motorways stayed in one piece, against G2's 98%.
  - **What closes the parkways now:** `adjust.json` closes DCR's three "cars only" River Roads by name, Storrow Drive, Soldiers Field Road and Memorial Drive (DCR press releases, URLs in the file). Clearances under 3.5 m still close a street. With these rules the trial closes 258 ways (540 before), and 98.45% of drivable km off motorways inside the extent is in one piece.
  - **G2 now leaves motorways and their ramps out of the share.** In the trial they account for 35.6 of the 60.1 km dropped inside the extent (of 1,749 km): the Mass Pike's west end, cut off by the clip, and ramps that lead only to the closed River Roads. Where they connect, they stay routable.
  - **On mini-Boston:**
    - 681 nodes and 1,037 edges (was 675 and 1,028);
    - 82 ways closed, by `adjust.json` (34), `bus=no` (28), clearances (18) and private access (2);
    - G2 82.0% off motorways (75% of all drivable km before);
    - `sim.json` 134,361 B.
    The debug line's riders are unchanged at 795.3 a day.
  - **Shown failing (TS-04):** with the `hgv=no` rule restored, the access test closes Summer Street; with G2 over all drivable km, a stranded motorway ramp lowers the share to 2/3.
- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench`, `npm run bench compare` and `npm run bench day`, in Node 22.22 on the bench bundled by Vite as the game's build bundles the sim): **the three-period recompute takes 52 ms p50 and 65–78 ms p95** over 30 runs, against I14's 150 ms and R2's 100 ms (D11). The case is a synthetic 812-cell Inner-Boston-sized grid carrying T03's 10 lines and 116 stops. The fixture with three lines takes 3.3 ms p50. Boston itself is measured on Nov 1.
  - **How:** the first loop compared every sub-point pair with every stop pair, at 13.2 s p95 on the grid. The fast loop:
    - visits only cell pairs that share a line (91,455 of the 658,532 with commuters);
    - times a trip per group of a cell's sub-points that reach the same stops of a line, using the group's average walk. Who can walk to which stop is still decided per sub-point.
  - **What it costs in accuracy,** against the old loop, kept in `src/sim/demand/reference/` as the yardstick:
    - on the grid, trips are 0.7% lower (worst line and period 1.5%);
    - the debug line is 0.5% lower;
    - where four lines overlap on the fixture, trips are 1.0% lower, and the split between lines moves up to 7% a day (11% in one period), because a group picks one line together.
  - **The debug line** now carries 795.3 boardings a day (799.3 before) and earns $3,060.75 on a full day. `tripScale` stays 111‰, inside the 600–1,000 band.
  - **Two versions failed on the way (TS-04):**
    - crediting every stop to the whole cell put the debug line 4.3% high at peak;
    - timing groups between their own centres let a new stop move a trip's distance, so I2 failed: a stop between the MFA and Longwood cut trips 0.15%. Distances are now the cell pair's, as the commuters were counted.
  - **Timing under vitest misleads.** Its module transform turns every imported constant into a getter call, and at one stage the same code took 226 ms there and 166 ms bundled. The bench therefore bundles and runs in Node.
- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/demand/calibration.test.ts`, `invariants.test.ts`):
  - **`tripScale` on mini-Boston is 111‰** (`cities/boston/city.json` calib). The debug line now carries 799.3 boardings a day and earns $3,076.15. Before calibration it was 7,200.7 and $27,720; V1's starter carried 1,058 and earned $4,075 (EC-11).
  - **I2 holds** for a stop inserted on the line's own streets between any two stops, for a second line on the same streets, and for four stops crowded around Symphony.
  - **With V1's 4-nearest-stops cap restored (K04)**, the crowded case cuts trips from 798.5 to 790.1, and I2 fails.
  - **A stop placed off the route costs riders, rightly.** The straight-line midpoint of the MFA and Longwood, in the Fens, drags the line 436 m out of its way and cuts trips 4%. The line tool will insert stops on the route.
- **2026-10-13** (`node tools/bake/heights.mjs boston` on the mirrored `massachusetts-20261011.osm.pbf`; 1 min 44 s): **10,403 of Inner Boston's 73,936 OSM buildings (14.1%) carry a height or a level count.**
  - **By municipality:** Cambridge 3,279 of 14,535 (22.6%), Boston 5,547 of 38,730 (14.3%), Somerville 1,377 of 13,724 (10.0%), Brookline 200 of 6,947 (2.9%).
  - **Which tag:** almost all carry `building:levels` (10,198); only 789 carry `height`.
  - **How a building counts:** its first node must lie inside the extent and inside one of the four municipalities' 2020 boundaries, the same boundaries G4 uses. Multipolygon building relations (7,067 statewide) are not counted, which is one reason the total sits under Overture's 80,145.
  - **Result:** under about half, so by the rule above **Overture's lidar heights join the r1 bake in week 5** and come off cut 1.
- **2026-10-13** (City sim file (manual) run 38346461662; `npm run measure:bundle`):
  - **The sim file is published.** Mini-Boston's `sim.json` is release `city-boston-sim-mini-boston-ba4110d6`, 132,908 B. On GitHub's runner the bake matched the golden hash byte for byte, so the fixture bakes identically on two machines (G8, in small).
  - **On the preview** the sim worker draws 683 dots. "People within a walk" at the centre of the opening view reads ≈16,000.
  - **Bundle budgets:**
    - menu 80.7 KiB gzip, against 200;
    - board 440.5 KiB gzip, against 520, the 6.8 KiB sim worker included.
- **2026-10-13** (`src/sim/fare-row.test.ts` and a probe over `src/sim/demand`): **the first-dollar signal is green**, 12 days before its Oct 25 deadline. On mini-Boston the debug line (Copley, Symphony, the MFA, Longwood; every 20 min by day, overnight off, $2.25) writes fare and subsidy rows on day 1.
  - **Ridership before calibration** (`tripScale` 1): 7,200.7 boardings a day, giving $27,720 of income. V1's starter carried 1,058 a day (EC-11), so the fixture runs about 7× V1's density, as PLAN §3 estimated (7–10×). Week 3 sets `tripScale` near 0.1.
  - **Recompute:** 37 ms with one line, measured after accumulating per period rather than per half-hour (190 ms before).
- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bake:sim`, `tools/bake/{streets,cells,sim}.mjs`): **mini-Boston's `sim.json`**, format 1, is 132,908 B (56.6 kB gzip). Its contents:
  - **Streets:** 675 street nodes and 1,028 edges after degree-2 collapse, 106.1 km of street.
  - **Cells:** 84 cells of Boston's 350 m grid, holding exactly the fixture's 105,620 residents, 41,148 resident workers (LODES RAC) and 186,144 jobs (WAC).
  - **Closed to buses:** 92 OSM ways. 30 are closed by `hgv=no`, 28 by `bus=no`, 18 by clearances under 3.5 m (feet-and-inches values, decoded) and 2 by private access. Another 14 (Storrow and Memorial Drive pieces OSM leaves unrestricted) are closed by `cities/boston/adjust.json`.
  - **Clipping stranded the Mass Pike and some one-way grids.** The piece kept is the largest in which every node reaches every other. It holds 75% of open drivable km: the clip strands the Mass Pike, which has no ramps inside the box, and one-way grids in Cambridgeport and Beacon Hill whose way back lies outside it.
  - **Residents near a street:** 97.4% live within 300 m of a kept street; the other 2,748 are all in those two grids.
  - **Gates:** G1–G3 read a profile per extent in `cities/boston/city.json`.
- **2026-10-12, commit 8fea306** (`npm run fonts`, `scripts/fonts/build.mjs`): Afacad Flux 400 and 600 Latin woff2 come to **14.9 KB + 15.7 KB = 30.6 KB**, above PLAN §8's 23–29 KB trial; each stack has 256 glyph ranges, 13 of them with glyphs (about 250 KB per stack on disk, fetched only as labels need them). Two runs from a fresh environment differed by about 80 bytes in the woff2 files while the glyph ranges were byte-identical; the subsetter is not fully deterministic, which matters only if font bytes are ever hashed.
- **2026-10-12, commit 8fea306** (`src/render/map/style.test.ts`): four of PLAN §8's starting light tokens break §8's own base-chroma limit of 0.04: park `#d5e4c6` (0.043), wood `#cfdfc2` (0.043), arterial `#f9e9c8` (0.047) and arterial casing `#e3c48d` (0.080). Style v0 uses them scaled to chroma 0.039 at the same lightness and hue (`#d6e3c8`, `#d0dec4`, `#f7e9ce`, `#d6c7ad`). The Nov 8 light-token pick decides between the rule and the stronger amber.
- **2026-10-12** (`.github/workflows/bake-sources.yml`, runs 38314934726 and 38315140053): census.gov, LEHD and Geofabrik all accept downloads from GitHub's runners (Geofabrik's 296 MB in 19 s), so no owner-machine fetch is needed. The eight pinned inputs are mirrored as release `city-boston-sources-20261012`, with sizes and SHA-256 in `cities/boston/sources.lock.json`:
  - OSM extract (`massachusetts-20261011.osm.pbf`): 310,967,666 B;
  - TIGER/Line 2020 blocks: 106,898,194 B; county subdivisions: 1,918,536 B;
  - LODES 8.4 2023 OD main: 17,463,033 B; OD aux: 2,170,558 B; RAC: 2,593,510 B; WAC: 1,245,230 B; crosswalk: 1,875,594 B.
- **2026-10-12** (`npm run bake:fixture`, `tools/bake/census.mjs`): **gate G4 holds exactly**. Σ POP20 over each municipality's 2020 blocks, on 15-digit ids, gives Boston **675,647**, Cambridge 118,403, Somerville 81,045 and Brookline 63,191.
  - The first run took municipalities from the LODES crosswalk and failed G4: Cambridge 118,778 (+375) and Somerville 81,498 (+453).
  - The crosswalk's municipality codes are not 2020's. It puts six populated blocks, 828 people, in Cambridge and Somerville that 2020's boundaries, and so the 2020 counts, put in Belmont and Medford. Across Suffolk, Middlesex and Norfolk it disagrees with the 2020 boundaries on 399 populated blocks (37,019 people).
  - The bake therefore takes each block's municipality from TIGER/Line 2020 county subdivisions, by its internal point (about 3 s for the three counties). The crosswalk stays as a cross-check, and its disagreements are listed in `fixture.json`.
- **2026-10-12** (`npm run bake:fixture`): **mini-Boston** (lon −71.11…−71.07, lat 42.335…42.36) holds 729 blocks with **105,620 residents**, 5,946 commute pairs with both ends inside, 478 RAC and 458 WAC rows, and no out-of-state commuters. Its OPL clip has 16,054 nodes, 1,744 ways and 218 relations.
  - The fixture is 2.5 MB of text in all, 1.96 MB of it OSM, against the 150–300 kB the plan's research estimated. Relations list only the members inside the clip; full member lists made the OPL 3.4 MB.
  - A local cut takes about 4 minutes, almost all of it the OSM passes over the 311 MB extract. PR CI only verifies the committed copy's hashes; `Bake fixture check (manual)` re-cuts it on a runner and fails on any byte of difference.
